# Decisions

Newest first inside each section. A decision names the options considered, the choice, and the reason. The Lead adds entries as it decides; Mohammad reverses any entry by adding a dated line under it.

## Awaiting Mohammad's review

### A1860 2026-11-03 A crashed test run shows the engine's fatal reason, read from the run's own output (M5.1n, m5-1 lead)
A learner whose code hits an engine fatal error (Pack 4 World 3: a plain `FArchive` in a save quest) got only "The engine stopped before writing a test report (exit code 3)", with no failed test; the useful line ("FArchive does not support FObjectPtr serialization. Use FArchiveUObject instead") was in an editor log the app never showed. Options: read the sandbox's `Saved/Logs/Dojo.log` after the run; read the output lines the runner already collects (the run passes `-stdout -FullStdOutLogOutput`, so the engine prints its log there, and `runSupervised` returns every line of this run); show the whole output tail. Chosen: the collected lines, through a new `EngineCrashReader` beside `AutomationTestRunner`. A log file can be an earlier run's (the engine renames the last one to `Dojo-backup-<time>.log` and a failed start writes none), needs a path per platform and a read that races the engine's shutdown, and was ruled out by the brief (only this run counts); the collected lines cannot be stale by construction. The fatal shapes were read from a real UE 5.8.3 Windows editor log in the verifier workspace: `LogWindows: Error: appError called: Fatal error: [File:...] [Line: 523] ` with the message alone on the next line, repeated in a `=== Critical error: ===` block. The shapes for a failed `check` (`Assertion failed: expr [File:] [Line:] message`), an access violation (`Unhandled Exception: EXCEPTION_ACCESS_VIOLATION ...`) and a Mac signal (`Caught signal 11 Segmentation fault`) come from the engine source, not from a log: they are unit tests only. Not verified: that the real stdout carries the same text with the same prefixes as the log file (no engine run was allowed). The reader accepts the line with or without the timestamp and category, so if stdout differs only in prefix it still works; if a fatal line is missing from stdout the old message stays. Reverse cost: delete the class and the one call.

### A1861 2026-11-03 Only a crash with a quest test running says "your code"; a stall, a timeout and a kill keep their old wording (M5.1n, m5-1 lead)
The brief wants a note that the learner's code stopped the engine, but a shared machine also stalls and kills engines. Rules: a timeout, a cancel and a program that could not start return before the report is read and are unchanged. With no report and no fatal line in the output the old message stays word for word. With a fatal line and a `Test Started` line for one of this quest's own tests (and no `Test Completed` for it), the message is "Your code stopped the engine while the test <name> was running, so no test report was written (exit code N). The engine said: <reason>."; if tests of the quest started but none was still running, "while your tests were running"; with a fatal line and no quest test started (a crash while the engine started, which may be the engine's or a plugin's), only "The engine crashed and stopped before writing a test report (exit code N). The engine said: <reason>.", with no blame. `BuildFailureExplainer` is unchanged: a compiled run whose pipeline text it does not know already keeps the blame with the quest and shows `RunResult.error` (A1199, A1203), and the verifier still gets `tests: []` plus a pipeline error, so a crash still counts as caught. A crash whose test-start line is missing from stdout gets the neutral wording, not a wrong accusation. Reverse cost: the wording is one function.

### A1862 2026-11-03 The reason is the first fatal line, in plain words, one line, capped at 200 characters (M5.1n, m5-1 lead)
The engine prints the same fatal text twice (`appError called` and again in the critical error block) and a callstack after it, and a message can be a whole expression or path. Chosen: the first fatal line wins; its message is taken from the same line, else from the next non-blank line (never a `[Callstack]` line); control characters and runs of spaces collapse to one line; over 200 characters it is cut with three dots. A failed check reads "a check in the code failed: <expression> (<message>)"; an access violation or segmentation fault reads "the code used memory it does not own (<engine name>)"; a stack overflow reads "the code called itself too many times and ran out of stack (<engine name>)"; any other fatal text is shown as the engine wrote it. Only lines with no category or an engine category (`LogWindows`, `LogCore`, `LogOutputDevice`, `LogMac`, `LogApple`, `LogLinux`, `LogUnix`, `LogHAL`) can start a fatal error, so a learner's own `UE_LOG(LogTemp, Error, "Fatal error: ...")` is not read as one. The learner's own `UE_LOG(Fatal)` or `check` is read, as it comes through `appError called` and is their code stopping the engine. Reverse cost: none.

### A1823 2026-11-03 Spot-check 3 does not change spot-check 2's severity rules (qa, M4.y spot-check 3)
Options: keep the A1621 meaning (Must fix is a wrong fact taught as true, copied wording, or the solution or deliberate failure given away beside the brief; Should fix is a promise the learner cannot see, a broken spec rule, a hint with code or length, a tell; Note is the rest); loosen it now that the deliberate-failure leaks are rarer. Kept it, so the counts compare across rounds: 2 Must fix in spot-check 2, 5 in spot-check 3. No reviewer rating was changed this round and no finding was dropped; I re-read the file or engine line behind every Must fix.

### A1822 2026-11-03 Glossary hover cards count as text beside the brief (qa, M4.y spot-check 3)
Two glossary entries (`line-trace`, `collision-query-params`) describe the fix to the Tracing sentry quest's deliberate failure, and both are in that quest's `terms[]`, so their hover cards show before the failing step. Options: rate it a Note because the glossary is general reference; rate it Must fix because the app shows the card inside the quest. Chose Must fix, since the rule is about what the learner can see before the failure and the card is on screen. The fix is to cut the sentences from the two entries; the `object-type-query` entry already carries the idea.

### A1821 2026-11-03 The `SetTimerForNextTick` "exactly one frame" claim is a Must fix, not a Note (qa, M4.y spot-check 3)
`TimerManager.GuaranteeEngineTickDelay` defaults to 0 in the installed 5.8 source and its own text says next-tick timers "could run during the same engine tick". The quest teaches an exact guarantee the engine does not give when set from an actor's Tick. It does hold from inside a timer callback, which is how the boss uses it, so the solution is right and only the prose is wrong. Options: Note because the boss works; Must fix because a learner will take the guarantee elsewhere. Chose Must fix and suggested wording that is true in both places.

### A1820 2026-11-03 Sampling for M4.y spot-check 3 (qa)
Seeded shuffle (mulberry32, seed 20261104) of each world's quest files: Pack 3 World 4 took 8 of 10 (not 01 and 07), Pack 3 World 5 took 8 of 10 (not 03 and 10, the boss), Pack 4 World 4 was read whole (6). One read-only reviewer per world, three in parallel, each told the engine source is at `C:/Program Files/Epic Games/UE_5.8` and the recurring Should-fix kinds from spot-check 2. Options: read every quest; sample as the Lead asked. Sampled. The brief said main was 8bc8b5a; main is 0eec77e, which has 8bc8b5a as an ancestor, so the worlds were all present. Pack 4 World 3 has no folder yet, so World 4 was numbered and checked on its own.

### A1489 2026-11-03 The tower's looks, awareness, and beam run on one timer, not in Tick (author-mechanics, M4.P3.5)
The Watchtower boss could let each sentry trace the hero in its own Tick, or let the tower check on a timer. Options: sentries ask in Tick; sentries own timers and report events; the tower owns one 0.1 second timer and asks. Chosen: the tower's one timer, because it keeps the order of the checks fixed (look, awareness, alarm, beam, exit), keeps one send-back per check, and teaches that a query is asked as often as its answer changes (the tracing habits card). Reverse cost: low (the sentry reports plan is the second reference plan).
### A1488 2026-11-03 Standalone tools: the rangefinder, feeler and piercer are not boss pieces; the feeler is the boss's first extension (author-mechanics, M4.P3.5)
The research's tracing bosses use a sentry cone and an awareness meter. Options: make every build quest a boss piece (the gate would need a moving door, a hero that blocks the Visibility channel, and a feeler per step, which makes the tests fragile); use two pieces and keep the others as side tools. Chosen: the boss uses the sight and laser pieces (A67 holds: reference integration plus a two round test), and extension 1 builds a gate from the feeler. Reverse cost: low.
### A1487 2026-11-03 The laser bounces only off surfaces whose actor carries a tag (MirrorTag, default Mirror) (author-mechanics, M4.P3.5)
A bounce needs a way to tell a mirror from a wall. Options: a component tag; an actor tag; a physical material; a custom trace channel. Chosen: an actor tag in a tunable FName, because the learner sets it in Details with no project settings, the tests set it with one line, and the brief can name it. A physical material or a channel needs project assets the sandbox does not ship. Reverse cost: low.
### A1486 2026-11-03 Tests are deterministic with real collision: boxes and a capsule placed by the test, results read as numbers (author-mechanics, M4.P3.5)
Every observable is a number the learner also sees: a distance, a stop location, a count, a path point, a meter value. The tests spawn their own solid boxes and a capsule through the sandbox's test world (no earlier quest's headers are included), so each quest compiles alone. Timer phase differs between a round started at BeginPlay and one started by R, so the two rounds' times are compared with one check of tolerance (as in World 2). Debug drawing is part of every rubric but is not asserted, because the line batcher is not a result a test can read. Reverse cost: low.
### A1485 2026-11-03 Glossary: six new terms and two first-quest settings; debug-draw links to the traces overview (author-mechanics, M4.P3.5)
Added debug-draw, multi-trace, collision-shape, object-type-query, dot-product, reflection-vector, and firstQuestId on line-trace and collision-query-params. collision-channel, collision-response and collision-preset are World 3's and are left alone. The docs page for the DrawDebugHelpers header could not be confirmed, so debug-draw links to Epic's traces overview. Each claim was written from UE 5.8 behavior: single traces skip Overlap responses; a multi trace lists the overlaps nearest first and ends with the first Block; a sweep's Location is the shape's centre and ImpactPoint is on the surface; object-type queries match an object's own type whatever its responses are. Reverse cost: low.
### A1484 2026-11-03 Query kinds: the rangefinder uses a channel, the sentry's eye uses object types, the other tools use channels (author-mechanics, M4.P3.5)
The deliberate failure needs a reason the learner can find: a hero capsule that ignores the Visibility channel, as a game character's capsule does, so the eye's first look (by channel, as in the rangefinder) never meets it. Options: a missing collision component (no lesson); a null pointer (not tracing); the Visibility response. Chosen: the Visibility response, with the fix by object type, which the shapes card then names. The tests give the hero those settings explicitly instead of a named preset, so the proof does not depend on a preset table. Reverse cost: low.
### A1483 2026-11-03 The deliberate failure is in the sentry's eye, and nothing shown before it says why (author-mechanics, M4.P3.5)
Rule 5 of the research asks one deliberate failure per world and that it be announced only after the step that fails. The sightline quest tells the learner only to trace on the Visibility channel as the rangefinder did, and says nothing about a failure or its cause before it happens; the hero is never seen, and hint 1 sends them to the trace on screen and the capsule's collision settings (the rubric states only outcomes). The hint ladder points at the trace on screen, then at channel versus object type, then at the API. The Parsons and the Alternative do not name the cause. Reverse cost: low.
### A1482 2026-11-03 Ladder and shape: worked rangefinder, faded sightline and feeler, stub piercer, blank laser, with three concept cards (author-mechanics, M4.P3.5)
Same shape as World 2: breakdown, three concepts (lines and hits, shapes and queries, tracing habits), five builds fading, one boss. The sweep and multi trace appear in the cards before the builds that need them, except that the sightline's object-type fix is named by the card only after the failure (A1483). No refactor quest: the ladder in the research has none for tracing, and the pack's refactor slots belong to later worlds. Reverse cost: low.
### A1481 2026-11-03 The tracing channel for every build is Visibility; walls are the BlockAll preset (author-mechanics, M4.P3.5)
Using one channel keeps the lesson on the targets' responses, not on project settings. A custom trace channel is named in the sightline's Alternative and in the shapes card. Reverse cost: none.
### A1480 2026-11-03 Pack 3 World 5 opens after Pack 3 World 2 for now, to be repointed when World 4 merges (author-mechanics, M4.P3.5) (author-mechanics, M4.P3.5)
Worlds 3 and 4 are written in parallel and are not on main. Options: no prerequisite; World 2's boss `m2-boss-clockwork-hall` (on main, teaches sweeps and movement this world builds on); a guess at World 4's last quest. ### A1810 2026-11-03 Windows e2e launches lift Chromium's frame rate limit, so a hidden window gets real frames (qa, slow build flow)
`e2e/ui-quests-flow.spec.ts` "a build: explain the marked lines..." took 52 to 57 s of its 60 s limit alone. A per-call trace (`DEBUG=pw:api`) showed no fixed wait: about 23 s went to Playwright's "visible, enabled and stable" check on 21 clicks, about 12 s to 11 screenshots, and about 11 s to waits that need a paint. The cause is the hidden window on Windows: `requestAnimationFrame` ran once every 1004 ms, with or without `backgroundThrottling: false`, and the stable check waits two frames. Options: `test.slow()` or a split (hides the cause, and the other 11 specs pay the same per-action tax); `force: true` on clicks (skips checks, weakens the specs); Chromium switches on the launch. Probed on the real app: `--disable-renderer-backgrounding`, `--disable-background-timer-throttling`, `--disable-backgrounding-occluded-windows`, `--disable-features=CalculateNativeWinOcclusion`, and `--disable-gpu-vsync` left 1004 ms frames; `--disable-frame-rate-limit` alone gave 17 ms. Chosen: `launchDojo()` passes `--disable-frame-rate-limit` when `process.platform` is `win32`. It is harness only (`e2e/support/launchDojo.ts`); the app and installers are untouched, and no assertion changed. Proof: the build flow dropped from 57.0 s to 10.5 s alone; `npx playwright test e2e/ui-quests-flow.spec.ts` passed 12 of 12 three runs in a row (about 54 s each, build flow 10.8 s) without `SAVE_PROOF`. Not applied on macOS, where A970 was proven without it and a hidden window's frame rate was not measured; logged under "Unverified on a platform". Reverse cost: none (delete the constant).
### A1560 2026-11-03 Pack 4 World 6 uses a Behavior Tree and Blackboard, not State Tree (author-systems, M4.P4.6)
The world teaches AI decisions with tasks, decorators, and services. Options: Behavior Tree and Blackboard (AIModule); State Tree (the StateTree and GameplayStateTree plugins); a plain C++ state machine component. Chosen: Behavior Tree. AIModule is already in `Dojo.Build.cs`, and its node base classes export their constructors, so quest classes derive from `UBTTaskNode`, `UBTService`, and `UBTDecorator_BlackboardBase` with no change to the sandbox. State Tree needs two plugins that the sandbox `.uproject` does not enable and `Dojo.Build.cs` does not list, and this task may not change the sandbox template; its tasks are also structs run through an execution context, which is harder to drive from a headless test. The C++ state machine is the second reference plan of the breakdown, and State Tree is named in its Alternative Note. Reverse: enable the plugins in the template and rewrite the three node quests as State Tree tasks and conditions.

### A1561 2026-11-03 Trees are built in code by a ready-made kit (author-systems, M4.P4.6)
A headless test has no Behavior Tree or Blackboard assets. Options: ship `.uasset` files with the sandbox (binary, editor-version bound); build trees in code; test node classes by calling their functions without a tree (most overrides are protected and need a tree component). Chosen: `FDojoS6TreeKit` in `s6-walk-beat` builds the blackboard (TargetActor, TargetVisible, ClueLocation, HasClue) and the tree node by node, the same route as Epic's AITestSuite `FBTBuilder`: composites get `InitializeFromAsset` as they are made, and `UBehaviorTreeManager::LoadTree` initializes every other node when `AAIController::RunBehaviorTree` starts it. The kit is ready-made, and every quest's "See it in the editor" builds the same tree as assets. A world made by `UWorld::CreateWorld` as a game world has no AI system (the engine creates one only for editor worlds there), so without one no tree starts and perception is silent; `FDojoS6TreeKit::EnableAI` creates and starts it (`CreateAISystem`, `InitializeActorsForPlay`, `StartPlay`), and every World 6 test calls it first. The sandbox's `FDojoTestWorld` is left unchanged because this task may not edit the template; a later sandbox task could move the call there (fix round 1, found by the reviewer). Reverse: none needed for content; an asset route would replace the kit.

### A1562 2026-11-03 World 6 tasks move the pawn themselves (author-systems, M4.P4.6)
The test world has no nav mesh, so `MoveToLocation` would fail. Options: build a nav mesh in the test world; move the pawn in `TickTask` with `SetActorLocation`; use a movement component. Chosen: `SetActorLocation` from `TickTask` at a fixed speed, which keeps every observable a position at a time. The walk beat quest's Alternative names `MoveToLocation` and the nav mesh as the game route. Reverse cost: the three movement tasks.

### A1563 2026-11-03 How World 6 tests reach perception (author-systems, M4.P4.6)
Options: real senses everywhere; the memory's plain functions only; both, by layer. Chosen: by layer. The memory's rules are tested through `NoteSight` and `NoteNoise`. Its perception handler is tested by broadcasting a real `UAIPerceptionComponent`'s `OnTargetPerceptionUpdated` with `FAIStimulus` values built from the sight and hearing sense defaults, after creating the test world's AI system and calling `RegisterSenseClass` on its perception system, so `GetSenseClassForStimulus` maps them back. That test makes the AI system inline, because the memory quest comes before the kit. The boss test uses real sight and real hearing (`UAISense_Hearing::ReportNoiseEvent`) through the guard controller's perception component, because the perception system ticks with `UWorld::Tick`. Reverse cost: tests only.

### A1564 2026-11-03 The world's deliberate failure is a decorator without an abort mode (author-systems, M4.P4.6)
A decorator with only a condition is checked when the tree reaches its node, so a guard that is walking a ten-second beat notices a noise only after it arrives. The blank decorator quest's brief and rubric state the observable (stops within half a second, resumes within half a second after the clue goes stale), the hints climb to the observer abort, and the Alternative explains Lower Priority, Self, and Both after a pass. Options considered: the shared-member-variable mistake in the walk beat task (kept as a test and a Parsons distractor, but it is easier to stumble on than to learn from as the world's one failure).

### A1565 2026-11-03 Shape and names of Pack 4 World 6 (author-systems, M4.P4.6)
World `06-ai-perception-and-behavior` (id `ai-perception-and-behavior`): a breakdown with two reference plans (Behavior Tree fed by a memory component; a C++ state machine component), then the fade ladder: `s6-sense-memory` worked, `s6-walk-beat` faded, `s6-memory-service` stub, `s6-clue-decorator` blank, and the boss `s6-boss-night-shift`. The boss's new nodes (chase, go to clue, target in sight) live in its own folder, so no piece is extended. Reference game: Thief: The Dark Project. The staleness rule lives in the service (`ClueSeconds`), not the memory, so the memory stays a plain fact store a non-tree guard can reuse. Five glossary entries are added (`bt-task-node`, `node-memory`, `bt-service`, `bt-decorator`, `ai-stimulus`). A task's node memory struct sits in the task's own header, next to the task, as the engine's tasks do; it is plain data only that task uses, so it is not split into a file of its own (an exception to one class per file, reviewer nit). The boss's go to clue task asks the guard controller to forget the clue (`ForgetClue`) instead of reaching into the memory and the blackboard, so tasks read keys and call their controller only. Reverse cost: content only.

### A1407 2026-11-03 The yard ends a round one frame after the last blast (author-mechanics, M4.P3.4)
A bomb's `OnDestroyed` has two listeners in the boss, the chain that lights the neighbors and the yard that counts, and UE does not promise the order in which a dynamic multicast delegate calls its listeners. Options: let the chain report the end; check in the yard's own handler; check one frame later. Chosen: the yard's handler counts the blast and sets `SetTimerForNextTick`, and the next-tick check ends the round when no bomb is lit. Why: by then every listener of that blast has run, so a spark lit by the chain is always seen; it also gives `SetTimerForNextTick` a real job. The breakdown's alternative note names the wrong version (deciding inside the same listener). In UE 5.8 the dynamic multicast delegate happens to call listeners in bind order, and the yard's own chain binds before the yard, so the round tests alone would let a yard that ends inside its own handler pass; the engine mutation run confirmed it (yard-ends-in-handler survived). `LateSparksKeepItBurning` closes that gap: it sets the yard's reach to 0 and binds a second chain to the bombs after the yard, so the only sparks come from a listener that runs after the yard's, and a yard that decides inside its handler ends after the first blast. Reverse cost: low.

### A1406 2026-11-03 Four glossary entries, no corrections (author-mechanics, M4.P3.4)
New entries: `spawn-collision-handling`, `spawn-actor-deferred`, `frandomstream` (first quest `m4-spawning-with-care`), and `on-destroyed` (first quest `m4-hearing-it-go`). Spawning, `TSubclassOf`, timers, handles, `Destroy`, `IsValid`, and the object pool are Pack 1's, and their first quests stay there. API links use class pages (`UWorld`, `AActor`) because member pages are not on the flat 5.8 path. Facts checked in UE 5.8 source: `UWorld::SpawnActor` returns nullptr before construction for DontSpawnIfColliding when a native shape encroaches (`LevelActor.cpp`); an actor's default handling is AlwaysSpawn (`AActor` constructor) unless its class sets another, as `APawn` does with AdjustIfPossibleButDontSpawnIfColliding; `FTimerManager::InternalSetTimer` clears a handle's running timer before setting the new one and a rate of zero or less sets nothing; `GetTimerRemaining` answers -1 without a timer; a looping timer runs `floor((now - expire) / rate) + 1` times in a frame; `AActor::Destroyed` runs `EndPlay` before broadcasting `OnDestroyed`, and the actor is marked as garbage only after the listeners return, so `IsValid` is still true inside the handler. Reverse cost: low.

### A1405 2026-11-03 Reuse on purpose: World 3's crate, runner, and plate, and World 2's wall (author-mechanics, M4.P3.4)
The scatter quest scatters World 3's `ADojoM3Crate` around World 2's `ADojoM2Wall`; the boss uses World 3's runner and plate (with its own second handler on the plate's zone, as World 3's room did on the pit) and two World 2 walls as pillars. All are reused unchanged and named on the "Compiles against earlier quests" lines; the rack lists World 2's `world-and-local-space` term. Reverse cost: low.

### A1404 2026-11-03 The world's deliberate failure is a bomb that a second lighting can delay (author-mechanics, M4.P3.4)
Options: a spawn counted without checking for nullptr (the concept card's own spot-the-bug, so it would not surprise); two timers on one handle (the bomb's header already has two handles); a looping refill that runs several times in a long frame (the keeper checks the budget on every call, so nothing breaks); a bomb whose `Light` restarts the fuse when it is already burning. Chosen: the last, in the faded fuse quest. The natural `SetTimer(FuseTimer, ..., Seconds)` replaces the running fuse, so a burning bomb lit again with a longer fuse waits longer; `LightingAgainNeverDelays` catches it with "Lighting a burning bomb with a longer fuse never makes it wait longer: 2 seconds are still left". The brief, rubric, and concepts state only the outcome (sooner, never later); the breakdown names the property and which class decides it, not how; the second hint is the first to say that `SetTimer` on a busy handle starts over. One honest limit: the timer card before the quest says that a second `SetTimer` on a handle replaces the first, because Pack 1 taught it and the two-handle rule needs it, so a learner who connects the two will not fail; the trap is applying a known fact, as World 2's was (A1133). The chain then relies on this rule: a spark never delays a bomb about to blow (`SparkNeverDelays`). Reverse cost: low.

### A1403 2026-11-03 Deterministic randomness: the scatter uses a seeded stream (author-mechanics, M4.P3.4)
A scatterer is random by nature, and tests and two-round bosses need repeatable results. Options: `FMath::FRandRange` with tests that only check properties; a seeded `FRandomStream`. Chosen: `FRandomStream(Seed)` and `RandPointInBox`, with tests that check properties (inside the area, never inside a wall or another crate, every try kept or skipped) and that the same seed gives the same points in two separate worlds; the boss test computes the expected chain from the actual layout (every bomb linked to the first by steps within the reach) instead of hard-coding numbers, then requires round 2 to match round 1 exactly. Reverse cost: low.

### A1402 2026-11-03 The boss is the Fuse Yard, a chain reaction from research Boss 2 (author-mechanics, M4.P3.4)
Research section 6.4 proposes a Horde Director (waves from data, spawn points, a budget, pooled enemies) and a Chain Reaction Field (fuses that light neighbors, started from a plate). Options: either. Chosen: the Chain Reaction Field as the Fuse Yard: a plate at X 200 (World 3's, needing 1), two pillars, 14 seeded tries over a field centered at X 1200, a chain with reach 350 and spark 0.4 seconds, a first fuse of 1 second, a round that ends when nothing burns, and R only after the end. Why: Pack 4's spawning director world is the full horde director, so Pack 3 should not build it first; the chain reaction needs every idea of this world (seeded spawning around blockers, deferred configuration, timers by handle, `OnDestroyed`) and reuses World 3's plate as its trigger. Reference game: Bomberman. Extensions in order: seed choice with a best chain, the keeper as a refill line, a blast that sends the runner back. Pieces: scatter, fuse, chain; the rack and keeper stand alone (as World 2's lift did). Reverse cost: medium.

### A1401 2026-11-03 Pack 3 World 4 shape and what was pruned (author-mechanics, M4.P3.4)
Folder `04-spawning`, world id `spawning` (unique, A73), quest ids `m4-<slug>`, classes `ADojoM4<Name>`, tests `Dojo.M4.<Quest>.<Case>` (A870). Ten quests: the fuse yard breakdown; three concept quests (spawning with care, holding timers, hearing an actor leave); five builds on the fade ladder (scatter worked, fuse faded, bomb rack faded, chain stub, keeper blank); and the boss. Used from research section 6.4, as structure only and with new mechanics, numbers, and text: Spawn In A Box (scatter), Configured At Birth (rack), Spawn Budget (keeper, with slots), Timed Lock's two handles (the bomb's fuse and countdown), Next Tick (the boss's end check), Timer Versus Tick (the fuse's Alternative). Not used: Owner And Instigator (one paragraph in the card; it needs a firing pawn), Cooldown Component (Pack 1 World 1's dash and Pack 1 World 5's cooldown already build it), Lambda Timer and Slow Motion Timer (text in the card; World 6 builds payload timers), Pool Primer (the Pack 2 Object Pool world), Spawn Director Lite (Pack 4). Wording was checked for 5-word overlaps with the research files. Reverse cost: medium.

### A1637 2026-11-03 Deferred from fix round 2: the engine-side items (M4.y fix round 2)

### A1623 2026-11-03 The "fails on purpose" sentence is house style, so it is a Note and not a leak (qa, M4.y spot-check 2)
The Pack 4 World 1 reviewer called "This one fails on purpose the first time for most people" in the equipment brief a leak, since the authoring rule says the failure is revealed only after the failing step. World folder `04-interaction-and-dialogue` (its own position in Pack 4, so Worlds 2 and 3 can land beside it), world id `interaction-and-dialogue`, quest ids `s4-*`, classes `ADojoS4*`, tests `Dojo.S4.*`.
