### A1501 2026-11-03 Focus is found by overlap, as a sphere component on the hero (author-systems, M4.P4.4)
Options: a camera line trace on a custom channel; a sphere overlap; a distance scan in Tick. Chosen: the interactor is a `USphereComponent` subclass that listens to its own overlap events and keeps a list of the interactables inside, announcing a change of focus by event. Why: the sandbox test world proves overlaps by moving actors and ticking (the Pack 1 and Pack 2 examples do the same), a trace needs collision geometry the test world does not have, and the learner can still read the trace version in the quest's alternative and the breakdown's second plan. The `overlap-event` and `line-trace` glossary terms are not taught by any concept quest yet, so the world does not list them in `terms`. Reverse cost: a second piece for a trace interactor.

### A1502 2026-11-03 The interactable contract has three functions (author-systems, M4.P4.4)
`GetPrompt`, `CanInteract`, and `Interact`, as `BlueprintNativeEvent` interface functions called through `Execute_`, following Pack 1's interact quest. `CanInteract` exists so that a locked door or a busy villager is never offered, instead of being offered and refusing. Reverse cost: none.

### A1503 2026-11-03 The world's deliberate failure: announcing the end before being idle (author-systems, M4.P4.4)
Options: choice numbering that counts the raw choices instead of the visible ones; a flag set after the move; the end notice sent before the runner is idle. Chosen: the end notice. The obvious runner broadcasts `OnDialogueEnded` and then sets its state to idle, so a listener that starts the follow-up conversation from the notice is refused (the runner still says active) or has its new conversation wiped. The numbering rule is in the spec and tested separately. Nothing the learner sees before failing names it: the brief only lists the events, the rubric says each event is broadcast once in order, the Parsons block is the visible-choices filter, and the test name is `OneAfterAnother`. The hints reveal it in three steps and the alternative says to announce last. This repeats World 1's lesson (finish your own state, then announce) in a new place on purpose. Reverse cost: none.

### A1504 2026-11-03 Choice announcements come after the move, with the position among the visible choices (author-systems, M4.P4.4)
`OnChoiceMade` carries the position among the visible choices and the choice's text, and is broadcast after the flag is set and the runner has moved to the next node, so a board that listens reads the new node. When the choice ends the conversation, `OnChoiceMade` comes first and `OnDialogueEnded` after it. The number keys are 1 to 3 and map to position minus one. Reverse cost: none.

### A1505 2026-11-03 The village gate boss (author-systems, M4.P4.4)
The director finds the hero's interactor with `FindComponentByClass`, creates the one runner and flag store and gives them to the villagers, and wires keys with `EnableInput` and `BindKey` like the Pack 3 and World 1 directors. The input mode swap is a getter plus `SetIgnoreMoveInput` on the first player controller when there is one (unverified headless, as with the key binding). Goal: opening the locked gate, unlocked by the `pass` flag that the gatekeeper's third answer sets once `knows-password` is set by the gossip. Fixed numbers: reach 200, places 600, 1200, 1800 along the road, a sign 150 beside the gatekeeper. Reference game: Skyrim's guard conversations. Extensions: a second gate, a quest that reads the flags, portraits and a typewriter line. Reverse cost: none.

### A1507 2026-11-03 The interactor refocuses when it moves and collects what is already in reach (author-systems, M4.P4.4 review fix)
The first review found that overlap begin and end events alone leave the focus stale while the hero walks between two things that both stay in reach, and that a thing already overlapping when the component starts play never fires a begin event. Options: refocus in Tick; refocus on the sphere's `TransformUpdated` event; make the tests call Refresh. Chosen: bind the native `TransformUpdated` delegate to Refresh (it announces only on a change, so no polling) and seed the nearby list from `GetOverlappingActors` in BeginPlay. The seeding covers the level-load order, where the other actors have begun play before the hero's interactor binds its handlers; the sandbox test world cannot reproduce that order (an actor that has not finished initializing records no overlaps), so the test only proves a sign in reach at the start is found, and the seed itself is proven by reading the engine source, not by a test. The tests now keep the hero far from the spawn point before anything is placed, add a test that walks between two signs, a test for a thing already in reach, and a recording interactable (`ADojoS4Echo`) that proves the owner is passed on. Reverse cost: none.

### A1508 2026-11-03 The runner and the flag store are Blueprint types (author-systems, M4.P4.4 review fix)
`UDojoS4DialogueRunner` and `UDojoS4FlagStore` are `UCLASS(BlueprintType)`, because the editor steps create them with Construct Object from Class and Blueprint variables, and the engine offers only Blueprint types there. Reverse cost: none.

### A1506 2026-11-03 No new glossary terms (author-systems, M4.P4.4)
Every term the world lists is already taught by an earlier concept quest (`uinterface`, `iinterface`, `blueprintnativeevent`, `data-asset`, `tset`, and the delegate terms), so no entry was added. Reverse cost: none.

### A1449 2026-11-03 One glossary entry for instanced objects (author-patterns, Lead's go)
World 13's `instanced-specifier` and Pack 4 World 2's `instanced-subobjects` both claimed the aliases `EditInlineNew` and `DefaultToInstanced`, so main failed the verifier after both merged. Kept `instanced-subobjects` (the fuller text), added the alias "Instanced specifier", set its `firstQuestId` to `p13-bytecode-card` because Pack 2 teaches it before Pack 4 (A72), deleted `instanced-specifier`, and pointed World 13's card and trap refactor at the kept entry; A1377 and the World 13 progress entry still name the old id as history. Options: drop the two aliases from one entry (two hover cards for one idea), or keep both entries apart by wording (the same thing twice). Reverse cost: low.

### A1291 2026-11-03 A second engine slot for verifier runs in their own workspace (Lead)
Six content sessions queued for one engine lock, and a mutation batch holds it for up to two hours (each mutation rebuilds, about 7 minutes). The machine has 32 logical cores and 63 GB of memory; with Mohammad's BadBoys tests running, load was 8% and 21 GB free. Options: keep one slot (safe, but the queue grew past half a day), two slots, or one slot per session (the earlier six-at-once run got sessions killed). Picked two slots: the Lead hands a session `dojo-engine-2.lock` (same Temp folder) together with its own short workspace (`--workspace C:/dojo-ws2`), so two runs never share a verify workspace; every other session keeps `dojo-engine.lock`. The Lead assigns each slot; nobody polls for a slot without the Lead's go. Reverse: delete the second lock line in the handbook and run one queue. Later at 07:10, with two slots busy, load was under 20% and 26 GB free, so a third slot opened the same way (`dojo-engine-3.lock`, `--workspace C:/dojo-ws3`). If a run is killed by the system or load passes about 70%, the Lead drops back to two. At 07:20 two sessions' auto-mode permission checks refused to start a run on the third slot ("Interfere With Workloads"); nobody worked around it, and the third slot is closed. At 07:50 Pack 4 World 2 had two of seven runs end inconclusive while both slots and Mohammad's three BadBoys editors ran (one editor start stalled on the shared Zen server, one build hit its timeout), so the second slot closes when Pack 2 World 13's batch ends and the queue goes back to one slot. The second slot is worth retrying when the BadBoys editors are not running.

### A1470 2026-11-03 e2e launches render in software (W17, qa; one line in app-shell's `src/main/index.ts`)
Under load (other sessions' test windows, Unreal builds) the GPU process of a hidden e2e window exited with 0xC0000409 (-1073740791) and every later screenshot in that launch failed with "Unable to capture screenshot": 14 of 48 tests in one full run, on main as well. Options: retry the screenshot; give the tests longer limits; turn off hardware acceleration for test launches only (`app.disableHardwareAcceleration()` before ready, or the `--disable-gpu` switch from the harness); leave it to quiet machines. Chosen: `app.disableHardwareAcceleration()` in main when `isTestLaunch(process.env, app.isPackaged)`, which is true only for an unpackaged copy started with `DOJO_TEST_HIDDEN_WINDOW=1`. The harness sets that on every launch, also when a person watches with `DOJO_TEST_SHOW_WINDOW=1`, so watched and hidden runs render the same way. The call is in main rather than a harness switch so the decision lives next to the hidden-window rule and is unit tested. Retries and longer limits were rejected because they hide the crash instead of removing it. No spec compares pixels, so nothing is weakened; `smoke.spec.ts` now checks that `app.isHardwareAccelerationEnabled()` is false in a test launch (fix round 1: a check of `gpu_compositing` alone also passed after a GPU crash, which falls back to software compositing). Installed apps and `npm run dev` are unchanged. Reverse cost: none (one line).

### A1460 2026-11-03 Every quit attempt waits for saves, so the Unreal stop's quit waits again (M5.1g2, app-shell)
Quit held once for pending saves (up to 3 s, A1211) while the Unreal stop (up to 5 s for runs plus 15 s for the editor) ran beside it with the window open; a change made after the first wait was lost. Options: a second save wait after the Unreal stop; block further edits once quit has begun (the renderer would need a quit event, every screen would need to honour it, and an edit already in flight would still race); fold the save wait into the Unreal stop. Chosen: the first, done inside `holdQuitForSaves` alone: it holds every quit attempt for one bounded `whenIdle(3000)` and lets through only the quit it starts itself. When the Unreal stop holds that quit and quits again later, the later attempt waits for saves once more. It needs no change to `stopEngineWorkBeforeQuit` or `index.ts`, keeps the two helpers independent, and closes the gap the A1211 entry named. Bounds: nothing pending and no Unreal work exits on the next turn, as before; a hung disk adds at most one more 3 s wait after the Unreal stop (the first runs beside it); quit attempts made while a wait runs add no wait. The window between the last `whenIdle` answer and the exit is one event-loop turn. Proof: `tests/window/quitSequence.test.ts` (both helpers on one fake app whose `quit()` fires `before-quit`; the change-during-stop test fails on the old helper). Reverse cost: low.

### A1399 2026-11-03 An `economy` fake scenario drives the M3.3 e2e flows (builder-rules, M3.3)
The flows need a learner one active day short of the 7 day milestone, with a spree, armor, a Seer Stone, unchosen daily offers, and a world ready to test out. Options: reuse the `quests` scenario and set state through IPC calls in each test; patch the save on disk before launch; a new fake scenario. Chosen: scenario `economy` (`data/fake/fixtures/economyFixture.ts`): the `quests` learner and map with streak 6 (last active yesterday), spree 4 with 1 armor, 1 Seer Stone, today's three offers not chosen, and Pack 1 World 1 eligible for its test-out. The fake now also prices `viewSolution`, keeps the chest in the daily state (always a Seer Stone while one fits, else an XP potion, so the tests know what opens), lets armor absorb a missed first try, reports the streak milestone from a drill (`recordStreakDay`), and marks World 1's worked and faded builds skippable once tested out (only World 1's real quests know their scaffold in the fake). The fake ledger never pays rematch XP, so after the rematch date the fake pays less than main; the e2e flows never cross that date. Proof: `e2e/economy.spec.ts`. Reverse cost: none.

### A1398 2026-11-03 The Studio shows the chest rewards that had no screen (builder-rules, M3.3)
A chest can hold an XP potion or a studio cosmetic, but no screen showed either, so those rewards looked like nothing (ui-core listed `studio.cosmetics` as "not shown yet"). Options: leave them for a later Studio pass; show them where they act. Chosen: the lead programmer card shows an active potion with its share and end time (`xp-potion`), and the engine room lists the studio touches from chests (`studio-cosmetics`). Cosmetics still change nothing visual beyond the list; drawing them is a later Studio task. Reverse cost: none.

### A1397 2026-11-03 Test-out shows on the map, in the drill, and in its result (builder-rules, M3.3)
The rules already tested a world out and marked its worked and faded builds `skippable`, and the map had a Test out button, but nothing showed what a test-out gave. Options: show only a badge; mark each skippable stage and explain in the drill. Chosen: each skippable, unpassed stage carries a small skip mark with "Tested out: you may skip it, or play it for the practice" in its tooltip, the legend explains the mark, the contract's Tested out badge counts the stages that may be skipped, the test-out drill's header names its world and says bosses never skip, and its result names the world either way (tested out, or "not tested out yet" with the missed items due for review), then goes back to the map. Skipping needs no button: a skippable quest already counts as met for its dependents (A700). Reverse cost: none.

### A1396 2026-11-03 The spree at stake shows on the Build screen; armor no longer spends itself on a spree of 0 (builder-rules, M3.3)
The spree mattered on Submit but the Build screen said nothing about it, and the loot showed armor only as a text suffix. Options for display: the Character meter only; a note beside Submit. Chosen: `SpreeStakeNote` under Run and Submit says whether this Submit is the first try that counts, what a pass makes the spree, when the bonus starts, and whether armor covers a miss (words from `lib/spreeStake.ts`, unit tested); the Studio's Today panel shows the spree meter with armor; the loot says when the spree bonus is on and calls out "Armor absorbed the missed first try". Rule change in `SpreeRules`: armor was spent on a missed first try even with a spree of 0, protecting nothing. Options: keep it; spend armor only while the spree is above 0. Chosen: only above 0, so a learner's armor is never wasted (unit tested). Reverse cost: low.

### A1395 2026-11-03 A chest opens on screen, a streak milestone is celebrated, and a month badge is named (builder-rules, M3.3)
The loot and drill finish drew an earned chest already open (no reward moment), a milestone was a text suffix on the loot and absent from the drill finish, and the month badge in the outcome was never shown. Options: one shared reward block; per-screen code. Chosen: `Chest` takes `opensOnMount` (drawn closed for two frames, then the lid opens with the reward motion tokens); `DailyReward` shows the opening chest with one line on what the reward does, and the month badge when earned; `StreakCelebration` is the one reward motion for a milestone (`reward-rise` in `components/rewardMotion.css`, zero under reduced motion), naming the next milestone from the rules and never using guilt copy. Both are on the loot and the drill finish. Reverse cost: none.

### A1394 2026-11-03 The daily state keeps today's chest, and a chosen offer has a Start button (builder-rules, M3.3)
The Studio could choose an offer but not open it, and its chest always drew closed because the save did not say what today's chest held. Options: read the chest from the last attempt; store it in the daily state. Chosen: `DailyQuestState.chest` (optional, so no save migration; a new day's state has none) is written in the same save step that opens the chest. The Studio draws it open with its reward once done, the chosen offer has "Start today's pick" (a drill offer opens today's due drill, a quest offer its quest; `lib/dailyQuestDisplay.ts`), "Done today" when finished, an empty state when there are no offers, and the month so far ("N of M days"). Reverse cost: low.

### A1393 2026-11-03 The daily drill offer is made only when a scheduled item is due today (builder-rules, M3.3)
A drill pays and finishes the daily offer only when it holds a scheduled due item (A320), but the daily quest always offered a drill. On a day with nothing due (every first day) a learner who picked it could never finish it. Options: let a practice drill finish the daily offer; leave the drill offer out on such a day; keep it and explain. Chosen: leave it out, matching the spec's "an offer with no candidate is left out": `DailyCandidates.hasDueDrillItems` comes from `getScheduledDueItemIds()` when the day's offers are made. Letting practice count was rejected because it would pay for drilling nothing due. Reverse cost: low.

### A1392 2026-11-03 The Seer Stone flow on the Build screen (builder-rules, M3.3; replaces A358)
After the failed-submit count the Seer Stone offer is now a working button. It opens `SeerStoneDialog`: two choices side by side, "Use a stone" (keeps the quest's full first-pass XP; disabled with a reason when none is held) and "Pay with XP" (nothing leaves the total, but a pass before the rematch pays no XP and the rematch pays the rules' share), both naming the rematch in the rules' days. Fix round 1: the price is worded as the quest's first-pass XP rather than the base number, since the band multiplier and hints change the real amount, and "Pay N XP" read as if XP left the total. The answer shows in the same dialog as `ReferenceSolutionView` (file tabs, read-only `CodeBlock`, what the view cost, when the rematch is due). Once viewed, the offer reads "Show the solution again (free)" and opens the files at once. Options considered: a separate route for the solution; the dialog. Chosen: the dialog, so the learner's code stays where it was. Reverse cost: none.

### A1391 2026-11-03 Viewing a solution again, or after a pass, is free and schedules nothing (builder-rules, M3.3)
`SeerStoneRules.viewSolution` charged and rescheduled on every view, and a view of a passed quest scheduled a rematch that paid 50%: pass, view, wait 3 days, rematch, repeat would farm XP. Options: refuse views of passed quests; make them free with no rematch; keep charging. Chosen: a repeat view answers the files and changes nothing (no stone, no new rematch date), and a passed quest's view is free and schedules no rematch, closing the farm. Asking to spend a stone with none held is refused (`invalid-payload`) instead of silently paying with XP, and a quest with no reference is refused before anything is charged. Fix round 1 (reviewer finding): `XpRules.passXp` checked the rematch first, so a learner who spent a stone and passed on or after the rematch date got 50%, the same as paying with XP, and the stone bought nothing. Now a first pass with no unpaid view pays in full whatever the date, then a due rematch pays 50%, then 0, as A121 intended ("With a stone, the pass pays in full"). Unit tested. Reverse cost: low.

### A1390 2026-11-03 `viewSolution(questId, useSeerStone)` reads `tools/solutions` through a `SolutionSource` (builder-rules, M3.3; small app-shell and ui-quests changes)
The Seer Stone flow needs the reference files (A358, A406 shipped them as `solutions/`). Options: reuse unreal-pipeline's `ReferenceSolutions` (needs the Unreal services, which an unsupported platform cannot build); a progress-owned reader. Chosen: `SolutionSource` with `SolutionFolderReader` (files directly in `<solutions>/<quest-id>/`, name order; a quest id that is not one folder name answers null), passed to `ProgressContentService` as an optional last argument (tests that never view a solution leave it out). Only build and refactor quests answer, and only the quest's own files (a boss folder's test file never leaves main). The answer is `SolutionViewing` (files, stone spent, stones after, XP forfeited, rematch due, already viewed). Cross-owner lines, logged like A954: the method and channel in `src/shared/ipc.ts`, its handler and `useSeerStoneSchema` in `src/main/ipc`, the reader built in `src/main/index.ts` (app-shell); the fake World 1 fixture now carries each build and refactor quest's own reference files, read by `tools/fake/print-fake-world-fixture.ts` with the same reader (ui-quests, A365). Reverse cost: low.

### A1308 2026-11-03 Two messages get advice for the screen they show on (builder-ui-core, M5.1k review)
Main's `sandbox-path-too-long` advice ("Open Settings, choose Set up the sandbox again, and pick a short folder") fits a quest run, but `explainSandbox` sends the same error to first run's and Settings' build steps, where it would send a first-run learner out of an unfinished setup. And the preflight's refusal of Rebuild sandbox says "open Settings" to a learner already there. Options: one wording per message in main; a context-aware override in the renderer. Chosen: the renderer, in `lib/sandboxFailure.ts`: `describeSandboxFailure` replaces the path-too-long advice with "Go back, choose a short folder such as C:\Dojo, then start the build again" on a setup build and "Use Set up the sandbox again and choose a short folder" on a rebuild, and `describeRebuildRefusal` words Settings' refused rebuild as "or use Set up the sandbox again". Why: main does not know which screen shows the error, the message, code and detail stay main's, and quest runs keep the advice that fits them. Reverse cost: low.

### A1307 2026-11-03 A failed or cancelled setup build is started again as a rebuild of its copy (builder-ui-core, M5.1k review; also fixes first run)
`SandboxProjectService.create` copies the template and saves the sandbox path before it builds, and refuses a folder that is not empty, so after a failed or cancelled first build every "Build again" or "Start again" was refused (`sandbox-folder-not-empty`) on first run and in Set up the sandbox again. The fake never refused, so the e2e tests passed. Options: delete the copy in main on a failure or cancel (unreal-pipeline, and it throws away a copy that is fine); let create accept a folder holding its own earlier copy (unreal-pipeline, more rules); have the build step call `rebuildSandbox` once its first try ended with a result. Chosen: the third, in `BuildStep` (a ref set when the status becomes failed or cancelled). Why: the sandbox in settings is already that copy, so a rebuild is exactly a clean second try (it deletes the build products first), it needs no change in main, and the advice "Try the build again" becomes true. An errored start (a refused folder, an engine error) still retries `createSandbox`, since nothing was copied. The fake now refuses a second create into a folder holding a copy and remembers the sandbox before building, as main does, so the existing first-run retry tests cover this. Reverse cost: low.

### A1306 2026-11-03 A fake scenario for a deleted sandbox (builder-ui-core, M5.1k)
The e2e test needs a returning learner whose sandbox is gone. Options: drive the real bridge with a sandbox path that does not exist (it needs a working engine for the engine check that runs first, which CI does not have); add a fake scenario. Chosen: `sandbox-deleted` in the fake, which reports the sandbox not ready and refuses Rebuild sandbox with the same words as main's `sandboxMovedOrDeleted` (`missingSandboxFixture`) until `createSandbox` passes. The real main process path is proven by the engine test `tests/integration/sandboxSetupAgain.test.ts` instead. Reverse cost: low.

### A1305 2026-11-03 One build context type with a third value, setup-again (builder-ui-core, M5.1k)
`SandboxBuildContext` existed twice (the tracker and `lib/sandboxFailure.ts`) with the same two values. Options: reuse "first-run" for the Settings flow; add "setup-again". Chosen: one type in `data/SandboxBuildTracker.ts`, re-exported by `lib/sandboxFailure.ts`, with "setup-again". Why: first run's template-error advice says "pick a different engine version in step 1", and there is no step 1 in Settings; "setup-again" says "pick another engine above". A lost run gets first run's advice (Rebuild sandbox), because the new folder already holds the copy; the rebuild advice is now the special case. Reverse cost: low.

### A1304 2026-11-03 Every message about a gone sandbox names an action that works (builder-ui-core, M5.1k; strings in unreal-pipeline and app-shell files)
Four messages sent the learner to something that could not recreate a sandbox. Chosen wording: the preflight (`sandboxMovedOrDeleted`) and the rebuild check (`sandboxNotReady`) say to put the folder back or open Settings and choose Set up the sandbox again, and that progress is kept; `sandbox-path-too-long` says to choose Set up the sandbox again with a short folder such as C:\Dojo (the way to move a sandbox after a quest run hit the limit; on a build step the renderer gives screen-specific advice, A1308); `sandboxFolderNotEmpty` says to choose another folder or empty this one, and that Rebuild sandbox is for the sandbox already in Settings (it used to send a learner whose old folder was half deleted to a rebuild that is refused). `SettingsService.completeFirstRun`'s refusal is shown only on first run's last step, which has no Back button and cannot be finished from Settings, so it says to put the folder back or open the Title screen and choose Set up your studio, not the Settings button. Options considered for that one: point at Settings (the learner would still be stuck in first run); add a Back button to the done step (a ui-core change outside this task). These files belong to unreal-pipeline and app-shell; only message strings changed, in separate commits, and their specs are updated. Reverse cost: low.

### A1303 2026-11-03 A refused rebuild offers no retry, and the setup steps hide the last rebuild (builder-ui-core, M5.1k)
Rebuild sandbox on a deleted sandbox fails with `sandbox-not-ready`, and Try again can only fail the same way. Chosen: that error shows without Try again (its advice names the new button). Opening Set up the sandbox again hides the last rebuild's outcome, since it was about the sandbox being replaced, and Rebuild is off until the steps close; the button is off while a rebuild runs. The engine is captured when the steps open (held by `EnginePanel`), so an environment refresh that loses the engine cannot close them in the middle of a build, and Pick another engine is off while they are open (with the reason in a tooltip), because `create` saves the engine it built with and would silently undo a pick made meanwhile. A failed setup build whose cause is a path too long offers no Build again, since a rebuild in the same folder fails the same way. Known gap, left as it is: choosing a folder whose `DojoSandbox` still holds files gives main's folder-not-empty refusal, whose "use Rebuild sandbox in Settings" is off while the steps are open; the way out is Back, then Cancel. Why: only one sandbox build runs at a time, and a learner never sees an old red error next to a new build. Reverse cost: low.

### A1302 2026-11-03 The button is always there when the engine works, and the old folder is left alone (builder-ui-core, M5.1k)
Options: show the button only when the sandbox looks missing; always show it. Chosen: always, off only while the environment has not answered or the engine in Settings does not work (a line says to pick a working engine first). Why: it is also the only way to move a sandbox to a short folder after `sandbox-path-too-long`, and "not built" also covers a sandbox whose first build failed. A line under the path, shown only when Settings names a sandbox the environment calls not built, says to put the folder back or use the button. Setting up again never deletes or touches the old folder (it may be a moved folder the learner wants back); settings point at the new sandbox once the copy is made, as first run does. Reverse cost: low.

### A1301 2026-11-03 Folder and first build only, no engine step and no editor step (builder-ui-core, M5.1k)
First run has five steps. Options: all five; folder, build, and editor; folder and build. Chosen: folder and build, with the engine in Settings. Why: the engine is already chosen and has its own Pick another engine in the row above; the editor step is a first-time introduction to the editor, not part of making the sandbox work (every run opens what it needs). Reverse cost: low (the editor step could be appended).

### A1300 2026-11-03 Set up the sandbox again opens inside Settings' Sandbox row, built from first run's own steps (builder-ui-core, M5.1k)
A learner whose sandbox folder was deleted or moved had no way to recreate it (problems.md, M5.1e). Options: reopen the first-run route in a "set up again" mode (it would also ask for the engine, end on Enter your studio, and leave Settings); a dialog over Settings; the steps opened inside the Sandbox row. Chosen: inside the row, as `screens/settings/SandboxSetupAgain.tsx`, which renders first run's `SandboxStep` and `BuildStep` unchanged except for two optional props (`backLabel`, `context`). Why: no logic is duplicated (same picker, blockers, warnings, `createSandbox` call, progress, quiet notice, cancel, and M5.1 error views), the learner stays where they found the problem, and the screen keeps its width at 1100x700. The Sandbox row moved out of `EnginePanel` into `SandboxRow.tsx` to keep one job per component. Reverse cost: low.

### A1339 2026-11-03 No custom object channel in a build quest (author-mechanics, M4.P3.3)
Research section 6.3 proposes a Projectile Channel exercise with a custom object type. Options: add a channel to the sandbox template's `DefaultEngine.ini`; teach it in text only; skip it. Chosen: text only, in the channels concept card (custom channels appear as `ECC_GameTraceChannel1` and onward) and in the pawn gate's Alternative (a Runner channel instead of `ECC_Pawn`). Why: a channel lives in Project Settings, so adding one changes the sandbox every quest shares, and an existing learner sandbox would need a rebuilt template; the gate teaches the same skill, a response per object type changed while the game runs, with an engine channel. Reverse cost: low (a later quest can add the channel together with a sandbox template change).

### A1338 2026-11-03 Physics-simulated hits are taught in concept cards only (author-mechanics, M4.P3.3)
Simulation Generates Hit Events and `NormalImpulse` belong to collision. Options: a build quest with a simulating body; concept text only. Chosen: concept text (`m3-overlap-and-hit` and `m3-shapes-and-actors`, with a question each) and the cracking wall's Alternative. Why: every World 3 test is deterministic because shapes move by teleports and sweeps; simulated bodies need the physics scene to step and would make hit timing depend on the solver, which the test world has not been proven on. The facts were checked in UE 5.8 source: a swept move's hit is dispatched to both shapes through `UPrimitiveComponent::DispatchBlockingHit` with a zero impulse and no flag check (`AActor::InternalDispatchBlockingHit`). Reverse cost: low.

### A1337 2026-11-03 Reuse on purpose: World 2's ball and wall are the things that hit (author-mechanics, M4.P3.3)
Author rule 10 asks each world from world 3 on to reuse an earlier library actor and say so. Chosen: `ADojoM2Bumper` and `ADojoM2Wall` are reused unchanged in the cracking wall quest (the ball cracks the wall by bouncing) and the pawn gate quest (the ball still bounces off an open gate), named in both briefs and on their "Compiles against earlier quests" lines, with `m2-bumper` in their prerequisites. The cracking wall also lists `sweep`, a World 2 term, which satisfies the verifier's repetition rule. Reverse cost: low.

### A1336 2026-11-03 The gate opens to Overlap, not Ignore, and only a pawn holds it open (author-mechanics, M4.P3.3)
A gate that only lets runners through could switch collision off, ignore pawns, or overlap pawns. Chosen: `Open` sets the box's `ECC_Pawn` response to Overlap, so the box still knows who is in the doorway, and shutting waits until `GetOverlappingComponents` holds no shape whose object type is Pawn; other overlapping shapes, such as a crate's handle, do not hold it open. The test `OpenGateStillStopsTheRest` fails the collision-off answer, and `NeverShutsOnARunner` fails the Ignore answer and the "any overlap" answer. Why: it is the room's real need (the plate can be released while the runner stands in the doorway, and a box turned solid around a pawn traps it), and it makes the object type, not the actor's class, the thing that decides. Reverse cost: low.

### A1335 2026-11-03 Two glossary entries, eleven first-quest claims, and two corrected entries (author-mechanics, M4.P3.3)
New entries: `collision-enabled` (NoCollision, QueryOnly, PhysicsOnly, QueryAndPhysics) and `get-overlapping-actors` (with GetOverlappingComponents and the IsOverlapping checks). First quests set on entries that had none: `collision-channel`, `collision-response`, `collision-preset`, `box-component`, `sphere-component`, `capsule-component` to `m3-channels-and-responses`; `overlap-event`, `hit-event`, `generate-overlap-events` to `m3-overlap-and-hit`; `physics-simulation` to `m3-shapes-and-actors`; and the two new entries to the cards that teach them. Corrected: `collision-preset` said OverlapAllDynamic "overlaps only moving things", but in UE 5.8's `BaseEngine.ini` it overlaps every channel and differs from OverlapAll by its own object type; the entry now also warns that OverlapOnlyPawn leaves other channels at Block. `hit-event` now says swept hits need no flag and carry a zero impulse. `sweep` and `fhitresult` keep World 2 as their first quest (A1134). The glossary has 219 entries. Reverse cost: low.

### A1334 2026-11-03 Test stand-ins: a runner that is a plain actor, a crate with two shapes, and bare props (author-mechanics, M4.P3.3)
Collision tests need things with shapes and object types. Options: `ACharacter` or `APawn`; plain actors with shapes. Chosen: `ADojoM3Runner` (a plain `AActor` whose root sphere has the Pawn profile, with a respawn point) is given in full in the flag quest, and `ADojoM3Crate` (a BlockAllDynamic body and an OverlapAllDynamic handle on top, with a home) in the plate quest; the tests also give bare `ADojoTestActor`s one sphere with a named profile through `FDojoTestWorld::AddComponent`. Everything moves by `SetActorLocation` (overlaps update after a teleport) or a sweep. Why: no movement component, controller, or gravity to make results depend on frames, and it shows on purpose that the object type is a setting on the shape, not the actor's class (a concept question asks exactly that). Reverse cost: low.

### A1333 2026-11-03 The world's deliberate failure is the plate counting shapes (author-mechanics, M4.P3.3)
Research rule 5 asks for one planned first-run failure per world. Options: a handler without UFUNCTION (the flag's header is given, so it cannot happen); opening the gate by switching collision off (the brief must state that the ball still bounces, which gives it away); a plate that adds one per begin overlap. Chosen: the plate. `OnComponentBeginOverlap` fires once per pair of shapes (UE 5.8 `UPrimitiveComponent::BeginComponentOverlap`), and the crate brings a body and a handle, so the natural count says 2 for one crate; `ACrateIsOneThing` catches it with "A crate on the plate is one thing, though its body and its handle both touch the zone", while `CountsRunners` (one shape each) passes. Nothing seen before failing says why: the brief and rubric state only that a crate counts as one; the overlap concept card does not mention pairs; the second hint is the first to say that one thing can have several shapes; the plate has no Parsons block; the concept card that explains pairs, `m3-shapes-and-actors`, comes right after the plate. Reverse cost: low.

### A1332 2026-11-03 The boss is the Vault Room, a plate puzzle that counts things rather than mass (author-mechanics, M4.P3.3)
Research section 6.3 proposes a Weighted Button Room (mass on a button, a dispenser, a return chute) and a Hazard Course. Options: either as proposed; a room built from this world's pieces. Chosen: the Vault Room, built from the flag, gate, plate, and pit, with a lever that drops at most 2 crates at a chute, a plate that needs 2 things, a gate that follows the plate, a pit that returns crates and counts the runner's falls, and a vault trigger owned by the room; R restarts only after the vault. Things are counted, not weighed, because mass needs simulated bodies (A1338); the puzzle still works, since the runner alone on the plate cannot also walk through the gate. The Hazard Course was not used because World 2's boss is already a hazard course. The plate gains one addition in the boss, `OnCountChanged` (shipped as `tools/solutions/m3-boss-vault-room/m3-scale-plate/`, A67 and A215), and the room adds a second handler on the pit's zone, so a multicast event with two listeners is part of the integration. Reference game: Portal. Two reference plans (`cpp-room`, `blueprint-room`), and the breakdown carries `room-listens` and `plate-drives-gate`. Extensions in order: carrying crates with a grab sphere, two plates and two gates, best time. Reverse cost: medium.

### A1331 2026-11-03 Pack 3 World 3 shape and what was pruned (author-mechanics, M4.P3.3)
Folder `03-collision`, world id `collision` (unique, A73), quest ids `m3-<slug>`, classes `ADojoM3<Name>`, tests `Dojo.M3.<Quest>.<Case>` (A870). Ten quests: the vault room breakdown; three concept quests (channels and responses, overlap and hit events, shapes and actors); five builds on the fade ladder (checkpoint worked, cracking wall faded, pawn gate faded, scale plate stub, return pit blank); and the boss. Used from research section 6.3, as structure only and with new mechanics, numbers, and text: Checkpoint Flag (checkpoint), Fragile Floor (cracking wall, hits instead of physics), Projectile Channel and One-Way Platform (folded into the pawn gate, a response changed per object type while the game runs), Speed Stack's enter and exit ordering (the plate's count), the kill zone (the pit). Not used: Gravity Well, Wind Tunnel, and Normal Bounce (they need a character and its movement component), Damage Field (timers belong to World 4), Surface Sound (needs physical material assets), Ragdoll Dummy (needs a skeletal mesh), Named Zones (kept as the "filter by shape" option in the shapes concept). Wording was checked for 5-word overlaps with every research file: the matches were engine names, test boilerplate, and stock phrases, and one phrase from our own author rules was reworded. Reverse cost: medium.

### A1330 2026-11-03 Pack 3 World 3 opens after World 2's boss (author-mechanics, M4.P3.3)
Options: require `m2-boss-clockwork-hall`; require nothing. Chosen: `m3-vault-plan` requires `m2-boss-clockwork-hall`, the same chain as World 2 after World 1 (A1130). The cracking wall and pawn gate also require `m2-bumper`, whose classes they compile against. Assigned under A71: this world uses A1330 to A1349. Reverse cost: none.
### A1317 2026-11-03 One diagnostics parser for clang and MSVC (builder-unreal, W15)
The rules of A411 (a header error placed on the snippet's instantiation note, else another snippet note, else the include line, else line 1; `ok` needs exit code 0 and no error; a killed run is a timeout) lived inside the clang checker, and MSVC prints the same structure (an error in an engine header, then `note: see reference to function template instantiation` lines, one of them in the snippet). Options: copy the parser into the Windows checker with MSVC's line format; one parser that takes each compiler's line format as a `CompilerDialect`. Chosen: the second (`snippetDiagnostics.ts`; the prelude and the line-preserving rewrites moved to `snippetSource.ts`). The clang checker's 13 unit tests pass unchanged apart from one import. One addition after review: diagnostics identical in line, column, severity, and message are reported once, because cl repeats a template error for each column of the same header line (clang never prints such twins, so nothing changes there). Not run on a Mac. Reverse cost: low.

### A1316 2026-11-03 Every platform has a snippet checker, so `not-implemented` is gone from the syntax check (builder-unreal, W15)
`QuestRunService.snippetChecker` was nullable for Windows (A402). Options: keep the null branch for an unknown future platform; make it required. Chosen: required. `createUnrealServices` already refuses any platform but Windows and macOS (`platformUnsupported`), so the branch could never run. The choice between the two checkers moved into `createUnrealPlatform` (`createSnippetChecker`), the one place that branches on the platform (D16), which also gives the Windows compiler search the same `ProgramFiles(x86)` value as the toolchain check (review finding). Reverse cost: none.

### A1315 2026-11-03 The Windows toolchain check also needs a Windows SDK of 10.0.19041.0 or newer (builder-unreal, W15)
A missing SDK showed up only as a build error (`visualStudioNotUsableByBuild`). Options: leave it to the build; ask vswhere for an SDK component (component ids differ per SDK and per Visual Studio version); look on disk the way UBT does. Chosen: on disk. `WindowsSdkFinder` reads `KitsRoot10` from `HKLM\SOFTWARE\Microsoft\Windows Kits\Installed Roots` (64-bit view, then `/reg:32`), always also `%ProgramFiles(x86)%\Windows Kits\10`, and counts a `10.0.x.y` folder under `Include` only when its `um` headers are there, because an uninstall can leave an empty version folder. The newest must be 10.0.19041.0 or newer. The Lead's brief named 10.0.22621.0, and the first version used it, but the review found that UBT itself accepts less: `Engine/Config/Windows/Windows_SDK.json` in UE 5.6, 5.7, and 5.8 gives `"MinVersion": "10.0.19041.0"` and only prefers 10.0.22621.0 (`"MainVersion"`). A blocking problem disables Continue on the first-run screen, so the engine's own minimum is the honest line; the check does not warn between the two, because UBT builds there. UE 5.4 and 5.5 are not installed here, so their minimum was not read. The problem blocks builds and says to tick the newest Windows 11 SDK in the installer; it names the newest SDK found when there is one. Checked only once Visual Studio is found, so a PC without Visual Studio gets one message, not two. On the Windows desktop the root is `C:\Program Files (x86)\Windows Kits\10\` and the folders are 10.0.19041.0, 10.0.22621.0, and 10.0.26100.0. Reverse cost: low.

### A1314 2026-11-03 The engine registry reads the 32-bit view only when the 64-bit view lists no launcher engine (builder-unreal, W15)
A164 read only the 64-bit view. Options: always read both views; read the 32-bit view (`reg query ... /reg:32`, which is `WOW6432Node`) only when the 64-bit view lists no engine; leave it. Chosen: the second, for the launcher key only. On the Windows desktop the 64-bit view lists UE 5.6 and 5.7, and the 32-bit view holds only a stale 4.0 entry, so reading both would add noise for nothing (the locator drops engines older than 5.4 anyway). The source-build key is under `HKCU\Software`, which is not split by view. Reverse cost: none.

### A1313 2026-11-03 A precompiled header cl cannot read is one plain error that says to rebuild the sandbox (builder-unreal, W15)
The check reuses the sandbox's shared precompiled header (A1310). When it was built by another MSVC version (C1853), is damaged (C1852), or is missing (C1083 on the `.pch`), the generic answer would be "The compiler failed:" followed by a long compiler line. Options: that generic line; throw `sandbox-not-ready`; a line 1 error with a plain message. Chosen: a line 1 error ("The syntax check cannot use the sandbox's last build: it was made by another Visual Studio compiler version, or its files are missing. Rebuild the sandbox in Settings, then check again."), the same shape as the Mac's stale header case (A411), so the scratch buffer view shows it like any other result. Reverse cost: none.

### A1312 2026-11-03 MSVC messages keep their code after the text (builder-unreal, W15)
cl prints `error C2065: 'DeltaSecs': undeclared identifier`. Options: drop the code; put it first, as cl does; put it after the text in brackets. Chosen: after, `'DeltaSecs': undeclared identifier [C2065]`, the way clang appends its warning flag (`[-Wunused-variable]`), so the learner reads the sentence first and can still search the code. Reverse cost: none.

### A1311 2026-11-03 The Windows snippet check uses the cl.exe UBT built the sandbox with, found from the response file, then vswhere (builder-unreal, W15)
The response file does not name `cl.exe` (A402). Options: vswhere and Visual Studio's default MSVC version; the MSVC folder the shared response file's `/external:I ".../VC/Tools/MSVC/<version>/INCLUDE"` line names; both in order. Chosen: both, the response file first. On the Windows desktop UBT built with MSVC 14.50.35717 while Visual Studio 2026's default is 14.51.36231: the default compiler would refuse the precompiled header (C1853), so only UBT's own compiler gives the fast check. When that folder is gone, `vswhere -latest -products * -requires Microsoft.VisualStudio.Component.VC.Tools.x86.x64 -property installationPath` and `VC\Auxiliary\Build\Microsoft.VCToolsVersion.default.txt` give the default (which then answers A1313's rebuild message until the sandbox is rebuilt). No compiler at all is `toolchain-missing`. Reverse cost: low.

### A1310 2026-11-03 The Windows snippet check is `cl /Zs` with UBT's own response file and precompiled header (builder-unreal, W15)
Replaces A402. Options: a UBT single-file build (10 s or more); a hand-made cl command with guessed include paths; UBT's per-source response file with the source swapped, like the Mac (A403). Chosen: the third. Windows sandboxes use unity builds, so `DojoTestActor.cpp` has no response file of its own (it is folded into `Module.Dojo.N.cpp`); the donor is `Dojo.cpp.obj.rsp`, else the first other `*.cpp.obj.rsp` in `Intermediate/Build/Win64/x64/UnrealEditor/Development/Dojo`, as long as it names `@"...Dojo.Shared.rsp"`. The check drops the source line, `/Fo`, `/experimental:log`, and `/sourceDependencies`; keeps the shared response file, the forced includes, and `/Yu` and `/Fp` of the shared precompiled header; and adds `/FI` of the prelude (A403's), `/Zs`, and the snippet. It runs from `Engine/Source` (the include paths are relative to it) and reads stdout, where cl prints its diagnostics. Measured by hand on the Windows desktop: 0.8 to 2.7 s per check. Keeping UBT's warning flags means the check reports what the real build would, including C4458 (a parameter named like an actor member), which clang never reports (A1050). The prelude needs no MSVC-only additions: `#pragma once` in a main file and a blanked `Super::` call raise nothing under `/Zs`. Paths in the response file are written as UTF-8; a sandbox path with letters outside the system code page is unverified. Only the `x64` folder is read (no Windows on Arm). Reverse cost: low.
### A1379 2026-11-03 World 13 review fix round 1 (author-patterns, M4.P2.13)
Reviewer verdict FIXES NEEDED on cd33f40, then PASS on a7ed98f. Changed:
- `ADojoP13Potion::Drink` is no longer `const`. UnrealHeaderTool marks a const `BlueprintCallable` function that has an output as `BlueprintPure` (`UhtFunction.cs`), and a pure node has no exec pins and runs again on every pull, so the 02 editor step and the boss Blueprint plan could not have run it. Options: drop `const`; keep it and add `BlueprintPure = false` (one more specifier to teach). Chosen: drop it; the marked line says why.
- Enum values take the A66/A267 initials with display names (A1371).
- Four 5 and 6 word overlaps with the research notes and the chapter's "play a sound" instruction were reworded (A1372).
- The Run Parsons prompt pins its first line, so only one order is correct (A314 grades exact order).
- Tests: the boss pins the 150 range (a press 151 from the purse), the label's fizzle count, Health, and Gold, and a win time measured from the round start (a 2 second gap before round 2); the door feeds And, Or, and Not values other than 0 and 1, so bitwise, additive, and subtractive versions fail.
- The boss spec says an opened door counts as used; the refactor's editor step places the trap it compares against; the worked quest's alternative notes `int32` overflow from a hostile recipe.
Not changed: `RoutineHoldsNoObjects` checks only object properties and arrays of them (the routine has no maps or sets); the card item `blueprint-is` and `bytecode-recall-1` stay close on purpose. Reverse cost: low.

### A1378 2026-11-03 World 13's engine proof (author-patterns, M4.P2.13)
Proven on Windows, UE 5.8.4, 2026-11-03, headless (`-NullRHI`), on the Lead's second engine slot (A1291, `dojo-engine-2.lock`, workspace `C:/dojo-ws2`), at b88c91f. Isolated run (`--pack patterns --solutions` a folder holding only the four p13 references): all eight World 13 files ok; the three build and refactor references build and pass, and the three starters compile and fail as designed (first build 123.8 s cold, solution tests 22.7 s, starter checks 212.2 s). Mutations, each a single edit to a copy of the references, run with `--quest` on the quest it belongs to (about 40 s each); every one was caught by exactly one test: stats not reset at round start, the later entry winning a tie, a 190 range, a 70 second round, a fizzled potion left unused, the win time measured from the start of play, and a WIN label without Health and Gold each fail `TwoRoundsPlayAlike` except the 70 second round, which fails `TimeRunsOut`; an opened door not marked used fails `TwoRoundsPlayAlike`; the door's operands popped in the wrong order (the deliberate failure) fails only `GreaterAndLessReadAsWritten`; a bitwise And and a Not written as 1 minus the value fail `EqualAndLogicDecide`; a potion run without the working copy fails `BrokenRecipeChangesNothing` (gold 5 kept); a stack not reset fails `MachineRunsAgainAfterAFault`. Full run with the default solutions (`--pack patterns`, 41 quests): no problems, no inconclusive checks; first build 800.9 s, solution tests 242.9 s, starter checks 9201.3 s (the machine was shared with slot 1 and Mohammad's editors). A first try of the mutations died in 2 s each because the shared `node_modules` was being reinstalled by the merge script; they were rerun with the Lead's go. Not proven: macOS (A75), and the boss's key bindings (headless tests have no player controller, so they call `PressUse` and `RestartRound` directly). Reverse cost: none.

### A1377 2026-11-03 Glossary and card changes for Bytecode (author-patterns, M4.P2.13)
Five new glossary entries, each first taught by a World 13 concept quest (A72): `stack-machine` (cpp idiom), `blueprint-vm` (reflection; Blueprint's script bytes on `UStruct::Script`, `FFrame`, `EExprToken`, the runaway check against `MaximumLoopIterationCount`), `edit-condition` (reflection), and `uparam-ref` (reflection), all in `p13-behavior-as-data`; and `instanced-specifier` (reflection, `linkMode: code-only` because "Instanced" also starts the `instanced-static-mesh` term and is an ordinary word) in `p13-bytecode-card`. None of the hover texts used before the card names the pattern (A373). Docs links were checked against Epic's site: a "Blueprint compiler overview" page does not exist at 5.8's flat paths (it returns the 31 KB shell), so `blueprint-vm` links the `EExprToken` API page, `stack-machine` the `FFrame` API page, and `uparam-ref` the `EPropertyFlags` page (which lists `CPF_ReferenceParm`). Options for the links: Blueprint landing pages (they never mention the interpreter); the API pages (chosen, they name the exact types). The Bytecode card in `patterns.json` was audited item by item: `intent`, `problem`, `unrealForm`, and `whenNot` rewritten around the vault; `bytecode-recall-3` reworded (it said "native DLLs" and "touch memory"); added `bytecode-recall-4` (the three sandbox limits), `bytecode-recall-5` (program versus run state), `bytecode-scenario-3` (dialogue rules, a which-pattern with State, Observer, and Type Object as distractors), and `bytecode-scenario-4` (a multiple-choice when-not: a hot particle path). Use cases: `p13-potion-recipes` and `p13-door-conditions`. No card item mentions operand order (A1374). Reverse cost: low.

### A1376 2026-11-03 The boss is the modded vault, with Minecraft adventure maps as the reference game (author-patterns, M4.P2.13)
The patterns research names no Bytecode boss for World 13; its Part 4 "moddable spell workshop" belongs to the combos world and is built on spells, the source chapter's own example. Options: that workshop renamed; a card duel whose effects are programs; a vault whose potions and doors come from one mod list. Chosen: the vault, because its pieces are this world's two builds unchanged and it puts the sandbox in the round itself: the Cursed brew is a broken recipe that must fizzle without changing anything while the round goes on. Fixed numbers: use range 150, a 60 second round, starting stats Health 50, Stamina 20, Gold 0, Keys 0, and a six-entry standard mod. Tie rule: the earlier entry wins. A potion is used up even when it fizzles; a shut door can be tried again; every door open wins. Reference game: Minecraft adventure maps, where map makers script doors and rewards with command blocks inside the game's sandbox. The trap routine refactor is the first extension rather than a piece, because boss pieces must be build quests. The reference integration (A67) is the C++ plan; the Blueprint plan uses only the pieces' BlueprintCallable and BlueprintPure members. Reverse cost: low.

### A1375 2026-11-03 The refactor moves run state out of shared step objects, and its sharing test compares twins frame by frame (author-patterns, M4.P2.13)
`p13-trap-refactor` starts with a routine Data Asset of `Instanced` step objects (`UDojoP13TrapStep`, `EditInlineNew`, `DefaultToInstanced`), where a Wait step keeps `WaitedSeconds` on itself. Traps that share one routine asset then share that wait. The refactor turns the step into the plain struct `FDojoP13TrapStep` in the same header and keeps the step index and wait time on the trap. Options for the failing test: count shots of two sharing traps started together (rejected: the shared counter gets twice the time and hands completions to one trap or alternately, so for some float roundings the counts match a lone trap's); compare totals (rejected, totals match by construction). Chosen: `TrapsShareOneRoutine` starts the sharing traps ten frames apart beside two twins with routines of their own and compares every frame for 190 frames, so any shared state shows up as the first frame that differs, whatever the rounding; the reference is exact because each trap does the same float sums as its twin. A second failing test, `RoutineHoldsNoObjects`, walks the routine's own properties by reflection (`EFieldIteratorFlags::ExcludeSuper`, array inners included) and fails on any object property, so keeping step objects and moving only their state is caught too. `OneTrapKeepsItsRhythm` and `RunawayRoutineJams` pass before and after. Semantics kept from the starter: a finished wait ends the trap's frame, 16 steps in one frame jam it, a jump to a missing step jams it. Reverse cost: low.

### A1374 2026-11-03 World 13's deliberate failure: comparisons read in the order written (author-patterns, M4.P2.13)
Options:
- A potion that changes the stats part way before failing: stated openly in the worked quest and tested there.
- A runaway trap routine: a hang would stop the test run, and the budget is given in the refactor starter.
- `Less` and `Greater` in the door quest written with the first value taken off the stack on the left.

Chosen: the last. A stack gives back the value pushed last first, which is the right-hand side of `ReadStat Gold, PushNumber 9, Greater`. The natural first `ApplyStep` takes two values and compares them in the order taken, which is right for `Equal`, `And`, and `Or` and wrong for the comparisons, so only `GreaterAndLessReadAsWritten` fails (the treasurer's door opens for Gold 5 and not for Gold 12). The worked quest's machine names its operands `Top` and `Under` and only has `Add` and `Multiply`, which give the same answer either way round, so it neither shows the trap nor models a wrong habit. The brief states the requirement by example, as a requirement must be stated; the concepts, the rubric, the card, card items, and glossary entries never mention operand order; the hints carry the reasoning. Engine check: see A1378. Reverse cost: low.

### A1373 2026-11-03 The machines are small on purpose (author-patterns, M4.P2.13)
Choices, each with the option not taken:
- Values are `int32` only (not a tagged union): every op stays one line, and stats are whole numbers.
- A step is a `USTRUCT` with an op `UENUM` and its operand inline (`Number`, `Stat`), with `EditCondition` and `EditConditionHides` showing only the field an op uses (not the source chapter's design, where a stat is chosen by a value popped off the stack). The Details panel is the front end; a text assembler is the boss's second extension.
- A fixed stack of 8 values (`TInlineAllocator<8>` in the potion machine, a local `TArray` checked against `StackLimit` in the door).
- A broken program is refused with false or `Broken`, never `check`; the Parsons distractors in quests 02 and 04 are both `check` lines.
- The potion machine runs on a copy of the stats and keeps it only on success, so a failed recipe changes nothing; the door's `Judge` is const and three-valued (`Open`, `Shut`, `Broken`), so a broken condition fails closed.
- Potions and doors have no jumps, so they always end; control flow and the per-frame step budget come only with the trap routine.
Reverse cost: medium once learners have saved progress.

Not used in briefs, cards, choices, distractors, or card items: wizards, spells or a duel, health/wisdom/agility stats, sound and particle instructions, the chapter's literal and add instruction names, its stat-by-popped-index design, or its remarks on the Interpreter pattern's object tree beyond the contrast every text on the subject makes. The setting is a vault of potions, doors, and dart traps written by designers and modders. The arc:

- `p13-potion-recipes` (worked: the learner writes `Run`).

- `p13-bytecode-when-not`.

- Folder `13-bytecode`; world id `bytecode`, unique across packs (A73).

### A1358 2026-11-03 Glossary and pattern card additions for the ability world (author-systems, M4.P4.2)
Options: no new terms, as in World 1 (A1156); new entries for every new idea (cooldown, energy, effect); entries only for Unreal features the world is the first to teach. Chosen: the third. `gameplay-tags` already existed but no quest taught it, so its `firstQuestId` is now `s2-gameplay-tags`. `instanced-subobjects` is new (EditInlineNew, DefaultToInstanced, `UPROPERTY(Instanced)`; category reflection; Epic's 5.8 property specifiers page), first taught by `s2-instanced-effects`. Cooldown, energy, and effect stay game design words. Pattern cards: `s2-ability-data` is a Type Object use case, `s2-effects` a Subclass Sandbox use case, `s2-energy` and `s2-ability-component` Component and Observer use cases; Type Object gains `type-object-recall-5` (per-user time left lives with the user, not in the shared asset) and Subclass Sandbox gains `subclass-sandbox-scenario-4` (effect kinds sharing a toolbox). Reverse cost: low.

### A1357 2026-11-03 Energy is whole points on a timer that runs only below full (author-systems, M4.P4.2)
Options: a float raised in `TickComponent`; whole points on a looping timer; a stored "last full" time worked out on demand. Chosen: whole points (tests never compare floats), a looping timer started by a spend only when it is not already running (a second spend must not push the next step later, which `SpendAgainMidRefill` checks), cleared by the step that reaches the top and by `Refill`. The pool fills in `InitializeComponent` with `bWantsInitializeComponent`, the rule Pack 2's `component-recall-4` teaches, so a part spending in its own BeginPlay finds it full. The tick-based float and the on-demand time are named in the quest's alternative. Reverse cost: low.

### A1356 2026-11-03 The sparring ring boss (author-systems, M4.P4.2)
Fixed numbers: ring 600, four dummies 200 from the middle, energy 100 coming back 5 per half second, Dash 20 and 1 s and 300 (blocked by Rooted), Shockwave 40 and 3 s, reach 400, push 300, roots 1 s (blocked by Channeling), Focus free and 12 s, refills, roots and channels 2 s, turns of 90 degrees. Reference game: Overwatch's hero kits. Options for where a move's effects live: a map from ability to effects on the director; an `Effects` list in the ability asset (an A215 override of `s2-ability-data`); the ability component running effects itself. Chosen: the asset list, so "a new move made of existing effects is a new asset" holds, with the director running a started move's effects from `OnAbilityActivated`; the component-runs-effects version is the second reference plan. The refill effect is a third Subclass Sandbox child written in the boss folder. The reference `ADojoS2RingDirector` owns no rule: it grants, routes keys to `TryActivate`, turns the hero, runs effects, rewrites the board on the three events, counts dummies out, and restarts after the finale with `Refill` and `ResetAll`. Tests: `TwoRounds` plays a fixed 14.6 second route twice (including a dash refused while Focus still roots the hero after the shockwave's root ends, a shockwave refused for energy, and R ignored mid-round), `NothingAfterTheFinale`, `OutIsMeasuredFlat` (a raised dummy within 600 on the ground is still in), `EffectsComeFromTheAsset` (two effects in a changed asset), `UnbindsWhenItLeaves` (which also checks the director never ticks). Extensions: a second hero sharing the assets, a wind-up move, seconds left on the board from a timer that runs only while something waits. Reverse cost: low.

### A1355 2026-11-03 Effects are a Subclass Sandbox of instanced objects that find targets by actor tag (author-systems, M4.P4.2)
Options for effects: an enum and a switch in the component; `TSubclassOf` effect classes tuned through Blueprint children (the Gameplay Ability System's gameplay effects); abstract `EditInlineNew` UObjects created and tuned inside the asset. Chosen: the third, taught first by the concept quest `s2-instanced-effects`, with a public non-virtual `Apply` that refuses a missing user, one protected `OnApply` hook, and two protected tools (`MoveActor`, `FindTargetsNear`). Options for what counts as a target: actors with a gameplay tag interface; a class filter; actors whose actor tags include a configurable `TargetTag` name. Chosen: the actor tag (default `Target`), because dummies need no new interface and the concept card already contrasts actor tags with gameplay tags. The effects quest is the world's blank piece: base plus two kinds from nothing. Reverse cost: low.

### A1354 2026-11-03 Ability component rules (author-systems, M4.P4.2)
`CanActivate` checks granted, not cooling, not running, not blocked by the user's current tags, and affordable (a free ability needs no pool); `TryActivate` changes nothing unless `CanActivate` allows it, then pays, starts a cooldown timer if the cooldown is above 0 (a rate of 0 would clear the timer), starts a run timer and adds the granted tags if the run is above 0, and broadcasts `OnCooldownChanged` (when a cooldown started) and then `OnAbilityActivated`. Each ability has its own handle in `TMap<TObjectPtr<UDojoS2AbilityData>, FTimerHandle>` maps and its own `FTimerDelegate::CreateUObject` payload; a cooldown's end removes its handle before broadcasting, so a listener asking `IsOnCooldown` hears false. Options for the clock: world time compared on request; timers. Chosen: timers, because the board needs an event when a move becomes ready; the world time version is the quest's alternative. `ResetAll` exists for rounds. Reverse cost: low.

### A1353 2026-11-03 Native gameplay tags declared in C++ (author-systems, M4.P4.2)
The world needs three tags (`DojoS2.State`, `DojoS2.State.Rooted`, `DojoS2.State.Channeling`). Options: Project Settings, which writes `DefaultGameplayTags.ini` in the sandbox template (owned by unreal-pipeline, and every learner's sandbox would need it); `RequestGameplayTag` of unregistered names (fails); native tags with `UE_DECLARE_GAMEPLAY_TAG_EXTERN` in a header and `UE_DEFINE_GAMEPLAY_TAG_COMMENT` in a .cpp, inside the `DojoS2Tags` namespace. Chosen: native tags, given in full in the data quest, so the content needs no config change. Checked in 5.8's `NativeGameplayTags.cpp` and `GameplayTagsManager.cpp`: a native tag constructed after the manager exists is added to the tree at once. The test `TagsAreRegistered` proves the tags and their parent exist on the engine. The sandbox `Dojo.Build.cs` already lists `GameplayTags`. Reverse cost: low.

### A1352 2026-11-03 The world's deliberate failure: overlapping runs that share a tag (author-systems, M4.P4.2)
Options: a refused start that still pays (the natural code calls `CanActivate` first, so few would hit it); one shared timer handle for every cooldown; removing a run's granted tags one by one when it ends. Chosen: the third, in the stub quest `s2-ability-component`. The header gives `ActiveTags` as a container and an `EndRun` to fill, so the obvious `RemoveTags(Ability->GrantedTags)` frees a hero that Focus still roots once the shockwave's shorter root ends. `FocusAfterShockwave` catches it, and so does the boss's `TwoRounds`. Nothing before failing names it: the breakdown's acceptance lines, rubric, reference plans, and after-pass note, the quest's brief, rubric, concepts, Parsons blocks, and the test name are neutral; the hints reveal it in three steps, and the alternative, shown after a pass, compares rebuilding from the runs still going with the Gameplay Ability System's tag counts. Reverse cost: none.

### A1351 2026-11-03 Shape of Pack 4 World 2 (author-systems, M4.P4.2)
Options: World 1's shape with no concept quests (A1151); the same shape plus concept quests for the two Unreal ideas no earlier concept quest teaches. Chosen: the second, eight quests: the breakdown (`s2-ability-plan`, gate bosses as prerequisites), the concept `s2-gameplay-tags`, four pieces on the fade ladder (worked `s2-ability-data`, faded `s2-energy`, stub `s2-ability-component`, blank `s2-effects`), the concept `s2-instanced-effects` before the blank piece, and the boss `s2-boss-sparring-ring`. Without the concept quests every quest listing `gameplay-tags` would carry an A72 warning. Patterns combined: Type Object (abilities as assets, "Kinds as data"), Component (ability and energy components, "Actors built from parts"), Observer (the three events and the board, "The lens, then the first payoff"), and Subclass Sandbox (effects, "Many kinds, one toolbox"); the first build requires the last quests of the Type Object, Observer, and Subclass Sandbox worlds (`p10-type-object-when-not`, `p1-observer-when-not`, `p8-subclass-sandbox-when-not`). Reverse cost: low.

### A1350 2026-11-03 Pack 4 World 2 is the ability system (author-systems, M4.P4.2)
The task asks for the next system after inventory and equipment in the order of `research/gamedevtv-exercise-design.md` section 6.10. Options: 2 Quest System, the next in that list; 3 Ability System, the next in `docs/product.md`'s Pack 4 list; 4 Save and Load. Chosen: the Ability System. The Quest System is already Pack 3 World 6's boss (`m6-boss-quest-system`), product.md's six Pack 4 worlds leave it out for that reason and put the ability system second, and a second quest system would repeat a boss the learner has already passed.
