### A1290 2026-11-03 CI runs macOS only by hand or for a release tag (Lead)
Mohammad said Windows is what matters now and the Mac version can wait. CI run 41495639538 on main failed only because GitHub never gave its macOS job a runner (cancelled after 15 minutes), which emails him a failure. Options: keep both platforms on every push; mark the macOS job allowed to fail; run macOS only on a manual run (`workflow_dispatch`) and for a release tag. Chosen: the third, in both `test` and `package` of `ci.yml`; `release.yml` still builds both installers for a tag. Why: a push to main now passes or fails on Windows alone, the macOS check is still one click away (`gh workflow run ci.yml`), and an allowed-to-fail job would still sit cancelled for 15 minutes on every push. The M5.1m macOS fix and the other macOS items stay under "Unverified on a platform" in problems.md. Reverse cost: low (one line in each matrix).

### A1139 2026-10-27 Hits are a timer in the hall, not Tick, and the boss director owns no motion (author-mechanics, M4.P3.2)
The Clockwork Hall boss could have let each hazard check the hero in its own Tick, or let the hall move the hazards. Options: hazards test the hero themselves and broadcast; the hall moves everything; the hall holds the hazards and tests distance on a 0.1 second timer. Chosen: the third for the reference, with the hero-sensor variant as the breakdown's second plan. Why: it keeps the four hazards ignorant of the hero, the hall, and the score (so each stays testable alone and reusable), and it teaches the point that a rule about two things together does not need a frame. The hall never ticks. Reverse cost: low.

### A1138 2026-10-27 Movement components are a concept quest, not a build (author-mechanics, M4.P3.2)
The brief says "movement components where they fit". Options: a build quest that replaces the dart or bumper with `UProjectileMovementComponent`; a concept quest after the dart that compares the component with hand-written Tick movement; alternatives only. Chosen: a concept quest (`m2-movement-components`) after the dart, plus an Alternative section in the bumper and dart quests that names the component's bounce and homing settings. Why: the world's builds are about the rule of the motion (ends, bounce, turn limit, arrival), which a component hides, and a component-only build would pass trivially on the engine's own code. The glossary gains `movement-component`, and `projectile-movement-component` now has a first quest. Reverse cost: low.

### A1137 2026-10-27 The lift has no arrival delegate (author-mechanics, M4.P3.2)
A lift that announces its arrival is natural. Options: a dynamic multicast `OnArrived`; polling `IsMoving`. Chosen: no delegate in this quest, only `IsMoving`, `GetCurrentFloor`, `GetTargetFloor`, and the label. Why: a test cannot bind a dynamic delegate without a UObject listener class, delegates are World 6's subject, and the extension to listen for arrival belongs with the pillar extension of the boss. Reverse cost: low.

### A1136 2026-10-27 Deterministic movement tests (author-mechanics, M4.P3.2)
Movement tests need exact frames. Rules used in every quest: `TestWorld.Tick(n)` at the default 1/60 second unless a test is about frame length; checks sit at frames that are not on the edge of an event (the patrol's ends are checked at frames 125 and 250, with a range wide enough for a clamp fix and a fix that carries the leftover distance over); the engine clamps a frame to 0.4 seconds, so no test asks for a longer one; distances compare with a tolerance of 0.5 to 1 unit; rotations with 1.5 to 3 degrees; and frame-rate tests run two separate `FDojoTestWorld` scopes. The boss hit timer is checked 8 frames (0.13 s) after a move so one check always lands. Reverse cost: none.

### A1135 2026-10-27 Sweep tests use a provided wall class (author-mechanics, M4.P3.2)
A swept move needs something to hit, and the test helper actor has no collision. Options: build a box component in the test; ship a small provided class. Chosen: `ADojoM2Wall` (a box with the `BlockAll` profile) is given in full in the bumper quest, in the style of the satchel in World 6's item quest, and the boss reuses it through `Quests/m2-bumper/DojoM2Wall.h` (named on the boss's "Compiles against earlier quests" line). The ball root is a sphere with `BlockAllDynamic`. Reverse cost: low.

### A1134 2026-10-27 Four glossary entries and three first-quest claims (author-mechanics, M4.P3.2)
New entries: `world-and-local-space`, `add-actor-offset` (AddActorWorldOffset and its three siblings), `interpolation` (the VInterp, RInterp, and constant families), `movement-component`. This world's concept quests are the first to teach `sweep`, `fhitresult`, and `projectile-movement-component`, so their `firstQuestId` now points at them (A72). If the collision world (Pack 3 World 3) is written later it should treat `sweep` and `fhitresult` as already taught and add its own terms. No existing entry text changed. Reverse cost: low.

### A1133 2026-10-27 The world's deliberate failure is the patrol's turn at an end (author-mechanics, M4.P3.2)
Research rule 5 asks for one planned first-run failure per world. Options: an unscaled mover (already in World 5's follower); an ease-out lift that never arrives; the accumulating patrol that carries a long frame's overshoot past its end. Chosen: the patrol. The natural first code adds the step, flips at the end, and leaves the block beyond its end for a frame; the slow-frame test (`SlowFramesStayOnThePath`, 0.4 second frames, the longest the test world allows) catches it as "Even with slow frames, the patrol stays between its two ends". Nothing the learner sees before failing says why: the brief, rubric, breakdown, and glossary describe the property (between its ends) but not the overshoot, the second hint is the first to talk about the frame that passes an end (the same ladder as A874), and the alternative note avoids the words drift and overshoot. The quest keeps the distance along the path in a private field already declared, so the failure is a clamp, not a design trap. Reverse cost: low.

### A1132 2026-10-27 The boss is the Clockwork Hall, built from four pieces (author-mechanics, M4.P3.2)
Research section 6.2 proposes a Clockwork Gauntlet (five hazards on one master clock with phase offsets) and a Rollercoaster Cart (spline). Options: build either as proposed; a smaller hall whose hazards are this world's builds; the cart. Chosen: a hall with four hazards that are the world's builds (crusher is the patrol, boulder is the sled in its own frame, bouncer is the bumper between two walls, dart is the dart), a fixed layout and numbers (2000 unit hall, hit radius 100, check every 0.1 seconds, boulder every 4 seconds, dart every 3), a hit and finish rule, and R for a fresh round only after the finish. Reference game: Fall Guys. The master clock with phases becomes extension 2, the lift pillar extension 1, so the research idea is kept in order of importance. Spline work (Pack 3 module 2 exercises 8 and 10 in the research) is not used: it needs a spline component the sandbox tests do not construct, so the cart is left for a later pass. Two reference plans: `cpp-hall` and `blueprint-hall`; the breakdown carries `hall-checks` and `hero-sensor`. Reverse cost: medium.

### A1131 2026-10-27 Pack 3 World 2 shape and what was pruned (author-mechanics, M4.P3.2)
Folder `02-transforms`, world id `transforms` (unique, A73), quest ids `m2-<slug>`, classes `ADojoM2<Name>`, tests `Dojo.M2.<Quest>.<Case>` (A870). Ten quests: the Clockwork Hall breakdown first; three concept quests (space and sweeps, interpolation, movement components); five builds on the fade ladder (sled worked, patrol faded, bumper faded, lift stub, dart blank); and the boss. Used from research section 6.2, as structure only and with new mechanics, numbers, and text: Local Versus World (sled, with Frame-Rate Proof folded into its tests rather than a quest of its own, because Tick and DeltaSeconds are Pack 1 World 5's), Elevator Call (lift), Homing Dart (dart), plus a patrol (the research's distance accumulator with reversal) and a swept bouncing ball (the Module 2 microjam's sweeping obstacle idea, kept small). Not used here: Pendulum, Conveyor Belt (needs overlap, Module 3), Bezier Lob, Spline Train, Breathing Idle, Ride Along (attachment), Yaw-Only Watcher, Shared Clock. Pendulum, Breathing Idle, and Shared Clock fit the boss's extension 2; Ride Along fits the collision world. FQuat basics are in the space and interpolation concept cards because Pack 1 World 3 already teaches FVector, FRotator, and FTransform. Checked for 6-word and 5-word overlaps with the three research files: one phrase from the research's frame-rate exercise ("covers the same distance per second") was reworded; the rest are engine names and stock English. Reverse cost: medium.

### A1130 2026-10-27 Pack 3 World 2 opens after Pack 1 World 5 for now, to be repointed when World 1 merges (author-mechanics, M4.P3.2)
Pack 3 World 1 (Enhanced Input, `p3-world1-input`) is written at the same time, so its last quest does not exist on main. Options: no prerequisite; `f5-boss-beacon-drill`, the quest Pack 3 World 6 opens with (A871); a guess at World 1's last quest id. Chosen: `f5-boss-beacon-drill` on `m2-hall-plan`, the only quest in the world that has a prerequisite outside it. When World 1 merges, repoint `m2-hall-plan`'s `prerequisites` to World 1's last quest, the boss `m1-boss-drone-handoff` (World 1's author confirmed the id; keep `f5-boss-beacon-drill` only if World 1 does not already require it), and merge World 2 after World 1. World 2 uses no Enhanced Input; the hero in the boss is moved by code and R and the pieces are driven by the tests. Assigned under A71: this world uses A1130 to A1139. Reverse cost: none. Update 2026-11-02: World 1 merged (892bb76), `m2-hall-plan` now requires `m1-boss-drone-handoff`, and nothing else in World 2 waits on Pack 1 World 5 any more.
### A1263 2026-10-27 The run-errors e2e jumps to the file start with the key Monaco uses on each platform (M5.1m, m5-1 lead)
CI run 40488859958 failed `e2e/run-errors.spec.ts` on macos-latest only (three tests; Windows passed). Two causes; this entry is the first and A1264 is the second. The helper `insertAtTop` clicked into the editor and pressed `Control+Home` to reach the first line. In Monaco that moves to the start of the file on Windows and Linux, but on a Mac the binding is Cmd+Up (Monaco's `cursorTop` is Ctrl+Home with a Mac override of Cmd+Up), so the cursor stayed where the click landed, mid-line in the quest file. This fully explains the 1440x900 failure: the CI screenshot shows the marker at the end of line 17, so it was not on a line of its own, the fake engine did not see it, and the Run gave an ordinary "tests 0 of 1" result. It does not explain the two 1100x700 failures, where the Run never started (A1264). The helper was the bug here, not the fake outside-failure scenario or the explainer rules: the matching has no Windows-only signature for Live Coding, a full disk, Xcode or the Apple linker (unit tests in `tests/unreal/buildFailureExplainer.test.ts`). One Mac wording problem was found and is queued, not fixed (problems.md: the path-too-long advice names Windows). Options: press both keys (hides which one worked); drive Monaco through its API (the editor is not exposed to the page); pick the key by `process.platform`, as the other specs do for the shortcut modifier. Chosen: the third, plus a check that the line really is the first rendered line, so a wrong position fails at once with its own message. Reverse cost: none, test code only. Not verified on a Mac here: the new key itself (see problems.md).

### A1264 2026-10-27 The quest header never wraps, so the Run button cannot move mid-click (M5.1m, m5-1 lead)
In the macOS CI run both 1100x700 tests clicked Run and no run ever started (no progress view, no result, no error). The cause is a layout shift, reproduced on Windows: `QuestTitleRow` was `flex-wrap`, and at 1100 wide the save status "Unsaved changes" (wider than "Saved") did not fit beside the title, so the header wrapped onto a second line and pushed everything below it, including the Run button, 28 px down (Run top 131 to 159 px). Pressing Run blurs the editor, which saves at once, the status goes back to "Saved", the header unwraps and the button moves up again between mouse down and mouse up, so the click lands elsewhere and is lost. It depends on timing, which is why Windows usually passed. A learner on a window that narrow, with an unsaved edit, can lose a Run or Submit click the same way, so this is a product fix, not only a test fix. Options: reserve the status's width (a fixed minimum, wrong for the long "Could not save" text); always give the status its own line (wastes a row); make the row not wrap and let the title truncate, which it already can (`truncate` on the h1). Chosen: the last, in `QuestTitleRow` (used by the Build, Breakdown and Concept screens; only the first two pass an action, so only they change, and only when the title is long). New test `at 1100x700 the Run button does not move when the save status changes`: it fails on the old header (131 against 159) and passes on the new. Reverse cost: restore `flex-wrap` and the row gap. Not verified on a Mac: that the lost click there is this one (the wrap is the only size-dependent shift found, and the CI screenshots show the Run column idle).

### A1280 2026-10-28 `--engine` builds in one persistent workspace per engine version, outside Temp (W14, builder-content-engine)
Mohammad found 77 `dojo-verify-*` folders of about 2.6 GB each in `%TEMP%`. `EngineSandbox.create` copied the template to a new temp folder every run, and `EngineVerifier` removed it only when everything passed; a failure, an inconclusive starter, a cancel and a thrown error all left it (the removal was not in a `finally`), and an engine proof scoped to one world nearly always fails on the worlds it does not have. Options: (1) keep the temp copy and remove it in a `finally`; (2) a persistent workspace that every run reuses; (3) both. Chosen: (2) alone, because (1) still copies the template and rebuilds the whole Dojo module from nothing on every run (minutes), and (3) keeps a code path that can fill Temp. Nothing the verifier makes now lives in `tmpdir()`. The workspace is `%USERPROFILE%\.unreal-dojo\verify\<major.minor>` on Windows (about 50 characters, under the 100 the build wants; see A1286 for why it is not under AppData as first built) and `~/Library/Caches/UnrealDojo/verify-workspace/<major.minor>` on macOS (`$XDG_CACHE_HOME` or `~/.cache` elsewhere). `--workspace <folder>` or the environment variable `DOJO_VERIFY_WORKSPACE` moves the root that holds the per-version folders; the flag wins. Builds are incremental, so the second run of a pack reuses the unchanged parts of the project build. Cost: the workspace is a few GB that stays until deleted by hand (it is a cache, so deleting it is safe). Reverse cost: low (`EngineSandbox.create` is the one place).

### A1281 2026-10-28 Each run syncs the workspace to the template and removes every quest the template lacks (W14)
The Dojo module is one module, so one stale quest file breaks every build. Options: remove only the quests this run's `install` knows about; mirror the template. Chosen: mirror. Before anything is written, `syncWorkspace` writes the template files that differ or are missing (a file whose bytes already match is not touched, so UBT does not see a newer timestamp and rebuild it), writes the `.uproject` with the engine association and the A41 plugin filter, and removes every file and folder the template does not have, which takes out every quest folder and test file of the last run, including one left by a killed run. `Binaries`, `Intermediate`, `Saved` and `DerivedDataCache` are skipped in both directions, as before (A184). A template that changes between runs is picked up the same way. Cost: this run's solutions are rewritten even when identical to the last run's, so the Dojo module's quest files recompile each run (A1285 has the timings); the engine modules do not. `TIMESTAMP_GAP` is kept.

### A1282 2026-10-28 A workspace lock keeps two verifier runs apart, separate from the agents' engine lock (W14)
Two runs from different worktrees must never share a workspace. Options: reuse the agents' engine lock; a `mkdir` lock next to the workspace. Chosen: `<workspace>.lock`, a folder that appears already holding `owner.json` (process id, host, start time, working folder): a run makes `<workspace>.lock.pending-<token>` with the owner file inside and renames it to `<workspace>.lock`, which fails while a lock exists because the lock is never empty (the first version made the folder with `mkdir` and wrote the owner after; the review showed a second run could break a lock in the gap). A waiting run logs the lock path and who holds it every 30 s and polls every 3 s, for as long as it takes (a legitimate run can last hours). It breaks the lock only when the holder is provably gone: its process id does not exist on this machine, or the machine restarted after the lock was made (ids are reused), or a lock folder has had no owner file for 60 s, or the lock is older than 12 hours (a recycled id on a machine that has not restarted; a full pack's starter checks take about two hours). Breaking renames the folder aside and checks the owner it took is the one judged dead; if not, it puts the folder back. Release removes the folder only if it still names this run. Windows reports EPERM, not EEXIST, when the target exists or is being deleted; that is treated as a wait, and a real EPERM with no lock folder to explain it is thrown after 20 polls. The agents' engine lock stays what it is (it keeps sessions from loading the machine); this one protects the files. A lock is released by renaming it aside and then removing it, so a waiter never sees a half-emptied lock folder; a stale lock that cannot be removed (something holds a file in it open) ends the run after 20 polls with a message naming the folder. Pending, broken-aside and released-aside folders a killed run left are removed once they are 10 minutes old. Known gaps: a process id recycled by an unrelated program on a machine that has not restarted holds the lock for up to 12 hours (delete `<workspace>.lock` by hand); Windows Fast Startup does not reset the uptime the restart rule reads, so only a real Restart makes it fire (it can only miss a dead lock, never break a live one); three runs racing within milliseconds to break the same stale lock can drop a lock that changed hands (the first two renames are atomic, the restore is not); and if the verifier process is killed hard, its UnrealBuildTool or editor child may still be running when the next run breaks the lock and starts building (stop it first).

### A1283 2026-10-28 Failure logs stay in the workspace, the last five runs (W14)
`keptSandbox` is replaced by `logsFolder` on `EngineReport`: `<workspace>/Saved/VerifierLogs/<UTC stamp>/` holds `build-N.log`, `tests-N.log` and the test reports of that run, and the five newest run folders are kept (older ones are removed when the next run starts). It is set on every report, pass or fail, and `runEngineStage` prints it. There is no kept sandbox to inspect any more; the project is the workspace, and the next run overwrites it. Reverse cost: none.

### A1284 2026-10-28 Every exit path releases the workspace, and the integration suite removes its temp sandboxes with retries (W14)
`EngineVerifier.verify` takes the sandbox, then runs the stages inside `try ... finally { sandbox.release() }`, so a pass, a failure, an inconclusive starter, a cancel (AbortSignal, also while waiting for the lock) and a thrown error all release the lock; a failure to prepare the workspace (for example the folder cannot be created) becomes an `(engine)` problem. A hard kill of the process leaves the lock, which the next run breaks because the process is gone (A1282). Separately, `afterAll` in `fastLoop`, `fastLoopWiring`, `headlessLoop`, `runQuest` and `submitQuest` removes its `dojo-*` temp folder with `maxRetries: 10, retryDelay: 300` (three `dojo-fast-loop-*` folders were left, probably by an editor still holding files), and the two that dispose an editor first now remove the folder in a `finally`, so a throwing dispose cannot skip it. No assertion changed. Unit test: `tests/content/verifyWorkspace.test.ts` points the system temp folder at an empty folder and checks it is still empty after a pass, a failure, a crash mid-build and a cancel.

### A1285 2026-10-28 Measured: the persistent workspace on UE 5.8.3, Windows, `--pack patterns` (W14)
Two identical runs in a row (37 quests judged, 77 solutions installed), then one run with a solutions folder that holds only World 1, `%TEMP%` listed before and after each. Workspace `C:\Users\Mohammad Shehab\.unreal-dojo\verify\5.8` (48 characters). Run 1 (workspace created, so a cold build): exit 0 in 1193 s, first build 110.2 s, solution tests 23.2 s, starter checks 1057.7 s. Run 2: exit 0 in 1192 s, first build 95.8 s, solution tests 19.1 s, starter checks 1075.5 s. Scoped run (3 quests, 34 files fail for the missing worlds): exit 1 in 113 s, first build 13.5 s. No `dojo-*` entry in `%TEMP%` at any point; the entry counts moved only by other programs' files (`.tmp` GUID files, `UECrashContext-<pid>.xml` and `*-debug.log` from the Unreal processes, a few kilobytes). An earlier cold run in the Packages-redirected path, with other sessions building at the same time, took 519.3 s for the first build, so the cold-versus-warm gap depends on machine load; on a quiet machine the second run's first build is about 13 percent shorter. The reason it is not much shorter: every run installs its 77 solutions again after the sync removed last run's quests (A1281), so the Dojo module's quest and test files, a large part of the work, are recompiled each run; what a warm run saves is the unchanged parts of the project build (the shared precompiled header, generated headers, UBT's makefile and the link state; the cause is not measured separately). Starter checks (about 30 s per quest) are the bulk of a full pack and are unchanged. A follow-up, not done here: keep a quest file whose bytes already match, so a second run of the same pack recompiles nothing (needs the writer to compare before writing and the sync to leave the quest area to the install step).

### A1286 2026-10-28 The one-second build gap is kept between runs, and the Windows workspace lives in the user profile, not AppData (W14 review round 1, Lead request)
Persistent build outputs mean a quest file rewritten within a second of the last build can be skipped by UBT (M1.8 saw this inside one run). The previous run may release the lock right after a build and the next may start within that second. Options: wait a fixed second at the start of every run; remember when the last build ended. Chosen: the second. Each build writes its end time to `<workspace>/Saved/LastBuildEnded.txt` (also when the build throws), and `EngineSandbox.create` waits until it is more than 1.1 s old before syncing the template, so nothing is written before then. A run that follows a long pause pays nothing. Also: `EngineSandbox.create` no longer resolves the workspace path with `realpath` on Windows (macOS still does, for the `/var` link).

**MSIX redirection (reviewers: read this).** The Claude desktop app is an MSIX package. Windows redirects the writes a packaged app (and every process it starts, including the verifier and UBT) makes under `%LOCALAPPDATA%` into the package's storage, `C:\Users\<name>\AppData\Local\Packages\Claude_pzs8sxrjxfjjc\LocalCache\Local\...`. The first W14 build put the workspace at `%LOCALAPPDATA%\UnrealDojo\verify-workspace\5.8`, and `realpath` showed the redirected path, about 118 characters, over the 100 the build wants (UBT's object paths pass 260 on top of it). It was also hidden from Mohammad in Explorer and would be lost when the Claude app is reset. Options: keep `%LOCALAPPDATA%` with a length check (fails on every agent run); `%USERPROFILE%\.unreal-dojo\verify\<major.minor>` (not redirected, visible, about 50 characters); a folder in the repository (`<repo root>\.verify-workspace`, gitignored: one workspace per worktree, so every worktree would do its own cold build of about 9 minutes and its own few GB). Chosen: the user-profile folder; one shared workspace for every worktree is the point of the lock. macOS keeps `~/Library/Caches` (no redirection there). Every run logs `engine: verify workspace path <path> (<n> characters)` first, and on Windows a path of 100 characters or more is an `(engine)` problem naming `--workspace` and `DOJO_VERIFY_WORKSPACE`, so a long path can no longer build quietly. A path the user gives under AppData is redirected the same way when the verifier is started from the app; the length check catches it, the redirection itself is not detected.

### A1100 2026-10-27 World 12 fix round 1 (author-patterns, M4.P2.12)
Reviewer verdict FIXES NEEDED, all taken except two notes. Changed:
- The deliberate failure was named in the rubric, the card, a card item, and the brief's opening line (the reviewer disagreed with the first wording of A1093). All now say only "from the parent"; the brief opens on Swift; the editor steps use Swift.
- Resolve's four "borrow" lines and four clamp lines are now two small functions on the row (`BorrowUnsetFrom` and `ZeroUnset`), and the chain guard is in the loop condition, so the Parsons block has one valid order (A314 grades exact order).
- The kit's editor steps compared printed object names, which are the same ("Charm") before and after; they now use an Equal (Object) node and set the favor on the source only.
- Wording: a copy is "offset by the template's own place" (it lands at the template's place only for a spawn at the origin); a spawn template gives the copy its own constructor-made components but copies other references as the same pointer; DuplicateObject copies every saved (non-Transient) property.
- The references' class comments now say "Prototype pattern" (A373); the quest 2 starter does not.

Not changed:
- The rubric bullet "spawns the tuned blade's own class" has no test: a test would need a native subclass of the blade, which a test file cannot declare (UHT only reads headers). It is graded by the rubric review only.
- The LayBlade Parsons block has three lines that work in any order (`SetActorLocation`, `Wake`, `Add`); exact-order grading (A314) marks another correct order wrong. This affects every Parsons block, not only this world, and is raised for the Lead.

Reverse cost: low.

### A1099 2026-10-27 Engine builds were blocked by an open windowed editor; the proof passes -NoHotReloadFromIDE locally (author-patterns, M4.P2.12)
While a windowed editor with Live Coding was open on this machine (Mohammad's KAEL project, which runs the installed `UnrealEditor.exe`), every sandbox build failed in about 10 s with "Unable to build while Live Coding is active". UnrealBuildTool names that check's mutex after the editor executable, which every project on the installed engine shares. Options: wait for Mohammad to close his editor; close it myself (no, never); change the app's build call. Chosen for this proof only: an uncommitted local edit adds UBT's own `-NoHotReloadFromIDE` flag to `UnrealBuilder.ts`, which turns that guard off for the sandbox build (the sandbox is never open in an editor), and the edit is reverted before the commit. A real fix belongs to the owner of `UnrealBuilder.ts`: the same flag in the committed build call. Reverse cost: none.

### A1098 2026-10-27 World 12's engine proof (author-patterns, M4.P2.12)
Proven on Windows, UE 5.8.3, 2026-10-27, headless with -NullRHI, against a scratch solutions folder holding only the three p12 folders. Each reference builds and passes its tests, and each starter compiles and fails (build 71.8 s, solution tests 13.7 s, starter checks 121.3 s, 209 s for the whole run). Mutations caught: the one-level Resolve fails only GrandchildBorrowsThroughItsParent (the deliberate failure, A1093); a LayBlade without Wake fails TunedStaysOutOfPlay (so a copy of a parked blade really is parked); a LayBlade that drops the template fails the value tests (Damage 10, not 40); a plain spawn without SetActorLocation fails CopiesStandWhereTheyAreLaid, and so did SpawnActorAbsolute (A1094); a charm-less clone fails only CloneOwnsItsCharm. The first run of CopiesStandWhereTheyAreLaid failed the reference, which is how A1094 was found. Reverse cost: none.

### A1097 2026-10-27 Glossary and card changes for Prototype (author-patterns, M4.P2.12)
Added `spawn-template` (framework; aliases FActorSpawnParameters::Template, template actor, spawn template) and `duplicate-object` (object model; aliases DuplicateObject<T>() and StaticDuplicateObject), both first taught in `p12-copy-the-tuned-one`, and the alias `GetMutableDefault` on `cdo`. The Prototype card in `patterns.json` was audited item by item. Rewritten: `intent`, `problem`, `unrealForm`, and `whenNot` (the old problem was a fast ghost, a slow armored ghost, and a ghost with a key, which is the chapter's spawner example), `prototype-recall-2` and `-3` (reworded), and both old scenarios (an elite guard, and thirty enemy kinds). Added `prototype-recall-4` and `-5` and `prototype-scenario-3` (trial rows) and `-4` (a multiple-choice when-not: copies are snapshots). Use cases: `p12-blade-copies` and `p12-trial-rows`. No card item names the grandchild chain, which is the deliberate failure (A1093). Reverse cost: low.

### A1096 2026-10-27 The refactor is checked by reflection, and the starter has drifted (author-patterns, M4.P2.12)
`p12-kit-refactor` starts with a hand-written `Clone` that copies the label, health, stamina, charm strength, and favor but not `LuckBonus`, the field "added after the first version". `CloneKeepsEveryValue` walks the kit's non-object properties with `TFieldIterator` and fails on the starter naming `LuckBonus`; `CloneOwnsItsCharm` and `CloneSharesTheFavor` pass before and after. The reference is one `DuplicateObject<UDojoP12Kit>(this, GetOuter())`, which also keeps the owned charm separate and the favor shared. Options considered: a test that fails only on a field it names by hand (rejected, it would miss the next field); a structural test counting lines (rejected, brittle). Reverse cost: low.

### A1095 2026-10-27 Trial rows use -1 as "not set", live in an array on an actor, and have no loop test (author-patterns, M4.P2.12)
Options for "this row does not set it": `TOptional` (not a `UPROPERTY`), one override flag per field (doubles the fields), or a negative sentinel. Chosen: the sentinel, since a blade count, a speed, a time limit, and a reward are never negative; zero stays a real value. The rows sit in a `TArray<FDojoP12TrialRow>` on `ADojoP12TrialBook`, not in a Data Table, so the tests need no asset; the Alternative names the Data Table form. Rows that name each other as parents are not tested and, after fix round 1, not stated before the failure either (saying a parent can have a parent points at the failing case); hint 3 and the reference carry the loop guard. A loop test would add a second failing test beside the deliberate one, and an unguarded learner loop would hang the test run. Reverse cost: low.

### A1094 2026-10-27 Laying a blade sets the copy's location and wakes it (author-patterns, M4.P2.12)
UE 5.8's `LevelActor.cpp` and `Actor.cpp` place a spawned actor's root at the transform given combined with the template root's own relative transform, and `bHidden` and `bActorEnableCollision` are plain saved properties that a template copy takes. So a template standing at (1000, 1000, 500) leaves copies off target, and a parked template makes a parked copy. Options for the place: `UWorld::SpawnActorAbsolute`, which the source says cancels the template root's place; a plain spawn followed by `SetActorLocation`. The engine run showed `SpawnActorAbsolute` still leaves a copy at the template's place when the spawn is at exactly (0, 0, 0), so the reference spawns plainly and calls `SetActorLocation(Where)` and `Wake()`; a plain spawn alone also fails on the first blade. Both are stated requirements in the worked quest's brief and rubric and covered by tests with the tuned blade away from the origin; neither is the world's deliberate failure. Reverse cost: low.

### A1093 2026-10-27 World 12's deliberate failure: a trial that borrows through a parent that itself borrows (author-patterns, M4.P2.12)
Options:
- Calling `GetMutableDefault` to tune a variant: taught openly in the first quest and the card.
- A template that is left visible: taught openly in the first quest.
- `Resolve` that reads the parent's row as written, one level up.

Chosen: the last. A natural first `Resolve` finds the row, then copies the parent's numbers into whatever the row left unset. That passes every one-level test, and fails `GrandchildBorrowsThroughItsParent`, where SwiftLong's blade count and reward are set two rows up, in Basic, while Swift leaves them unset. The book lists children before parents, so a precompute pass in array order also fails. The reference climbs the chain until every number is set. Nothing the learner sees before the failure says a parent may borrow from its own parent: the brief, rubric, front matter, card, and card items say only that an unset number comes from the parent (fix round 1), and the brief opens on Swift, not SwiftLong. Engine check: see A1098. Reverse cost: low.

Not used in briefs, cards, choices, distractors, or the card's items: a spawner class per monster kind, ghosts, demons, a goblin and its archer, or the chapter's remarks on prototype-based languages. The originality check ran against the research's Prototype section (6 and 5 words, no hits in quests, solutions comments, glossary, or the card); the source PDF is gone, so a direct check against the book is pending. No boss and no breakdown. Reverse cost: low.

### A1262 2026-10-27 The data wait limit rises from 15 s to 45 s (M5.1l, m5-1 lead)
W12 gave every save, rules, and content read a 15 s wait limit. On the Windows CI run 40467374564 one app launch had its content reads answered after 16.2 to 16.7 s, so a learner on a slow first launch would see "taking too long" for a moment before the data replaced it. Options: keep 15 s; 30 s; 45 s; per-key limits with a long one only for content. Chosen: 45 s for every save, rules, and content read (the 120 s machine-probe limit is unchanged). A read main truly never answers still ends in the error view with Try again, 30 s later than before, and the Studio no-answer test now takes about 47 s. One constant, `DATA_QUERY_WAIT_LIMIT_MS`; a per-key split can come later if 45 s feels long for a save read. Reverse cost: change the constant and the e2e timeouts.

### A1261 2026-10-27 The content loader is not rewritten and the thread pool is not resized (M5.1l, m5-1 lead)
Asked to find why content loads slowly on a cold Windows disk. Measured on the Windows desktop with the real 150-quest content: 5 ms to read every quest file, 45 ms to parse them, 44 ms for the whole load, 77 to 109 ms for the first load of a freshly copied folder (the closest cold case available), and no change with `UV_THREADPOOL_SIZE` 4 versus 16. The loader already reads in parallel and does one read per file. In the CI log the same content loaded fast in 33 other launches inside tests of 2 to 5 s; only the launch of one test stalled, for about 16 s. That rules out the loader's own CPU work on this desktop and shows the stall is intermittent; it does not prove whether the runner's per-file opens (a virus scanner) or something holding the main process caused it. The cause is not proven. Options: bundle all content into one file at build time (fewer opens, but dev and e2e would still read the folder, and a stale bundle is a new risk); answer the glossary before the quests (does not help a stall that blocks everything); raise `UV_THREADPOOL_SIZE` (no measured gain); change nothing and make the next stall name itself. Chosen: the last, plus A1262. If the new warning (A1260) shows slow reads per file, the single-file bundle is the next step and the reason will be in hand. Reverse cost: none, nothing was changed.

### A1260 2026-10-27 A content load of 2 s or more warns with where the time went (M5.1l, m5-1 lead)
The CI log could not say whether the 16 s was the disk or something blocking the main process, because the e2e harness prints only main's `[ipc]` lines to the job log (the rest goes into the report attached to a failing test). Chosen: `loadContentWithDiagnostics` wraps the real load; at 2 s or more it logs one `console.warn` line with the quest file count, the summed read time and milliseconds per file, the parse time, the longest stretch the main process was blocked (a 10 ms timer whose late ticks are measured from the moment the load starts, read after one extra tick so a last stall is counted), and one sentence on what the figures point at: a blocked stretch of half the load or more means the reads were held up by something else in main, a shorter one points at the disk or a virus scanner. The read figure counts any blocked time against every read in flight, so it is read together with the stall. `e2e/support/launchDojo.ts` now echoes lines starting `[content] slow load` to the runner console like it does `[ipc]` lines, so a passing run still leaves the line in the job log. The loader reports its timings through an optional `onTimings`. Cost on a normal load: one timer tick per 10 ms and two clock reads per quest file. Reverse cost: remove the wrapper in `createContentCatalog.ts` and the echo.

### A1219 2026-10-27 The load budget test may retry twice, because a busy machine can lose a timing run (M5.1 lead)
In three full `npm test` runs on the busy Windows desktop (other sessions running tests at the same time) the 300-quest budget test failed once, then passed twice, and passed three times alone. Options: leave it (a red CI run now and then); raise the 300 ms budget; let this one test retry up to twice. Chosen: retry twice, keeping 300 ms as the contract. A real regression is slower on every attempt and still fails. Reverse cost: delete the `retry` option. If CI still goes red, log it in `docs/problems.md` and consider gating on the median of five.

### A1207 2026-10-27 The folder pick is checked against the engine saved in Settings; /Volumes is not warned; a deleted sandbox is not pointed at Rebuild (M5.1e, builder)
Three small choices. (1) At pick time the engine is already saved in Settings (the engine step comes first), so `folderBlockers` uses `savedEnginePath` from `createUnrealServices`; `create` uses the engine it is given, which wins. Options: send the engine path with the pick request (a new IPC argument, an app-shell change); check only in `create`. Chosen: the saved engine, so the pick answer needs a one-line change and `create` is the final guard. (2) On a Mac `/Volumes/...` holds external drives as well as network shares and there is no way to tell them apart without asking the disk, so it is not warned about; only Windows UNC paths are. (3) Nothing in the app can set up a deleted sandbox again: Settings' Rebuild sandbox needs an existing `Dojo.uproject`, and first-run is one-time. So the sandbox-gone message says only "put the folder back where it was (or reconnect its drive)", and the older `sandboxNotReady` message no longer points at Settings either. The missing action is logged in `docs/problems.md` and planned as M5.1k. The engine and sandbox messages are worded for every reason a read can fail, not only a move. Reverse cost: edit one message, or add the checks.

### A1206 2026-10-27 Folder comparison ignores letter case and slash style on Windows only; an engine inside the picked folder is ignored (M5.1e, builder)
`C:\Program Files\Epic Games\UE_5.8` and `c:/program files/epic games/ue_5.8/` are the same folder on Windows, so the blocker compares through `PlatformPaths.pathKey` (lower case, separators and trailing slash normalised) and then part by part, so `UE_5.8.1` is not taken for being inside `UE_5.8`. A Mac keeps exact case (APFS can be either, and an exact match is always safe). The engine root inside the chosen folder is not a blocker: the sandbox is created in a new subfolder and never touches the engine. Options: block that too; compare as plain text prefixes. Chosen as stated, to block only what can break. Reverse cost: one function.

### A1205 2026-10-27 A failed preflight is a rejected call, not a RunResult (M5.1e, builder)
A moved engine or deleted sandbox means no run happened, so there is no result to describe. Options: resolve a `RunResult` with `unrelatedFailure` set; reject with the `AppError`. Chosen: reject, through a new `RunPreflight` used first by `runQuest`, `checkSnippetSyntax` and `rebuildSandbox`. The renderer already shows a rejected run, Submit already stops on a rejected `runQuest` without reviewing or recording (A1208), and `checkSnippetSyntax` and `rebuildSandbox` have no result shape for it. A locator failure that is not a version problem, even a raw `ENOENT`, becomes `engine-not-found` with the raw text kept only as `detail`; a too-old engine keeps `engine-version-unsupported`. Reverse cost: wrap the rejection into a result in `QuestRunService.runQuest`.

### A1204 2026-10-27 Warn about five risky places, block only inside the engine folder (M5.1e, builder)
A bad sandbox location should be explained before a build fails, but a warning that blocks a folder that works would only annoy. Options: block everything risky; warn about everything. Chosen: warn (the folder can still be used) about a Windows path over 100 characters (as before), non-ASCII letters (some build tools mishandle them), cloud-synced folders (OneDrive, Dropbox, Google Drive, iCloud Drive; sync can lock files mid-build), network paths (slow, drops mid-build) and Program Files (Windows protects it, so builds can fail with permission errors). Block only a folder inside the engine folder: an engine update, repair or uninstall can wipe it, and the build would mix sandbox files with engine files. Each is one plain sentence in `SandboxFolderRules`. Reverse cost: one entry per rule.


### A1217 2026-10-27 A failure from the main process wins, a blocked folder blocks the step, and the save notice is dismissed once per run (M5.1i, builder)
The first-run and Settings screens used to guess a build failure's cause from the compiler output. Options: keep guessing; use `SandboxSetupResult.failure` when it is there and guess only when it is absent; drop the guess. Chosen: the second, through `describeSandboxFailure` in `lib/sandboxFailure.ts` (the compiler output stays behind its toggle). A folder with `blockers` shows each reason and turns "Use this folder" off, since the main process says it cannot work; warnings only inform. The save notice (`saveNotice`) shows on Title and in the Settings engine row with a Dismiss button; dismissing is remembered by code and message in a small module-level store outside the query cache (`lib/dismissedNotices.ts`), because main sends the notice on every read for the whole session and the cache's copy is replaced on every focus, mount and refresh. So it goes from both screens, survives navigation and refocus, and comes back only after the window reloads. Reverse cost: delete the helper and show `failure` nowhere.

### A1216 2026-10-27 One error boundary per screen, keyed by route, with Reload and Go to Studio (M5.1i, builder)
A thrown render used to blank the whole window. Options: one boundary around the whole app; one around each screen inside the frame; a boundary that also logs to a file. Chosen: `components/ScreenErrorBoundary.tsx` inside `ScreenOutlet`, with `key={routeId}`, so the navigation rail stays usable and moving to another screen clears the error. It says "Something went wrong on this screen", offers Reload, Go to Studio, and a details toggle with the error message; the component stack goes to the console. Reload first forgets the cached answers no mounted screen reads (`QueryCache.forgetUnobserved`) and refreshes the ones that are read, so bad cached data that broke the screen cannot break it again, then clears the error and moves keyboard focus to the main region (which has `tabIndex` -1). Go to Studio only navigates (the route key remounts the boundary, so the crashed screen is not drawn a second time) and clears the error itself only when the Studio is the screen that broke. No file logging (the main process owns logs). To test it, the fake scenario `screen-crash` returns pattern cards with a null entry, which makes every screen that reads the cards throw while rendering (Character, Drill, Loot, concept quests; Title, Studio and Settings do not read them); the scenario exists only in the fake, which a packaged build never loads (A251), and no screen has a crash switch of its own. Reverse cost: remove the boundary element.

### A1215 2026-10-27 The engine chip says Missing, a Build tools chip appears once a check ran, and the problems are written out (M5.1i, builder)
Title showed "Not set" for an engine that was set but gone. Options: keep "Not set"; say "Missing" when `engineProblem` is set and keep "Not set" for no engine; add a tooltip only; also write the problems out under the chips. Chosen: "Missing" (red) with the message and action in the tooltip, plus an `EnvironmentProblems` panel under the chips showing the engine problem, every toolchain problem and the save notice through `AppErrorView`, because a tooltip alone is easy to miss and cannot be reached by keyboard. The new "Build tools" chip reads `toolchain`: hidden when it is null (no check ran, so nothing to say), OK when fine, Blocked when a problem has `blocksBuild`, Warning for smaller ones. The same wording lives in `lib/environmentChipStates.ts` and is reused by the Settings "Build tools" row. First-run's engine step also shows `engineProblem`. The engine chip says "Too old" instead of "Missing" for the code `engine-version-unsupported`. Main keeps the toolchain answer for the session (A1210) and the only refresh is `checkToolchain`, which a returning learner could not reach from Title or Settings; so every toolchain problem there (`components/ToolchainProblemList.tsx`) carries a "Check again" button that runs `checkToolchain` and refreshes the environment. Reverse cost: remove the chip and the panel.
### A1214 2026-10-27 The fake's outside failure is a marker comment line, and a rejected Submit clears the other call's error (M5.1h, builder)
The screen needed a way to show an outside failure without a real engine. Options: a special quest id; a settings switch; a marker line in the learner's file. Chosen: a `// dojo:fake-outside-failure` line in any file (`FakeOutsideFailure.ts`), so a test or a curious learner triggers it from the editor on the fake data source (the real app ignores it), and it works on every quest. The fake's Run then answers with `unrelatedFailure` (code `build-environment-failed`, Live Coding active, no tests) and its Submit throws that same error before anything is reviewed or recorded, as the real submit does (A1208). Related screen rule: `useQuestRun` gained `clearError`, and a new Run or Submit clears the other call's error, so a rejected Submit's message does not stay beside a newer result. When a rejected Submit shows the same failure a Run result also carries, only the Submit's box shows. Reverse cost: delete one fake file and one method.

### A1213 2026-10-27 The headline "Not your code" and which errors get it (M5.1h, builder)
The learner must see at once that a failure is not their fault. Options: reword each message; a headline prop on `AppErrorView`; a separate view for the Build screen. Chosen: an optional `headline` prop on the shared `AppErrorView` (a bold line above the message, used here as "Not your code"), always set when a Run result carries `unrelatedFailure`, and set on a rejected Run or Submit only when the code is an environment code (`build-environment-failed`, `toolchain-missing`, `engine-not-found`, `engine-version-unsupported`, `sandbox-not-ready`, `sandbox-path-too-long`, `sandbox-path-unsupported`, `sandbox-copy-failed`, `editor-launch-failed`, listed in `outsideFailureCodes.ts`). Other rejections (invalid payload, unexpected) get the plain compact error with no claim about whose fault it is. The label beside a result says "could not build (not your code)" instead of "did not compile". The error box offers Run again or Submit again. Reverse cost: one prop and one small file.

### A1212 2026-10-27 A failed start shows a plain dialog and quits; a crashed window reloads once (M5.1g, builder)
Before, a failed `start()` only wrote to the log and left the app running with no window. Options: leave it; show the error with its stack; show a plain dialog, log the detail, and quit. For a crashed page process (`render-process-gone`): leave it; reload every time; reload once. Chosen: the plain dialog (title "Unreal Dojo could not start", no stack, no error text) then quit, even if the dialog itself fails; and one reload per window, with later crashes only logged, so a page that crashes on load never loops. A clean exit is ignored, and a destroyed window is left alone. In a hidden e2e run the failed-start box is skipped (log only) so no modal native window appears (A77, A970). Reverse cost: delete one call in `index.ts` each.

### A1211 2026-10-27 Quit waits up to 3 s for pending saves (M5.1g, builder)
The last change should reach the disk before the app closes, but a stuck disk must not freeze the quit. Options: no wait; wait without a limit; wait up to 3 s through the bounded `whenIdle` (A1195). Chosen: 3 s (`QUIT_SAVE_WAIT_MS`), held once in `src/main/lifecycle/holdQuitForSaves.ts`, separate from the Unreal quit sequence so each does one job. The quit is always held for the length of one `whenIdle` call, which answers at once when nothing is pending. The second `app.quit()` is deferred to a later turn (`setImmediate`, injectable) so it never runs inside the first quit's own event; on macOS that could leave the window closed and the app in the Dock. That case (Cmd+Q with nothing pending) is unverified on macOS (`docs/problems.md`). The real limit: saves queued before quit are awaited for up to 3 s. A change made after that first wait, while the Unreal stop (up to 5 s for runs plus 15 s for the editor) is still running with the window open, is lost, and the file stays intact because the write is atomic. Accepted, because the window is closing and the learner just pressed quit; a second hold was considered and left out to keep this small. Closed 2026-11-03 by A1460: every quit attempt now waits for saves. Reverse cost: change the constant or remove one call in `index.ts`.

### A1210 2026-10-27 The toolchain check is cached for the session (M5.1g, builder)
Title refetches the environment status on every window focus, and the check runs vswhere (Windows) or xcode-select (macOS). Options: check on every read; check once per session and refresh on request; check on a timer. Chosen: once per session; `index.ts` starts it in the background at startup (`void refreshToolchain()`), so it is usually done before the Title first asks. `refreshToolchain()` runs it again and replaces the kept answer, and the existing `checkToolchain` IPC call (the "Check again" button) goes through it. A build failure outside the learner's code should call the same refresh (M5.1d's side). A read that comes while the check is still running waits for it (bounded by the checker's own timeout), and concurrent reads share one check. A check that throws, even before it returns a promise, or cannot run caches null for the session until a refresh. Reverse cost: drop the cache field.

### A1209 2026-10-27 The detail tail is capped at 40 lines and comes from the build output only (M5.1f, builder)
A rejected Submit shows the learner why the run failed, so the end of the build output goes into `detail`. Options: the whole output; the last 40 lines; the last 40 lines plus the test messages. Chosen: the last 40 lines of `compilerOutput`, after the failure's own detail and a blank line (named constant `UNRELATED_FAILURE_DETAIL_LINES`). The cause of a crash is at the end of the log, a full build log can be megabytes, and test messages belong to the learner's code, not to a failure outside it. When the failure has no detail and the output is empty, `detail` stays undefined. Reverse cost: change one constant.

### A1208 2026-10-27 A Submit that failed outside the quest rejects with the AppError and records nothing (M5.1f, builder)
A UBT crash, timeout, missing engine, Live Coding being active, a full disk or a broken earlier quest is not the learner's code, so it must not break the spree or count toward the two-failed-submit offers. Options: record it as today; reject and record nothing; resolve with `recorded: false`. Chosen: reject with the run's `unrelatedFailure` after the cancel check, with no review and no `recordCodeAttempt` call. It matches the cancel rule, needs no `SubmitOutcome` change, and the renderer already has an error path. A cancel still wins. A normal failed compile or failed test is recorded as before. `progressSchema.ts` drops unknown keys on load, which is harmless because such attempts are never recorded. Reverse cost: record the attempt again by removing one call in `QuestSubmitService.submit`.

### A1198 2026-10-27 The load log line and the injectable clock (M5.1c, builder)
The catalog needs to say how long a load took, in the app and in the test. Options: log from `startLoading` in `index.ts`; log inside `ContentCatalog.loadAndIndex`; time only in the test. Chosen: one `console.info` line, `[content] loaded N quests in X ms`, written by `loadAndIndex` after the lookups are built, with an optional `now` in `ContentCatalogDependencies` (default `performance.now`). Logging inside the catalog covers every start path (startup, first request, a retry after a failure) and the time includes the folder check, the loader, and indexing. The fake clock lets a test check the line without waiting. Reverse cost: delete one line and one optional field.

### A1197 2026-10-27 The large fixture clones real quests instead of synthetic ones (M5.1c, builder)
The older 300-quest check (`writeLargePack`) cycles through 8 format examples, which are small and have no prerequisites. Options: reuse it; generate synthetic quests; clone the real pack quests. Chosen: `tests/support/largeContentFixture.ts` reads every real quest file in order and writes `LARGE_QUEST_COUNT` (300) retagged clones, running through the real quests again and again. Each pass over the real quests gets its own id prefix, so ids are unique; prerequisites and the `pieces:` ids of bosses are rewritten to the clone of the same pass, and the build throws if any would dangle. Worlds are numbered across all clones, 25 quests each, so there are always `ceil(300 / 25)` = 12 whatever the real quest count is, and each world sits in the pack of the pass its first quest belongs to. The real glossary and patterns are copied; each glossary `firstQuestId` is pointed at the first pass's clone, and that pointer joins the dangling check. Each pattern card's `useCaseQuestIds` keep the original ids on purpose: only the verifier reads them, not the load path. Real quests carry the real sizes (starter files, tests, reference plans), the real prerequisite chains, and the real term counts, so the parse, the alias table, the drill bank, and the unlock graph all do real work. A synthetic quest would understate every one of those. Cost: the clones follow the real quest shapes, so the timing drifts as the real content changes, which is the point. If a clone cannot satisfy a pointer (a quest id the real content does not have), the fixture fails loudly instead of loading with problems.

### A1196 2026-10-27 What "content load" measures, and the budget (M5.1c, builder)
"Content load under 300 ms for 300 quests" needs one definition. Options: only `ContentLoader.load`; the catalog until `listPacks`; the whole first-request path. Chosen: from `startLoading()` on a fresh `ContentCatalog` (made by `createContentCatalog`, the app's own wiring) until `listPacks()`, `getGlossaryAliases()`, and `ProgressContentService.getQuestAvailability()` (a fresh in-memory save) have all answered. That last call builds the drill bank from every world, so it is the real cost of the World Map's first paint. Input: 300 retagged real quests with the real glossary and patterns on real disk (A1197). Gate: the median of three fresh loads is under 300 ms (`LOAD_BUDGET_MILLISECONDS` in `tests/contentLoadBudget.test.ts`). The first (cold) run is printed with the others and is not hidden by a warm-up, but it is not gated because other builders share the machine and one cold run can spike. Reverse cost: change the constant or the run count.

### A1195 2026-10-27 `whenIdle` is bounded and never rejects (M5.1b, builder)
The app should let queued saves finish before it quits, but a stuck disk must not freeze the quit. Options: `whenIdle()` with no limit; `whenIdle(timeoutMs)` that rejects on timeout; `whenIdle(timeoutMs)` that always resolves, with true when the queue drained and false when the time ran out. Chosen: the third. A failed save is already its own caller's error (the queue keeps going after one), so the wait does not repeat it, and a caller needs no try/catch on the way out. Reverse cost: one method on `ProgressService`.

### A1194 2026-10-27 The kill test runs a TypeScript child through tsx so it tests the real code (M5.1b, builder)
A save that survives a kill has to be shown with a real killed process, not only the in-memory crash points. Options: compile the app files first and run the output; run a small hand-written JavaScript copy of the write steps; run `tests/progress/support/killedSaveWriter.ts` with `node --import tsx`, importing only `AtomicJsonFile` and `NodeFileSystem` by relative path. Chosen: the third. It needs no build step, `tsx` is already a devDependency, and the child runs the very same classes the app does, so a change to the write steps changes what the test proves. The parent kills it with SIGKILL (TerminateProcess on Windows) at 20 seeded random moments (seed logged, `DOJO_KILL_SEED` replays it). Reverse cost: swap the spawn line.

### A1193 2026-10-27 The backup is written to `.bak.tmp` then renamed; a complete `.tmp` is ignored on load (M5.1b, builder)
Copying straight onto `progress.json.bak` could leave a torn backup when the process is killed mid-copy, which would remove the fallback exactly when the main file is also bad. Options: copy in place (the old way); copy to `progress.json.bak.tmp` and rename it over `.bak`; also promote a complete `.tmp` on load. Chosen: the second, and no promotion. A leftover `.tmp` or `.bak.tmp` is never read; the next save overwrites it. A complete `.tmp` belongs to a call that never answered, so loading the old file is consistent with what the app told the learner. Reverse cost: one rename in `AtomicJsonFile.writeNow`.

### A1203 2026-10-27 A failed sandbox build always gets an explanation; a failed quest run only gets one with outside evidence (M5.1d, builder)
A sandbox build runs no quest, so any failure of it is about the setup or about quest code already in the sandbox. Options: the same conservative default as runs (no `failure` without evidence); always answer. Chosen: always answer. After the known signatures and the listed pipeline errors, `SandboxSetupResult.failure` names quests whose code in the sandbox has errors (a rebuild keeps them), then support code, then a blocking toolchain problem (only when no compiler or linker line exists), and last a generic `build-environment-failed` that says the setup on this computer is the cause and quotes the first error line.

A quest run is stricter (review fix round 1). Pipeline texts count as outside only when they cannot be the learner's doing: a program that could not start, none of the quest's tests running, and, before the code compiled, a build timeout or a build tool that crashed while starting. After a compile, a test timeout or a missing or unreadable test report may be the learner's crash or endless loop, so those, and any pipeline text nobody listed, stay with the quest (`RunResult.error` still shows them). Reverse cost: add the text to `outsidePipelineFailure`.

### A1202 2026-10-27 Signature strings confirmed on Windows UE 5.8.3, not run on macOS (M5.1d, builder)
The build output strings in `buildFailureSignatures.ts` come from three places. Confirmed by reading the UE 5.8.3 UnrealBuildTool source on Windows (HotReload.cs, MicrosoftPlatformSDK.cs, AppleToolChainSettings.cs): "Unable to build while Live Coding is active", "Unable to find valid ... C++ toolchain", "UnrealBuildTool requires at minimum the MSVC", "Unable to find requested Windows SDK", "Your Mac is set to use CommandLineTools", "Selected Xcode ... doesn't exist", "requires Xcode ... or newer". Taken from the compilers and operating systems (not reproduced from a real log here): LNK1180, LNK1104, C1081, "No space left on device", "being used by another process". No failing build was provoked on either machine. The Xcode and clang shapes were not run on macOS and are logged as unverified in `docs/problems.md`. Options: provoke each failure on the real engine (a full disk, a locked DLL); read the source and write fixtures in the real tool shape. Chosen: the second, because provoking a full disk or a locked DLL is unsafe on the learner's machine and the engine lock cost is high. Reverse cost: replace a fixture with a captured log.

### A1201 2026-10-27 The toolchain check runs only for failures nothing else could place (M5.1d, builder)
`checkToolchain` starts `vswhere` or `xcodebuild`, which takes a moment. Options: check on every failed run; check only when rules 1 to 5 of the explainer found no cause. Chosen: only then, and (review fix round 1) only when the output has no compiler or linker error line, because such a line proves the toolchain ran (Apple's linker output such as `Undefined symbols for architecture arm64:` counts too). A pass, a cancel, a run that compiled and only failed its tests, any line naming the learner's quest, and any output with a compile error never reach it, so an ordinary red test costs nothing extra and a toolchain problem cannot be blamed for the learner's typo. Reverse cost: move the call to the top of `explainRun`.

### A1200 2026-10-27 Failures are explained in main; the renderer's string guessing is only a fallback (M5.1d, builder)
Options: keep `lib/sandboxFailure.ts` guessing from the compiler text in the renderer; classify in main where the paths, the prerequisite sources and the toolchain check are known. Chosen: main. Only main knows which earlier quests were filled from a reference and can run the toolchain check, and strings are matched once, in data (`buildFailureSignatures.ts`), with fixtures. The renderer shows `unrelatedFailure` and `failure` when present and falls back to `lib/sandboxFailure.ts` when they are absent (an older main, or no explainer). Reverse cost: delete the explainer wiring; nothing else depends on it.

### A1199 2026-10-27 Blame the quest unless there is outside evidence (M5.1d, builder)
When a run fails and nothing points outside the learner's code, the learner sees the ordinary compile or test failure. Options: tell the learner "this may not be your code" whenever the cause is unclear; say nothing unless there is evidence. Chosen: say nothing. Evidence means a known outside line, a pipeline error, errors only in an earlier quest or support code, or a blocking toolchain problem. A wrong "it is not your code" sends a learner hunting for a fault they cannot fix and hides their real bug, which is worse than a missing hint. Unplaced linker lines, mixed earlier-quest and support errors, and a healthy toolchain all stay with the quest. Reverse cost: a one-line change in `explainRun` returning a generic failure.

Review fix round 1 tightened it: any line of the output that names the quest's folder or test file (a note, an include chain, a "requested here") keeps the blame with the quest, so an engine error the learner caused is never refunded; a signature phrase on a line that names a quest file is the learner's own text and is ignored; quest paths are anchored on `Source/Dojo/`.

Review fix round 2: an error located in an engine header counts as outside only when it says a file is missing (`Cannot open include file`, C1083, `file not found`, `No such file or directory`); any other engine-header error, which MSVC prints without an include chain, stays with the quest. An error only in a quest that is not an earlier quest (a later or dependent one) stays with the quest too. Signatures for failures that stop a build before compiling (Live Coding, Visual Studio or SDK, Xcode) are ignored when the output names the quest, and clang code excerpt lines never match a signature.

Review fix round 3: once the code compiled, signatures are matched only against the pipeline error, never against the kept warning lines, so a coincident warning cannot refund a test-time crash or loop. A support header (`Support/*.h`) is treated like an engine header (no include chain, so the learner's header may be the cause); only `Support/*.cpp` and template files count as outside. "Only" in rules 4 and 5 is strict: an unknown-located error line blocks them. In a sandbox build, any line naming a quest gets the open-or-reset message, and an engine error that is not a missing file gets the generic message.

### A1192 2026-10-27 The save notice rides on the environment status, not a new IPC method (M5.1a, m5-1 lead)
A restored-from-backup or refused save has to reach the learner. M5.1g: the reader builds the notice once and returns it on every read of the session (not only the first), so a refusal is never lost and a second window or refetch still sees it; the renderer hides it after the learner dismisses it, with a flag kept outside the shared query copy (M5.1i). Options: a new IPC method and channel; an optional `saveNotice` field on `EnvironmentStatus`, which the Title screen already reads. Chosen: the field. No new channel, the fake keeps compiling, and the Title and Settings screens show it through the existing `AppErrorView`. Reverse cost: move the field to its own method.

### A1191 2026-10-27 New friendly-failure fields are optional (M5.1a, m5-1 lead)
`RunResult.unrelatedFailure`, `SandboxSetupResult.failure`, `SandboxFolderChoice.blockers` and `EnvironmentStatus.engineProblem`, `toolchain` and `saveNotice` are all optional. Options: required fields (every producer and the fake change at once); optional fields (each system fills its own in its own task). Chosen: optional, so the renderer fake in `FakeDojoApi.ts` and every existing producer keep compiling, and a missing field means "nothing to say". ### A1270 2026-10-27 An editor that does not answer in an engine integration test leaves its logs behind (W13, builder-unreal)
The integration suite twice timed out in `waitForReopenedEditor` (the editor the app reopens did not answer MCP within 3 min on a loaded machine; on rerun it answered in 8 to 13 s), and the cause stayed unknown because the suite deletes its temp sandbox and the editor's log with it. Options: (1) keep every sandbox: rejected, each is gigabytes; (2) raise the timeout: rejected, it hides the question and weakens the test; (3) on a timeout only, copy the sandbox's `Saved/Logs` out before the sandbox is removed. Chosen: (3). `tests/support/keepEditorTimeoutEvidence.ts` copies the logs to `dojo-reopen-timeout-<label>-<timestamp>` under the OS temp folder and returns a sentence with that path, whether the editor session was running, and which processes still mention the sandbox. `fastLoop.test.ts` (editor open and reopen waits) and `fastLoopWiring.test.ts` (reopen wait) put that sentence in the assertion message; the assertions and the 3 min limits are unchanged, and the helper never throws, so it cannot hide the timeout. A unit test covers the copy without an engine. Reverse cost: none; the kept folders are small and Mohammad can delete them.

### A1250 2026-10-27 A query main does not answer ends in the AppError view after a wait limit (W12, builder-ui-core)
Twice on windows-latest push runs (40452256211, 40458755679) the Studio against the real bridge stayed on its loading block for 5 s and then 20 s, with no error and no data; the cause was not found (A1253). Nothing in the renderer bounded the wait, so a learner on a machine where one answer never comes would see an endless loading block. Options: (1) a timeout on every call in the preload: rejected, runs, builds, and reviews take minutes; (2) a timeout per handler in main: does not cover an answer lost between main and the window; (3) a wait limit per query key in the renderer's query cache. Chosen: (3). `data/queryWaitLimits.ts` gives 15 s to save, rules, and content reads (each answers in well under a second here, 2 to 4 s on CI) and 120 s to the machine probes (`engines`, `toolchain`, `environment`, `reviewProviders`), whose commands carry their own 15 to 20 s limits in main. When the limit passes with no answer, the key stops loading with the new renderer-made code `no-answer` ("Unreal Dojo is taking too long to load this.", Try again, the key and the wait in the details), and the fetch resolves so an action that waits for its refresh is not stuck. An answer that arrives later still replaces the error. Every screen that reads through `useDojoQuery` gets this, not only the Studio. The e2e test that checks it makes main's first `getDailyQuest` answer never come with `e2e/support/ipcStubs.ts`, through Electron's internal `ipcMain._invokeHandlers` table; it fails loudly if Electron moves that table. Reverse cost: low.

### A1251 2026-10-27 Main names any IPC call still running after 10 s (W12, builder-shell)
The CI artifacts could not say which call the Studio was waiting on. Chosen: the IPC wrapper logs `[ipc] <method> has not answered after 10 s` once, and `[ipc] <method> answered after <n> s` when a slow call ends. Runs, builds, and reviews are slow by design and are logged the same way rather than kept in a list of exceptions that would drift. The timer is unref'd, so it never keeps main alive. Reverse cost: none.

### A1252 2026-10-27 A failing e2e test attaches main's console (W12, builder-shell)
The harness never kept main's output, so a red CI run showed the window but not what main was doing. Chosen: `launchDojo` collects main's stdout and stderr with arrival times from when the window opens (startup output before that is not kept), and the `dojo` fixture attaches it as `main-process.log` to every failing test; the HTML report keeps it. Playwright's list reporter prints only the first 300 characters of a text attachment, so the job log could cut the line that matters: `launchDojo` also prints every main line containing `[ipc]` to the runner's console as it arrives, prefixed `[dojo main]`, so slow-call lines are in the CI job log on passing runs too (review round 1). Passing tests attach nothing. Touches the QA harness (`e2e/support/`, owner qa) as a small cross-owner change for W12. Reverse cost: none.

### A1253 2026-10-27 The Studio CI hang: cause not found, the test is unchanged (W12, builder-ui-core)
Not reproduced on the Windows desktop: the test alone 30 times, the whole screens file in order three times (36 passed), and 15 times while two processes kept the disk busy with flushed writes; it takes 3 to 6 s each time. Ruled out: the renderer cache (a key stays loading only while its promise has not settled); every main call the Studio waits on (`getProgress`, `getRules`, `getDrillSummary` answer at once; `listPacks`, `getQuestAvailability`, `getDailyQuest` do file reads and the progress queue, with no wait that cannot end in code; outside Electron all of them answer within 150 ms of start on a fresh save, content in 76 ms); the engine and Visual Studio probes (a fresh learner has no engine or sandbox path, so neither the Title nor the Studio reaches them, so hiding the engine changes nothing); network (main makes no requests); a long synchronous step in main (main starts no process synchronously; its synchronous file work is the small settings file and the Claude Code lookup, a few hundred existence checks; not proven, since Electron keeps no record of it); test order (the same order passed in three manual runs); recent merges (`80713d9..3e9f6ca` touched only the Unreal pipeline and the hover card). What the artifacts show: both failing runs were on a slow runner (the window took 4.6 s to open in 40452256211; in 40458755679 the next test, a fresh app, took 16 s against 4 to 6 s in passing runs, and its Studio loaded within about 8 s). Leading suspect, unproven: `getDailyQuest` on a fresh day writes the save, with a disk flush and rename retries, before it answers, and it is the only write on the Studio path, so a stalled disk would hold exactly one Studio key. Not changed: that write belongs to progress-and-rules and nothing shows it is the cause. The next CI failure will name the call (A1251, A1252); if it is `getDailyQuest`, progress-and-rules can answer from memory and save behind it. The test keeps its 20 s wait and its two outcomes, and since review round 1 it also fails when the error view it reached is `no-answer`, so a recurrence is not hidden by A1250's safety net: it fails and attaches main's console. Lead decision: the test does not fail on a `[ipc] ... has not answered` line, which a slow runner can print without a hang; those lines are printed in the job log instead (A1252). Reverse cost: none.

### A1240 2026-10-27 World 9 boss Blueprint plan uses the Add Component by Class pins; the parts expose their settings on spawn (M4.y follow-up; amends A837)
