### A1080 2026-10-26 A glossary hover card opens only on a real pointer move, not when content lands under a resting pointer (W9, builder-ui)
The real-engine learner flow failed because, after "Lock in my guess" was clicked, the reveal put the glossary term `VisibleAnywhere` under the resting pointer; the browser then sent the term a pointer enter, its hover card opened, and the card covered "Read the card". Options: (1) open only after the pointer really moves onto the term; (2) place the card so it avoids the continue button; (3) make the card ignore pointer events outside its own content. Chosen: (1). A window-level listener keeps the last two pointer positions, and the card's pointer open starts only when a pointer move event has a position different from the one before it; the events a browser sends for content moving under a still pointer repeat the position, so they are ignored. Each pointer entry allows one open: Escape, or any other close, keeps the card closed until the pointer leaves and enters again, so a small move inside the term does not reopen it or count another hover. Keyboard focus still opens the card as before, and touch is unchanged. Why not the others: (2) cannot know where every screen's primary button is, and the card would still pop up unasked; (3) leaves a card on screen that the learner did not ask for and still covers the button visually. The cost: a pointer that is already resting on a term when the page loads needs one small move to open its card. Test: `tests/renderer/pointerTrail.test.ts` and the W9 Playwright test, which fails on the old component and passes on the new one. Also found: a string passed to Playwright's `evaluate` is an expression, never called, so W5's `scrollIntoView` string in the World Map hover test did nothing; it now passes a function (`scrollIntoViewNow`).
### A1076 2026-10-26 The integration suite stops at once when another editor has Live Coding active (W8, builder-unreal)
The first full run on Windows had Mohammad's own editor (another project, same engine install) open with Live Coding. UnrealBuildTool then refused every sandbox build with "Unable to build while Live Coding is active", because it checks a mutex named after the engine's `UnrealEditor.exe`, which every project on that install shares. Fourteen tests failed with the same message, and one waited out its ten minute timeout. Options: pass `-NoHotReloadFromIDE` to UBT (skips the check; also changes what learners get); run anyway and let it fail; check first and stop. Chosen: every integration file checks for running `LiveCodingConsole.exe` processes before its first test and fails at once, naming them, with a plain "close that editor and run again". The quit test also fails at once when no editor is open instead of waiting for a quit that never comes. Why: it keeps the product's build arguments as they are. The same hazard reaches a learner who has another UE 5.8 project open with Live Coding while pressing Run in Unreal Dojo; that is logged in `docs/problems.md`. The Lead has W10 adding `-NoHotReloadFromIDE` to the UBT arguments, which is outside this task's files; once it merges the check passes and this guard only fires for a build that should not run anyway. The check is cheap and can stay. The second launcher in the second-editor test is also disposed in a `finally`, so a failed expectation cannot leave a real editor running (a slow editor start made it launch one in a failed run). Reverse cost: none.

### A1075 2026-10-26 Editor launches run one at a time (W8, builder-unreal)
A run that falls back to a full build closes the editor and reopens it in the background (A531). The fast loop test then opened an editor itself while that reopen was still starting, and two editors ran at once: the first launch's session was overwritten, so nothing could close it, and it was still running after the app's quit sequence. Options: tell callers to wait; serialise `EditorLauncher.launch`. Chosen: `launch` queues behind any launch in progress, so the second launch finds the first session and closes it. Why: the app can do the same by itself (Open editor pressed during the background reopen), and a leaked editor holds the sandbox's files and the MCP port. A unit test starts two launches at once. Reverse cost: low.

### A1074 2026-10-26 Tests and agents can never open a windowed editor (W8, builder-unreal)
`EditorService` opened a windowed editor on a clean Windows toolchain (A532 only goes windowless when the toolchain reports a problem), so `fastLoopWiring.test.ts` very likely showed a real editor window for about 30 seconds. Options: an environment variable the service reads; an injected option. Chosen: injected options, no environment switch in product code. `createUnrealServices` takes `forceWindowlessEditor` (EditorService's `alwaysWindowless`) and `decorateProcessLauncher`. `tests/support/createIntegrationServices.ts` sets the first and wraps the launcher in `GuardedProcessLauncher`, which records every launch and throws before starting a `UnrealEditor` that lacks `-NullRHI`. All five integration files use it; the fast loop files print every editor launch and assert each has `-NullRHI`. The learner's Open Editor button is untouched and stays windowed. Reverse cost: low.

### A1073 2026-10-26 The scratch buffer syntax check test is skipped on Windows (W8, builder-unreal)
The test in `runQuest.test.ts` that checks scratch buffers with the compiler failed on Windows with `not-implemented`, which is the known A402 gap (no Windows snippet checker). Chosen: `it.skipIf(process.platform === "win32")` with a comment naming A402; the checker itself is a separate task and stays open in `docs/problems.md`. Reverse cost: none. When the Windows checker lands, remove the skip.
2026-11-03: the skip is removed (W15, A1310).

### A1072 2026-10-26 The windowless editor can run Live Coding, but its console window appears, so the suite does not (W8, builder-unreal)
Looked at the engine source (`LiveCodingModule.cpp`) and ran it. `-NullRHI -Unattended` never starts Live Coding: it auto-starts only in an editor that is not unattended. Options: (1) `-LiveCoding` on the command line forces a start, and works, but starts `LiveCodingConsole.exe` as a visible 1200 by 640 window; (2) set `bEnabled=True` and `Startup=AutomaticButHidden` through `-ini:EditorPerProjectUserSettings:...` and drop `-Unattended`: the console starts hidden and `CompileLiveCoding` succeeds, the sample test runs in the editor and the fast loop passes for real; (3) leave Live Coding off. Option 2 works, but every `CompileLiveCoding` calls `EnableForSession(true)`, which unconditionally shows the console and two small notification windows, and no setting stops that. So a real Live Coding compile always puts a window on the desktop. Chosen: build option 2 behind `startLiveCoding` (EditorLauncher) and `startLiveCodingWhenWindowless` (EditorService, off by default), switched on in the suite only by `DOJO_TEST_LIVE_CODING=1` for someone at the machine; the suite's default is option 3 and expects the fallback path (A1071): the first run answers `Full build: Live Coding answered "unavailable".`, builds headless, and the app reopens the editor. `RunTests` over MCP is checked directly against the windowless editor. Live Coding is ready 2 to 6 seconds after the MCP server answers, so `waitUntilAnswering` also waits for the log line `Live coding ready` (up to 60 s) when it was asked for. The opt-in path passed the fast loop test on this machine (6 passed, 1 skipped, console windows seen) before the last refactor of how the flag reaches the launcher; it was not run again, so that is unverified in its final form. Without `-Unattended` a crash would open the crash reporter, which is another reason it is opt-in. Reverse cost: low.

### A1071 2026-10-26 "Live Coding is not enabled" is unavailable, not a compile failure (W8, builder-unreal)
In the windowless test editor `CompileLiveCoding` answers `Error: Live Coding is not enabled for this session. Enable it in Editor Preferences.` in 18 ms. `readLiveCodingAnswer` matched the generic `error` and returned `failed`, so the learner would have seen a compile failure with no compiler output. Chosen: wording that means Live Coding cannot run here (`not available`, `not enabled`, `not loaded`, `not started`, `cannot be enabled`, `unable to start`) is `unavailable` and is checked before the `error` match; `Result: CompileNotStarted` is `unavailable` too. The engine's real format is plain text starting `Result: <name>` (read from the toolset source), so that line is read before any wording in the compile output, which could otherwise turn a successful compile that prints "error" into a failure. `unavailable` makes the runner fall back to the headless loop with the reason. Unit tests cover each wording, the `Result:` line, and the runner falling back for both a text answer and a tool error. Reverse cost: low.

### A1070 2026-10-26 The "no process left behind" check works on Windows (W8, builder-unreal)
Three integration files each had their own `processesMentioning`, which ran `pgrep -fl`. There is no `pgrep` on Windows, so `.stdout` was undefined and five tests threw before checking anything; A182's process cleanup (`taskkill /T /F`) had never been checked on Windows. Options: PowerShell `Get-CimInstance Win32_Process`; `wmic` (removed from current Windows). Chosen: one shared `tests/support/processesMentioning.ts`. On Windows it runs PowerShell with the folder passed in an environment variable, so the query's own command line cannot match, and excludes its own process id; elsewhere it still uses `pgrep`. Its own unit test starts a node process with a unique folder on its command line, finds exactly that one, and finds nothing after it ends. No assertion was weakened. The first real use found the leaked editor behind A1075. Reverse cost: none.
### A1174 2026-10-27 Where a C4458 finding is reported (builder-content-engine, V1)
Options: attach each finding to the quest's `.md` file (section Files) with the solution path in the message; attach it to the solution file itself. Chosen: the quest file, so `--quest` and `--pack` scoping keeps working and the finding sits next to the other solution and starter problems; a solutions folder that matches no quest is reported under its own `solutions/<folder>/<file>` name. Both solutions and starters are errors, as asked. The message names the class, the function, the hidden member and the class that declares it, and for a parameter suggests the Unreal `In` prefix. Reverse cost: none.

### A1173 2026-10-27 The sandbox template gets no clang shadow flag (builder-content-engine, V1)
Options: add `-Wshadow-field` or another clang shadow warning to `resources/sandbox-template` (Build.cs or the targets); leave the template alone and rely on the verifier lint. Chosen: leave it alone. Why: (1) the template already sets `DefaultBuildSettings = BuildSettingsVersion.Latest`, and in the UE 5.8 UnrealBuildTool that makes `ShadowVariableWarningLevel` an error: MSVC gets `/we4458`, clang gets `-Wshadow` (CppCompileWarnings.cs, ApplyWarningsAttribute.cs), except that UBT switches it off for clang 17 up to 18.1.3, which is probably the range the Mac's Apple clang reports (not checked, no Mac here). So on the Mac the flag is either off by UBT or does not catch this case, and both known clashes got through. (2) `-Wshadow-field` is a different diagnostic (a derived field hiding a base field), so it would not catch a parameter hiding a member. (3) The template is also what learners build with, and an extra flag can turn engine-header or learner-code warnings into build errors for a result that cannot be tested here (no Mac with the engine on this machine). The verifier lint is the dependable guard because it runs without an engine on any machine. Still to try on the Mac when the engine is back: `-Wshadow` as an error from Build.cs (`CppCompileWarningSettings.ShadowVariableWarningLevel`) and see whether it flags a parameter named `Instigator`. Reverse cost: none.

### A1172 2026-10-27 A run that judges nothing fails (builder-content-engine, V1)
Options: only fail a scoped run; fail every run that judges nothing. Chosen: both layers fail. Content: `--pack` on a pack with no quests, or `--quest` / `--pack` naming nothing, is an error (`<scope> judged no quests`). Engine: an `--engine` run that finds no reference solution to build, scoped or not, is an error in `(engine)` naming the scope and the solutions folder; a quest already reported as unusable (a boss with no tests) is not reported twice. `--solutions` pointing at a missing folder or a file stops the run before anything is read, exit code 2, with a hint about a shell eating a backslash. Why not only scoped: an unscoped engine run over a wrong folder is the same silent pass. Reverse cost: none.

### A1171 2026-10-27 The C4458 lint is a text scanner, not a compiler (builder-content-engine, V1)
Options: run clang with an AST dump on each file (needs a Mac, headers, and the engine); a small C++ scanner in the verifier; a regex over `(Type Name)`. Chosen: the scanner in `tools/verifier/shadow/`. It blanks comments, strings, preprocessor lines and template arguments, finds class bodies and their data members (bit fields, `UPROPERTY` members, statics), finds function definitions (out of line `AFoo::Bar`, and inline in a class), and checks each parameter and local against the members of the class, its Dojo bases (looked up in the same quest first, then in any other), and the engine class it derives from. Limits, accepted: a constructor initializer list that uses braces (`ADojo() : Count{0} {}`) swallows the next member declaration; a member declared by a macro is invisible; `unsigned int X` is missed; the first declaration in a `switch` case is skipped; structured bindings (`auto [Owner, Tags]`) and operator overloads are not scanned; an overload set that mixes static and non-static versions is exempt as a whole (A1170, a static member is exempt), so a clash in the non-static overload is missed; parameters of functions that Unreal Header Tool generates (`BlueprintImplementableEvent` bodies) are never in the scanned text. Misses are possible, false alarms were not seen on the 150 quests, the 74 solution folders and the 8 examples. The engine proof on Windows stays the final judge. Reverse cost: none.

### A1170 2026-10-27 C4458 only fires for data members of non-static functions; the member list is curated from the 5.8 headers (builder-content-engine, V1)
Measured with `cl /W4` (Visual Studio 18) on 2026-10-27: C4458 fires for a parameter or local that hides a data member of the class or of any base, even a private base member (`AActor::Owner` is private); it does not fire for a name that matches a member function, for a lambda parameter or a local inside a lambda body (even with `[this]`), or for anything inside a static member function. The scanner follows exactly that. The first run on main flagged `Kind` in `ADojoP10Quiver::SpawnOfKind` (static), which the Windows proof had already built, and that is how the static rule was found. Member list: `tools/verifier/shadow/engineClassMembers.ts`, read from `Actor.h`, `Pawn.h`, `Character.h`, `ActorComponent.h`, `SceneComponent.h` and `UObjectBase.h` of UE 5.8.3 (data members only, nested structs left out, editor-only members kept because the sandbox builds the editor target). Left out on purpose because they are not members in 5.8: `World` (AActor has `GetWorld()`, no field) and `bActive` (UActorComponent has `bIsActive`). Engine bases outside the six curated classes map to the nearest one by naming (`AGameModeBase` to AActor, `UStaticMeshComponent` to USceneComponent, other `U...Component` to UActorComponent, other `U` classes to UObject). Refresh the list after an engine upgrade. Reverse cost: none.
### A1186 2026-10-26 The macOS guide gives the Sequoia "Open Anyway" route and `xattr -dr` next to right-click Open (M5.2, qa)
Mohammad's task named right-click Open or `xattr -d com.apple.quarantine`. On macOS 15 and later the right-click route often has no Open button on an unsigned app, and `-d` without `-r` only clears the top folder of an app bundle. Options: the two routes as asked; add System Settings, Privacy & Security, Open Anyway; use `xattr -dr`. Chosen: all three, with `xattr -dr` as the one that also fixes a "damaged and can't be opened" message. Why: a reader on a current Mac should not hit a dead end. A review round moved `xattr` first for Apple silicon: the config sets `mac.identity: null`, so the app has no signature at all (not even ad-hoc), and an arm64 Mac then usually says "damaged", which only `xattr` gets past. An option I did not take: sign ad-hoc (`identity: "-"`, free) so the app is at least internally consistent; it is a packaging change for the Lead to decide after Mohammad's install test. Unverified: none of the routes was run here (no Mac, and installing is Mohammad's step, A77). Reverse cost: edit one section of `docs/install/macos.md`.

### A1185 2026-10-26 Artifact names and the dmg file names the READMEs rely on (M5.2, qa)
The upload artifacts are `installers-windows-latest` and `installers-macos-latest` (the `release.yml` names, kept so the two workflows read alike; they never run in the same run, so there is no clash). The READMEs call the dmg "the one ending in -arm64" and "the one without that ending" rather than quoting file names, because electron-builder's dmg naming was not seen on this Windows machine. Reverse cost: edit the READMEs once the first macOS run shows the names.

### A1184 2026-10-26 No app icon in M5.2 (qa)
No icon is configured, so installers, the taskbar, and the Dock use Electron's default (electron-builder says so in its log). Options: design one now; ship the default and say so. Chosen: the default, noted in the READMEs and `packaging.md`, as the task said not to design an icon. Reverse cost: add `build/icon.ico` and `build/icon.icns` (the config already names `build` as `buildResources`).

### A1183 2026-10-26 One README plus a guide per platform under `docs/install/` (M5.2, qa)
Options: everything in `README.md`; `README.windows.md` and `README.macos.md` at the top level; a short top-level README plus `docs/install/windows.md` and `docs/install/macos.md`. Chosen: the third. Why: the top level stays short and shared (what the app is, getting a build, requirements, saves, AI review, building from source), each platform guide holds its own warnings and first-run steps, and a learner on one platform reads one file. The old README's "planned, not yet built" text and the `/project-lead` start instructions were removed because they are no longer true or are already in `CLAUDE.md`. Install steps say "download the artifact from the Actions run", with a placeholder line for a public release (the repository is private, A78). Reverse cost: move text between files.

### A1182 2026-10-26 The package job checks the unpacked build for the bundled resource folders (M5.2, qa)
Options: upload whatever electron-builder made; also check that `content`, `resources`, `solutions`, and `licenses/fonts` exist in each unpacked build. Chosen: check, in a shell step between packaging and upload, failing the job if one is missing or no unpacked build exists. Why: an installer without its quests builds fine and only fails on a learner's first launch, and `extraResources` is easy to break. It costs a few milliseconds. Reverse cost: delete one step.

### A1181 2026-10-26 Packaging runs as its own job after the tests, repeating install and build (M5.2, qa)
Options: add packaging steps to the `test` matrix job; a separate `package` job that `needs: test` and rebuilds; a separate job that downloads the test job's `out/`. Chosen: a separate job that `needs: test` and runs `npm ci` and `npm run package` itself. Why: the test job stays as it is (the task asked for that); installers come only from a commit whose tests passed on both platforms; passing `out/` between jobs would add an artifact upload and download and couple the two jobs for a saving of one build. The downloads are cached, keyed on the lockfile: the tools through `ELECTRON_BUILDER_CACHE` under `.cache/electron-builder`, and the Electron builds in electron-builder's default download folders (`~/AppData/Local/electron/Cache`, `~/Library/Caches/electron`), because `electron_config_cache` is read only by Electron's own `install.js`. Options for the Electron builds: those default folders (chosen); `electronDownload.cache` in `package.json` (changes local runs too); no cache. The job runs on a push to `main` and on manual runs from a branch; pull requests skip it to keep their runs short, and tag runs skip it because `release.yml` has its own `package` job and calls `ci.yml` (inside a called workflow `github.ref_type` is `tag`, so the condition keeps the two from building twice). Reverse cost: delete the job.

### A1180 2026-10-26 CI installers are workflow artifacts kept 7 days, never a GitHub Release (M5.2, qa)
Mohammad's rule is that publishing waits for him (M5.3). Options: upload as artifacts; create a draft or pre-release GitHub Release. Chosen: artifacts only, `retention-days: 7`, `compression-level: 0` (the files are already compressed), and the workflow keeps `permissions: contents: read`, so it could not publish if it tried. The 7 days follow the task; `release.yml` keeps its 30 days for tag builds. Anyone who needs an older build presses Run workflow. Reverse cost: change one number.
### A1220 2026-10-27 Sandbox builds pass -NoHotReloadFromIDE unless the app's own sandbox editor is open (W10, builder-unreal)
Mohammad's own game editor, open with Live Coding on (the default), made every sandbox build fail in UBT with "Unable to build while Live Coding is active". Cause, in UE 5.8's `HotReload.cs`: `CheckForLiveCodingSessionActive` throws when `bAllowHotReloadFromIDE` is true and a mutex named `Global\LiveCoding_` plus the target's executable path exists. For an editor target on an installed engine that executable is the shared `Engine\Binaries\Win64\UnrealEditor.exe`, so one project's live editor blocks every other project's build, though the sandbox's modules are separate files that editor never loaded. Learners hit the same thing whenever their own game editor is open. Options: tell the learner to close their editor; look for other editors and wait; pass `-NoHotReloadFromIDE`, which clears `bAllowHotReloadFromIDE` (`BuildConfiguration.cs`, `[CommandLine("-NoHotReloadFromIDE", Value = "false")]`) and so skips the guard. Chosen: `UnrealBuilder` adds `-NoHotReloadFromIDE` to every build it starts (the app's sandbox create and rebuild, the headless loop, the verifier's engine stage, the engine smoke tool), except while the app's own sandbox editor is open (`EditorLauncher.current() !== null`, given to the builder as a function by `createUnrealServices`). Why the exception: that editor has the sandbox's modules loaded, and a headless build would overwrite them (A45, A483); UBT's guard and hot reload handling are the right behaviour for it, so those builds keep today's command line. On Windows the app already closes its editor before every build (A483, A531), so in practice the exception only matters on macOS, where there is no Live Coding but an open editor keeps its loaded code (A45) and UBT's hot reload mode keeps writing to new module names. Reverse cost: delete one array element and the constructor argument.

### A1221 2026-10-27 What -NoHotReloadFromIDE changes besides the guard (W10, builder-unreal)
Read from `HotReload.cs`: with `bAllowHotReloadFromIDE` false (1) `CheckForLiveCodingSessionActive` is skipped, which is the point; (2) `GetDefaultMode` never calls `ShouldDoHotReloadFromIDE`, so UBT no longer scans `Engine\Intermediate\EditorRuns` for running editors (that scan also deletes stale editor-run files, which we no longer trigger) and never picks `HotReloadMode.FromIDE`, in which a build made while an editor of the same engine runs is written under hot reload module names with a state file; the mode falls to `Disabled`, so the sandbox's modules keep their plain names and old hot reload state files are deleted. `LiveCodingPassThrough` is only for partial `-SingleFile` style builds, which the app never starts. Nothing in the sandbox needs FromIDE: the app never relies on a foreign editor hot reloading the sandbox, and the app's own editor uses Live Coding through MCP, not the IDE route. Unverified on UE 5.4 to 5.7 and on macOS (see `docs/problems.md`).

### A1222 2026-10-27 The shared Live Coding mutex is left alone; the app does not look for other editors (W10, builder-unreal)
Options: detect any running `UnrealEditor.exe` and warn or wait; close, or ask the learner to close, their editor; patch or rename anything the engine owns. Chosen: none of those. The switch makes the other editor irrelevant to the sandbox build, so there is nothing to detect, wait for, or close, and the app never touches a process or file it does not own (Mohammad's KAEL editor stayed open during the proof). Reverse cost: none.

### A1223 2026-10-27 If UBT's Live Coding guard still fires, the learner is told the cause, not shown a compile error (W10, builder-unreal)
The guard can still fire when the switch is not passed (the app's own sandbox editor was open, for example after a race with the editor reopening) or on a UBT that lacks the switch. `UnrealBuilder` now recognises the guard line "Unable to build while Live Coding is active" in a failed build and sets the build's pipeline error to a plain message: another Unreal editor with Live Coding is open, close it, run again, and the code was not compiled (Ctrl+Alt+F11 is Live Coding's compile shortcut and does not turn it off; the editor holds the mutex from the moment Live Coding starts until the editor shuts down, even after Live Coding is disabled for the session, so closing the editor is the only way out). Sandbox create and rebuild only prepend that message to the compiler output, so the first-run and Settings headlines come from `explainSandboxFailure`, which now recognises the guard and shows the cause (fix round 1). The raw lines stay in the output as detail (the filter keeps nothing, so the last 20 raw lines show, A891), but UBT's own wording reads like a build failure, so the pipeline error is what names the cause. A pipeline error reaches the learner as the run's error and not as a compile error in the quest (`HeadlessRunner`, `SandboxProjectService`). Reverse cost: delete the check in `buildPipelineError`.

The input glossary entries existed with no first quest. Two ideas the world teaches had no entry: what a mapping layer does to a key it takes (`input-consumption`), and the running-action object a handler can take (`finputactioninstance`). Both are new, with Epic links pinned to 5.8. First quests (A72): `p5-actions-as-values` (Pack 2 World 5, earlier in course order, found in review) for `input-action`; `m1-actions-and-values` for `enhanced-input`, `input-mapping-context`, `finputactionvalue`, `input-modifier`, and `legacy-input` (recognized, not taught: one question shows the old `BindAxis` style); `m1-binding-input` for `enhanced-input-component`, `setup-player-input-component`, `etriggerevent`, and `local-player-subsystem`; `m1-priority-and-consume` for `input-consumption`; `m1-triggers-and-events` for `input-trigger` and `finputactioninstance`. ### A1115 2026-10-27 Pack 3 World 1 boss: a drone handoff driven by a director (author-mechanics, M4.P3.1)
The research offers two input bosses: Combo Trainer (a history buffer, a combo table, a matcher) and Possession Swap (a pawn per mode with its own context). Options: Combo Trainer; Possession Swap. Chosen: Possession Swap, reshaped as the drone handoff, because it uses all four pieces without changes: a walker, a turning drone, a charging drone, and the pause. The new rules sit in one director: F hands control over (nearest drone within 250, the scanner wins a tie, F again hands control back), Tab toggles the pause component, R starts a fresh round only after the drill, and the drill ends at the one moment control returns to the hero, so nothing polls. The director has its own context at priority 5 between the pawns (0) and the pause (10), and it receives keys through `EnableInput`. A new round destroys and respawns the three pawns because the pieces have no reset and "no additions" is a boss rule. Plans: `cpp-director` (the reference) and `controller-handoff` (a player controller owns the keys and the table of modes). Reference game: Kerbal Space Program's vessel switch under its pause menu. Extensions: a chorded handoff, runtime rebinding, and a device prompt (these are research ideas 12 and 8, used as extensions). The Combo Trainer is left for a later pass. Reverse cost: medium.

### A1114 2026-10-27 The deliberate failure of Pack 3 World 1 is the diagonal in the move pad (author-mechanics, M4.P3.1)
The world's one deliberate failure: a walker whose handler adds the pushed pair times speed moves about 41 percent farther holding two keys than one, because the engine merges W and D into (1, 1) by picking the largest value for each slot. The test `SameSpeedInEveryDirection` fails with a message that names the distance covered. Nothing the learner reads before failing mentions it: the brief states only the single key distances, the rubric does not mention speed along a diagonal, the concept card on values does not mention vector length, and the third hint, which is only revealed after failing, points at the length of the pushed vector. The Parsons block for the move pad is about the mapping for S and the arriving controller, not the handler, so it does not hand out the failing shape. Options for the failure: the diagonal; a mapping context left on the player after the pawn is let go; a key held while a context is added. Chosen: the diagonal, because it is invisible without a measurement, it is a famous real mistake, and it needs no engine detail the learner cannot see. The other two are taught as card questions and tested as plain checks. Reverse cost: low.

### A1113 2026-10-27 Tests drive Enhanced Input through a real local player in the test world (author-mechanics, M4.P3.1)
The headless tests need to press keys and see mapping contexts take effect. Options: call the handlers directly (rejected: it skips the mapping, the modifiers, the triggers, and the priority, which are the point); a fake subsystem (rejected: the interface is not mockable without a reflected class in the sandbox); a pretend local player. Chosen: the pretend local player, `FDojoM1InputRig`, a header-only helper in `m1-turntable`'s files (the first quest that needs it, shown as provided and not to be edited) and included by later quests as `Quests/m1-turntable/DojoM1InputRig.h`. It creates a `ULocalPlayer`, calls `PlayerAdded`, spawns an `APlayerController` into the test world, and calls `SetPlayer`; then `Possess` runs the engine's own path (`PawnClientRestart`, `SetupPlayerInputComponent`, `NotifyControllerChanged`), `Press` and `Release` call the controller's `InputKey` with simulated events, and the world tick runs the controller's input processing. `Possess` ticks one frame so a pawn's contexts are in effect before the first key goes down; tests do the same after any context change, which is also taught (mapping changes are applied shortly after the call). Reverse cost: medium.

### A1112 2026-10-27 Controls built in code, owned by the pawn, and added in NotifyControllerChanged (author-mechanics, M4.P3.1)
The sandbox has no assets, so actions and contexts are made in C++ with `NewObject` in `PreInitializeComponents` (before calling `Super`), only when a designer has not assigned one to the `EditAnywhere` property, so the same class works with assets in a real project. Options for where to make them: the constructor with default subobjects (rejected: instances would share the objects made for the class defaults); `BeginPlay`; `PostInitializeComponents`; `PreInitializeComponents`. Chosen: `PreInitializeComponents`, built before calling `Super`, because `APawn::PreInitializeComponents` itself possesses the pawn at once when Auto Possess Player is set and a player controller already exists, so anything built later (`PostInitializeComponents`, `BeginPlay`) would arrive after the context should have been added (found in review). Options for where to add a context: `BeginPlay`; `NotifyControllerChanged`. Chosen: `NotifyControllerChanged`, which the engine's own UE 5 templates use, with the old controller read from `PreviousController` before calling `Super`, so the context follows the controller and a pawn that is let go answers to no keys. Local variables named after pawn members are avoided on purpose (`Controller`, `PreviousController`, `InputComponent`), since MSVC rejects them with C4458. Reverse cost: low.

Pack 3 World 1 needs pawns, controllers, possession, and the local player subsystem (Pack 1 World 4). It does not need delegates, timers, or data tools. Options for the first quest's prerequisite: `f4-boss-course-setup` (the end of the framework world); `f5-boss-beacon-drill` (the end of communication, where World 6 sits); nothing. Chosen: `f4-boss-course-setup`. Course order is by pack, so Pack 3 follows every Pack 1 and Pack 2 world anyway, and the prerequisite only gates the first quest. For the same reason World 6's first quest (`f5-boss-beacon-drill`) is not wrong, but once Pack 3 World 5 exists it should require that world's last quest, and World 2 should require this world's last quest, `m1-boss-drone-handoff`. Reverse cost: none.

### A1152 2026-10-27 The world's deliberate failure: a swap the bag cannot take back (author-systems, M4.P4.1)
Options: the equip swap when the replaced item does not fit the bag; unequip into a full bag; an event sent before the change. Chosen: the swap, in the equipment quest. The natural Equip takes the new item out, puts the old one back, and wears the new one, and loses the old item when the bag is too heavy to take it back (a dagger for a sword in a bag at 18 of 20). The same family catches an unequip into a full bag, which the quest also tests. Nothing the learner sees before failing names it: the brief, rubric, Parsons block (GetTotalPower), front matter, header comments, and the test name (`TightBags`) are neutral, and the breakdown never says nothing may be lost. The hints reveal it in three steps and the alternative, shown after a pass, compares undoing with checking first. Reverse cost: none.

### A1153 2026-10-27 Bag and equipment rules (author-systems, M4.P4.1)
Weights are whole numbers so tests never compare floats. Add fills existing piles in slot order, then opens slots, limited by free room, slots, and the weight limit, and returns how many went in; Remove empties the newest pile first, drops empty piles, and returns how many came out; each broadcasts once, after the bag holds its new contents, and only if something moved. Equipment holds the pointer to the bag and the bag knows nothing about equipment, so a chest can reuse the bag. A refused Equip undoes its removal by adding the new item back, which may move it to the end of the bag's slot order; the boss test therefore finds slots with a helper and never by position after a refused equip. Reverse cost: none.

### A1154 2026-10-27 The expedition boss (author-systems, M4.P4.1)
The director finds the hero's bag and equipment with `FindComponentByClass` instead of owning them, so the components are really on the hero and the same pieces would serve a chest. It spawns the bag view itself and rewrites a two-line board on events only. Goal: sword in hand, helmet on head, three potions in the bag; the round ends with `READY in <seconds> s`. Fixed numbers: 4 slots, weight limit 20, reach 200, drop 100 in front, potion heals 25, health 50 of 100. Reference game: Resident Evil 4's attaché case. Extensions in order of importance: a chest with its own bag, dropping a whole stack, weight slowing the walk. Reverse cost: none.

### A1060 2026-10-26 W7 how the tracker's reset test observes a stale clock (builder, M3.2 Pack 3 World 6)
The reset mutation survived because a stale clock does nothing once the quest is no longer Active, so the state read the same either way. Options: restart the quest after the reset and check it is still Active past the old deadline; read the timer handle through the timer manager. Chosen: restart the quest, with its time limit set to zero first. Why: the handle is private to the tracker, so a learner's test cannot read it, and restarting a timed quest would replace the old clock under the same handle and hide the leak. With no limit on the restart, no new clock is set, so only a clock the reset left running can still fail the quest. The boss reference also renamed its local `bHidden` to `bHiddenOnBoard`, because `AActor` has a member of that name (MSVC error C4458).

What replaces it: the card's early-finish question becomes a question on payloads being copied when the delegate is made, the tracker's second hint now explains that a clock keeps running whatever happened to its quest, and the tracker's new Parsons block practices starting a clock with a payload instead of `Complete` or `HandleTimeUp`, so it does not give away the fix. Options for that block: Complete, HandleTimeUp, the clock start. Chosen: the clock start, for the reason above. After the re-review, everything the learner sees before failing the tracker quest is neutral about the clock: its rubric line, the `timer-delegate` glossary text (shown on hover), the timer card's example, and the breakdown, which now asks the learner to say what happens to a quest's clock when the quest ends without giving the answer (Lead decision). Reverse cost: none.

Pack 3 gets `content/packs/mechanics/pack.json` (id `mechanics`, order 3). Its World 6 folder is `06-delegates` so later worlds slot in before it; world id `delegates` (unique across packs, A73), quest ids `m6-<slug>`, classes `ADojoM6<Name>`, tests `Dojo.M6.<Quest>.<Case>`. Shape: the Quest System breakdown first (research rule 13: a system starts with its breakdown), four builds on the fade ladder that are the boss's pieces (worked item, faded ordered course, stub tracker, blank giver), one concept quest for the only new term (timer delegates with a payload, a new glossary entry `timer-delegate`), and the boss. Delegates, binding, and unbinding are not taught again; their terms point at Pack 1 World 5. The builds use the course's module 6 ideas only as concept coverage (items announcing pickups, plates and doors, quest givers and completion events) with original text, mechanics, and numbers; they do not follow the research's section 6.6 exercise list, because that list is a menu of standalone drills, while this world needed its builds to be the boss's pieces, so each build is shaped by the Quest System instead. The section 6.6 ideas not used (native delegates, return-value delegates, payload structs, logic gates) are left for a later pass. Checked for 6-word and 5-word overlaps against the GameDev.tv research: one phrase from our own author rules was reworded; the rest are engine macro names. Reverse cost: medium.
### A1050 2026-10-26 World 5's interface parameter is named Interactor, not Instigator (W6, builder)
The World 5 interface function `Interact(AActor* Instigator)` and its implementations failed to build on Windows with MSVC warning C4458 (treated as an error), because `Instigator` hides the member `AActor::Instigator`. Clang on macOS does not warn, so it was never caught, and since the verifier builds every solution together it stopped any whole-pack engine run. Options: suppress the warning in the sandbox project; rename the parameter. Chosen: rename to `Interactor` in the interface, the crate and lever implementations, the starters, the cards, the glossary example, and the hints, and add a hint telling learners not to use the old name. Suppressing the warning would teach a habit the learner should not copy. Reverse cost: a rename back.

### A722 2026-10-21 World 6 starts after World 5 (author-foundations, Pack 1 World 6)
World 6 was written while World 5 was in review, so its pieces use only Worlds 1 to 4 and its own builds, and it uses none of World 5's terms (Tick, timers, delegates). Options for its first quest's prerequisite: World 4's last build (`f4-fence`); World 5's last build (`f5-follower`). Chosen: `f5-follower`, now that World 5 is on main, so course order runs World 5 then World 6. Reverse cost: none.

### A721 2026-10-21 Range day: a boss run entirely from data (author-foundations, Pack 1 World 6)
The boss had to use every topic of the world. Options: a crafting bench reading recipes from a table; a wave-based shooting range; a quest board. Chosen: the range, because each topic has a visible job in it: waves and targets come from two data tables, weapons are data assets, levels follow an XP curve, the showcase loads the equipped weapon's mesh from a soft reference, and a wave naming a missing kind is logged in the director's own category and skipped. The tests build the data in code the way a designer builds assets, and a second test edits the target table between rounds to show the round follows the data. Fixed numbers stay in the director only where the rules need them (targets 150 apart, the upgrade at level 3, a reload press is not a shot); everything else is data. The pieces need no additions. Reference game: Zelda's shooting galleries. Reverse cost: low.

### A720 2026-10-21 Pack 1 World 6 shape (author-foundations, Pack 1 World 6)
World 6 uses the world id `data-and-tools`, unique across packs (A73), and the prefix `f6`. Six concept quests: data assets (with primary data assets); data tables; logging (UE_LOG, log categories, on-screen messages, console commands); curves; check and ensure; soft references and loading on demand. Four builds on the fade ladder: worked blaster reading a weapon data asset; faded spawner of targets from a data table, with its own log category for a missing row; stub level component reading XP per level from a curve, whose deliberate failure is levelling up once with an if when one big gain pays for several levels, and which reports a missing curve with ensure; blank showcase that loads a soft-referenced mesh on demand. Breakdown: a loot drop system, with a data table plan and a primary data asset plan. The product list for this world also names build tools (UBT, Build.cs, Live Coding); those stay for later because the Lead's brief names the six topics above. Reverse cost: medium.
### A1000 2026-10-26 The boss layout reader joins paths through the folder reader (W2, builder-unreal)
Four boss cases in `questRunService.test.ts` failed on Windows with "No reference solution for the earlier quest". `readBossSolution` joined paths with `node:path` while the file system it read from spelled them its own way: on Windows `node:path` gives backslashes, the test's fake (and `MacPlatformPaths`) use `/`, so the folder was never found. The real Windows app was not affected (`WindowsPlatformPaths` and `node:path` both use backslashes), so this was a latent mismatch that only the mixed test setup exposed. Options: change the fake to use `node:path`; make the test skip on Windows; add `join` to `BossFolderReader` so the reader spells paths like its file system. Chosen: the third. The app passes `PlatformPaths.join`, the verifier keeps `node:path`, and `tests/unreal/bossSolution.test.ts` uses a reader with a `>` separator, which fails before the fix on every OS. Reverse cost: low, one interface member and two call sites.
### A1022 2026-10-26 Node 24.22.1 on the Windows desktop comes from winget's OpenJS.NodeJS.LTS id (W0, Windows Lead)
Mohammad asked for `winget install OpenJS.NodeJS --version 24.22.1` if that version was available, otherwise the nodejs.org installer with its checksum checked. The `OpenJS.NodeJS` id lists 24.x only up to 24.10.0; 24.22.1 is listed under `OpenJS.NodeJS.LTS`, from the same publisher and the same nodejs.org MSI. Options: the LTS id; the nodejs.org installer by hand; the older 24.10.0. Chosen: the LTS id, which downloaded `node-v24.22.1-x64.msi` from nodejs.org and checked its hash ("Successfully verified installer hash"), replacing the 24.12.0 already installed. Reverse cost: none.

### A1021 2026-10-26 CI runs on push to main again (W0, Windows Lead; closes A80)
A80 paused the push trigger until the Windows unit tests passed. W1 (progress), W2 (prerequisite filler), W3 (review cleanup), and the `.gitattributes` in A1020 make `npm test` pass on this Windows machine, in both an LF checkout and a checkout made with `core.autocrlf=true` as on GitHub's windows-latest. Chosen: restore `push: branches: [main]` in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` in the same change, as A80 asked. A79 (push in batches) still holds. Reverse cost: two lines.

### A1020 2026-10-26 A `.gitattributes` keeps LF line endings on every checkout (W0, Windows Lead)
GitHub's windows-latest runner checks out with `core.autocrlf=true`, so every text file arrived with CRLF and 8 tests in `tests/content/parseQuestFile.test.ts` failed: their fixtures are edited with `
` patterns that no longer match (the parser itself already splits on `
?
`). Options: make every test CRLF-proof; normalize in the parser and the tests; tell git to keep LF. Chosen: `* text=auto eol=lf`, with images, fonts, and Unreal maps and assets marked binary, so a Windows clone has the same bytes as the Mac and the packaged content is LF everywhere. The index was already all LF, so no file changes. Reproduced and proven in a worktree made with `core.autocrlf=true`: 6 failing files before, `parseQuestFile` passing after. Reverse cost: delete one file.

### A980 2026-10-26 The World Map's stage path says when its layout has settled (M2.1f, builder-ui-core)
The locked-stage hover test flaked on GitHub CI (macos-latest) with "element is not attached / not stable". Cause: each stage path paints its first frame guessing six stages per row, because the card has not been measured yet. Once it is measured the rows regroup, and because each row is its own element, React remounts the stages into the new rows. That replaces the node the test had just located. Options: retry the scroll only; wait a fixed time; or expose the moment the layout settles. Chosen: both a signal and a retry. `StagePath` sets `data-measured="true"` once it knows its width. The test waits until no path is unmeasured, then scrolls and hovers inside the same retry, so a late resize cannot break it either. The attribute is not shown and changes nothing for the learner. Reverse cost: none.

### A973 2026-10-26 Hidden macOS e2e runs launch a background copy of Electron.app (M0.1c, builder-shell)
Even with A972, every launch spent its first tenth of a second as a regular app, before any app code runs, because Electron.app's Info.plist starts it that way; System Events lists it as visible then (never active, never the menu bar owner). Options: accept that; edit `node_modules/electron/dist/Electron.app` in place (lost on every install, and it would also change `npm run dev`); keep a separate copy with `LSUIElement` set and point Playwright's `executablePath` at it. Chosen: the copy. `e2e/support/backgroundElectron.ts` builds it once per Electron version under `node_modules/.cache/dojo-e2e/` (about 2 s, then reused), and `launchDojo()` uses it on macOS unless `DOJO_TEST_SHOW_WINDOW=1`. Electron's ad-hoc signature does not seal Info.plist, so no re-signing is needed. `-ApplePersistenceIgnoreState YES` was not used: it only skips window restoration and does not change activation. Reverse cost: none (delete the file and the cache folder).

### A972 2026-10-26 A hidden macOS test app is "prohibited", without dock.hide() (M0.1c, builder-shell)
Options: keep `app.dock.hide()` (policy "accessory"); set the activation policy to "prohibited"; both. Chosen: "prohibited" alone, as the first statement of `start()`: no Dock icon, no menu bar, and the app can never become active. Calling `dock.hide()` afterwards set the policy back to "accessory" (seen with a process watcher), so it was removed. Nothing in the e2e suite broke. Reverse cost: none.

### A954 2026-10-26 Follow-up timeouts say "Ask again"; the fake's first reflection times out (M3.4b fix round 1, builder-ui-quests; one line in ai-review's ReviewService)
A timed-out "Why?" or reflection fell back with the review error's suggested action, "Press Retry review to ask again", but the loot screen has no Retry review for a follow-up: the learner asks again from the same place. Options: reword in the renderer; reword where the reason is built. Chosen: where it is built. `ReviewService`'s failover options gain an optional `timeoutAction`, which follow-ups set to "Press Ask again to try once more. Your tests still count." (`FOLLOW_UP_TIMEOUT_ACTION`); code reviews keep the Retry review wording. This is a small edit in ai-review's file, named here; `tests/review/reviewExtras.test.ts` now checks the wording for both follow-ups. The fake's first reflection under Claude Code now times out the same way (a tests-only self-check with that reason and `retryReviewSuggested`), like its first review, so the e2e can show Ask again leading to an AI grade. Reverse cost: none.

### A953 2026-10-26 "Why?" can be asked again; the fake matches the real bridge under Tests only (M3.4b fix round 1, builder-ui-quests; Lead decision on the fake)
A fallback answer to "Why?" is never kept (A855) so that the next ask reaches a reviewer, but the button hid after the first answer. Now it stays as "Ask again (free)"; a kept AI answer comes back at once, still free. And under Tests only the real `ProviderSelector` gives no `fallbackReason`, while the fake added one to the review and the reflection: the fake now leaves it out and words the reflection self-check as main does ("Self-check: reread your sentence against the question and your code."). `ProviderSelector` is unchanged (Lead decision). Reverse cost: none.

### A952 2026-10-26 "Why?" stays as it was; the fake falls back like the review service under Tests only (M3.4b, builder-ui-quests)
`explainFinding` answers text whether an AI or a fallback answered (a fallback is the finding's detail plus why, never kept, A855), so the finding row shows any answer the same way and needs no new state; a second ask of a kept answer simply returns at once. The fake now matches main when Settings' review provider is Tests only: the code review is tests-only with the "set up a reviewer" reason, "Why?" answers the detail plus that reason, and the reflection answers a self-check with `fallbackReason`; neither is kept. Before, the fake answered every follow-up as an AI. Reverse cost: none.
Fix round 1: the fake no longer adds a fallback reason under Tests only, and "Why?" can be asked again (A953, A954).

### A951 2026-10-26 The reflection box shows the kept answer and lets the learner revise it (M3.4b, builder-ui-quests)
Options: keep the box locked after one answer; show the kept answer and allow a new sentence. Chosen: the second, as A852 grades a new sentence and replaces the kept one. On load the box is prefilled once from `PlayerProgress.attempts[].reflection` with its grade (the learner's typing is never overwritten). The grade shows while the box holds the graded sentence; editing hides it and offers "Send the new answer". An AI grade reads as before with the reviewer's name; a tests-only answer reads "Not graded: compare your sentence with the note" with its reason, and offers "Ask again" only when `retryReviewSuggested` (a timeout). Reverse cost: none.

### A950 2026-10-26 The loot screen's alternative comes from getQuestAlternative (M3.4b, builder-ui-quests)
The loot screen read `quest.alternative` and `quest.alternativeNote`, which the catalog sends before a pass. Now `AlternativePanel` asks `getQuestAlternative(questId)` after a pass (cached per quest as `alternative:<id>`): a placeholder while it loads, nothing for `null` or `alternative-locked`, the error view with a retry for anything else, and otherwise the markdown with the pattern chips and, when an AI reviewer wrote one, "Your reviewer suggests: ..." (A850). Retry review refreshes it, since a new AI review can bring a hint. No screen reads `alternative` or `alternativeNote` from the quest any more, so content-engine can stop sending them (A850 follow-up). Reverse cost: none.

### A971 2026-10-26 The app fits its starting size to the screen itself (M0.1b, builder-shell)
Options: keep asking for 1440x900 and let the OS shrink it; compute the size from the primary screen's work area before creating the window; loosen the smoke test for hidden windows. Chosen: compute it, 1440x900 shrunk to the work area and never below 1100x700, the same rule as `expectedWindowSize` in the e2e harness (A117). macOS only shrinks a window when it is shown, so a hidden test window stayed 900 tall on an 841-point work area and the smoke test failed; Windows never shrank it at all. Now a shown window, a hidden one, and a Windows window all start at the same fitted size. Reverse cost: none.

### A970 2026-10-26 E2e windows open hidden; DOJO_TEST_SHOW_WINDOW=1 brings them back (M0.1b, builder-shell, under A77)
Every `_electron` launch used to open a full-size window that came to the front, so Mohammad could not use the Mac while sessions ran e2e. Options: move the window off screen; hide it with a test-only switch; run e2e headless in a separate desktop session. Chosen: a switch read by the main process. With `DOJO_TEST_HIDDEN_WINDOW=1` (set by `e2e/support/launchDojo.ts` on every launch) the window is created with `show: false`, `paintWhenInitiallyHidden: true`, `focusable: false`, and `backgroundThrottling: false`, is never shown or focused, and on macOS `app.dock.hide()` runs before `whenReady`. `DOJO_TEST_SHOW_WINDOW=1` overrides it for someone who wants to watch. Packaged builds ignore both, so a stray variable can never make an installed app invisible. Proof on the M5 Air: during a full run, a window-list sampler (every 100 ms, 1,100 samples) saw no app window on screen and the front app never changed; with the opt-out it saw the window at once. Not tried on Windows, where there is no Dock and `focusable: false` behaves differently. Reverse cost: none.

A941's finish-card line said self-checked items "come back once a reviewer can grade" them, but under A801 they stay due and come back in every drill. (plural: "Compare them with the model answers; they stay in your drills until a reviewer can grade them."). The Tests-only e2e now answers every graded item right from the content's answer keys (matched by prompt) and asserts the score reads N/N, no due line, and the self-checked line, which is the M2.4 bug's case. Reverse cost: none.

### A943 2026-10-26 The fake pays nothing for a drill with no graded answer (M2.2c fix round 1, builder-ui-quests)
The fake paid XP, the streak, and the daily credit for any finished drill, and tested a world out when 0 of 0 graded answers were right. The fake's rule now lives in `data/fake/drillReward.ts`: a finished drill pays and can test out only with at least one graded answer, and the Parsons fallback never pays. The fake has no record of which items were scheduled due, so "at least one graded answer" stands in for "a graded scheduled due item". Unit tests cover the all-self-checked drill and test-out. A self-checked explain item is neither right nor a miss (A801). Item feedback: a neutral banner "Not graded: compare your sentence with the model answer" with the model answer, no verdict line, and "Not scheduled: it stays in your drills until a reviewer can grade it" instead of a due time. ### A940 2026-10-26 A drill with nothing graded shows how many were explained, not "0/0" (M2.2c, builder-ui-quests, Lead request)
When every item of a drill was self-checked (Tests only and an all-explain drill), the score would read "0/0". Options: hide the headline; show "0/0"; show the count explained. Chosen: the headline becomes the count with the word "explained" (for example "2 explained"), with no due line and the self-checked line below. Reverse cost: none.

### A818 2026-10-21 The stored-attempt path is tested end to end without a reload (M2.2b fix round 2, Lead request)
The fake's state lives in the page, so a reload cannot show A814. The e2e drives the same path instead: a Submit of f1-score-keeper that does not compile, then a Submit of f1-practice-bell (which replaces the loot hand-off), then back to f1-score-keeper, where the run column must say "Last submit: did not compile" with the error in the build log. A true restart is covered by M2.4's real-bridge relaunch. Reverse cost: none.

### A817 2026-10-21 Next is known once and focused once (M2.2b fix round 2, builder-ui-quests)
A815 tied Next to `!availability.isLoading`, so every window-focus refetch disabled Next for a moment and then focused it again, pulling the keyboard away from Improve or the reflection box. Now Next becomes known after the first availability fetch since the loot screen opened (the same pattern as `BuildQuestView`'s fresh read) and stays known; background refetches neither disable it nor move focus. It is focused a single time, behind a ref guard. The e2e focuses Improve, sends a window focus event, and expects Improve to keep focus. Reverse cost: none.

### A816 2026-10-21 Next reviews name each day once, with no counts; past times read "due now" (M2.2b fix round 1, Lead decision)
A811's counts ("3 reviews tomorrow") claimed more than the data holds: `nextDueDates` carries only the three soonest times. Now the line reads "Next reviews due now, tomorrow and in 5 days": each distinct day once, in time order, no counts, keeping the "Next reviews" label so it never reads like the "Next: <quest>" button. Any past or current time reads "due now" (`lib/dueGroups.ts`; the shared `formatDue` used by the Studio is unchanged). The fake now answers `nextDueDates` as the progress service does, the three soonest schedule times with past ones kept. Reverse cost: none.

### A815 2026-10-21 Next takes focus once it is known (M2.2b fix round 1, builder-ui-quests)
A812 disabled Next until availability loads, so its `autoFocus` landed on nothing and Enter no longer moved on. Now a ref and an effect focus Next when it becomes known (and only then, so Retry review or Why? keep focus after they run). Reverse cost: none.

### A814 2026-10-21 The last Submit survives a reload: read from the stored attempt (M2.2b fix round 1, builder-ui-quests)
A810 took the last Submit only from the in-memory loot hand-off, so a reload or restart lost the compiler output again. Reverse cost: none.

### A813 2026-10-21 A build quest opens from a fresh read of the save (M2.2b, builder-ui-quests)
The build workspace took its starting files from the renderer's cached progress, which can be older than a draft saved since, and a Submit then sent the older files. Options: read the draft again at Submit time (a second source of truth beside the editor); open the workspace only from a fresh read. Chosen: the second. `BuildQuestView` waits, once, for the progress refetch that opening a quest starts, so the editor starts from the draft as saved now; from then on the editor is what Submit sends (its own autosave keeps the save in step). ### A812 2026-10-21 Next on the loot screen goes to the next open quest (M2.2b, builder-ui-quests)
Next went to the next quest by order even when it was locked. ### A811 2026-10-21 Repeated review dates are counted (M2.2b, builder-ui-quests)
The loot screen listed "tomorrow, tomorrow, tomorrow". Chosen: due times are grouped by how they read, in time order, with a count: "3 reviews tomorrow, 1 review in 5 days" (`lib/dueGroups.ts`). Reverse cost: none.
Fix round 1: the counts are gone; see A816.

### A810 2026-10-21 The run column shows the last Submit's result (M2.2b, builder-ui-quests)
After a Submit that did not compile, Improve showed only "not passed yet, score 0", and the loot screen sent the learner to compiler output that was not there. Chosen: the run column starts from the last Submit kept in the loot store, labelled "Last submit", with its tests or "did not compile" and the build log open; a Run replaces it, labelled "Last run". The loot screen now says Improve shows the compiler output beside the code. The fake already answers a non-compiling Submit with compiler output like the real bridge, so it needed no change.
