File names count from build and refactor quests' files and from a boss's top-level integration code; a boss's piece overrides do not, since they replace the piece's files in the piece's own folder. Reverse cost: none.

### A441 2026-10-21 A bare include of another quest's file is a dependency (M1.8b, builder-content-engine; Lead request)
The dependency check matched only `Quests/<id>/` paths, but A181 puts every quest folder on the include path, so `#include "DojoF1TrainingDummy.h"` from another quest compiles. Chosen: each code quest's file names map to its id, and a bare `#include "<name>"` of another quest's file needs the same A268 line (naming that quest with its `Quests/<id>/` path) and prerequisite as a path include. Engine headers never match because no quest owns them. Reverse cost: none.

### A440 2026-10-21 Ctrl+C and SIGTERM cancel `--engine` (M1.8b, builder-content-engine; Lead request)
`runEngineStage` turns SIGINT and SIGTERM into one `AbortController` whose signal goes through `EngineSandbox` to M1.3's `UnrealBuilder` and `AutomationTestRunner`, which kill their whole process tree on abort. The verifier then starts no further step, keeps the sandbox for inspection, reports "cancelled during <step>", and exits with 130. Options for the exit code: 1 like any failure; 130 as shells report an interrupt. Chosen: 130, so a script can tell an interrupted run from a failed one. Reverse cost: none.

### A215 2026-10-21 How the verifier reads and requires boss reference integrations (M1.8 round 2, builder-content-engine)
A67 puts a boss's integration and tests in `tools/solutions/<boss-id>/` but does not say how to tell them apart; a boss quest file has no Tests section. Options: a fixed file name for the tests; a suffix; a new Tests section in boss files (a format change for every author). Chosen: every `*.Test.cpp` in the folder is a test file (joined into `Tests/<boss-id>.Test.cpp`), every other `.h` and `.cpp` is integration code, and any other file is an error. A subfolder named after one of the boss's `pieces` holds extended versions of that piece's files (proposed by author-foundations: the boss needs members a subclass cannot add, such as fields on a private class and a non-virtual `Hit`); its files must have the piece's file names, and `--engine` writes them over the piece's solution after every solution is written, so the piece's own tests also run against the extended code. `--engine` builds and tests bosses like build quests and skips the starter check; before the starter checks it removes every boss and restores overridden pieces, since a piece starter would always break the boss. A missing folder is a warning for now, because the Pack 1 World 1 boss on main has none yet and an error would fail every run of main; it becomes an error (one constant, `MISSING_BOSS_INTEGRATION_IS_ERROR` in `tools/verifier/checks/solutionCheck.ts`) once that integration lands. The example boss `example-boss-lighthouse` has no integration yet either, so it also warns. author-foundations was told the layout. Reverse cost: low.
### A429 2026-10-21 A corrupt `config.json` is set aside and settings start from the defaults (M0.2c fix round 1, builder-shell)
electron-store parses the file when it opens and on every access, so an unparseable `config.json` threw during startup and the app never opened. Options: electron-store's `clearInvalidConfig` (silently discards the file); rename and start over. Chosen: `openSettingsStore` opens the store and reads it once; on a `SyntaxError` it renames the file to `config.invalid-<time>.json` in the same folder (kept for the learner or a bug report), opens a fresh one, and logs one warning. If the rename or the second open fails, or the file fails for another reason such as permissions, the app runs on an in-memory store for the session (settings are not saved) and logs that once. Checked in the built app with a truncated file in a temporary user data folder. Reverse cost: none.

### A428 2026-10-21 `updateSettings` and `completeFirstRun` became asynchronous (M0.2c fix round 1, builder-shell)
Checking an engine folder reads the disk, so `SettingsService.update` and `completeFirstRun` now return promises; the folder checks reach the service as a `SettingsChecks` interface (the engine locator and a `Dojo.uproject` check in the composition root, fakes in tests), so the service still does not import the unreal folder. `current()` stays synchronous. `setSandboxPath` writes directly, since the sandbox service has just created and resolved that folder. A refused engine path writes nothing from that patch, including its other fields. Amends A424: `completeFirstRun` also answers the locator's error or `sandbox-not-ready`. Reverse cost: low.

### A427 2026-10-21 Settings no longer trust paths from the renderer (M0.2c fix round 1, the Lead's decision)
The reviewer saw the packaged app accept `sandboxPath: "/definitely/not/a/sandbox"` and `enginePath: "/tmp"`, and `completeFirstRun` stamp anyway. Decided (Lead): `sandboxPath` leaves `SettingsPatch` except `null` for a reset, because only the sandbox service sets it, after realpath (A190); a non-null `enginePath` must pass `EngineLocator.inspectFolder` or the call answers the locator's error, and the stored value is the engine root the locator found (an `Engine` subfolder pick becomes its parent); `completeFirstRun` checks both with the same code (engine valid; sandbox holds `Dojo.uproject`). Reverse cost: low.

### A426 2026-10-21 SettingsService becomes the sandbox path store; ReviewService is built in the composition root for statuses only (M0.2c, builder-shell)
The sandbox service saved its folder through `ElectronStoreSandboxPathStore` until settings existed (A186). The composition root now passes `SettingsService` as the `SandboxPathStore` (same file, same `sandboxPath` key, so nothing moves), and `ElectronStoreSandboxPathStore` is no longer used; deleting it is builder-unreal's call since it sits in `src/main/services/unreal/`. `getEnvironmentStatus` needs the review provider statuses, so the root builds `createReviewService(new SafeStorageReviewSettings({ currentSettings, loadEncryptedApiKey, decryptor: safeStorage }))` lazily, exactly as ai-review's spec describes, and reads only `getProviderStatuses()`. The review handlers themselves (`getReviewProviders`, `testClaudeCode`, and the rest) stay ai-review's to wire against that same instance. Two other test files that build `HandlerDependencies` by hand (`tests/progress/progressHandlers.test.ts`, `tests/contentCatalog.test.ts`) got the two new fields. Reverse cost: none.

### A425 2026-10-21 Environment status: each part read on its own and blanked on failure (M0.2c, builder-shell)
`getEnvironmentStatus` reads the engine in settings through `EngineLocator.inspectFolder` (null when unset, moved, or unsupported), calls the sandbox ready when its folder holds `Dojo.uproject` and `Binaries` (the first full build writes `Binaries`), resolves the review provider a review would use now, and reports `editorConnected: false` until the fast loop (M1.4) can ask the MCP server. Provider rule: `tests-only` picked answers the tests-only status; an explicit `claude-code` or `anthropic-api` answers its status when available; `auto` answers the first available in the order Claude Code, API key; otherwise null (only tests-only left, matching A301's fallback). Options: fail the whole call when one source fails; blank that part. Chosen: blank it, so a missing engine never hides the review chip. Reverse cost: low.

### A424 2026-10-21 `completeFirstRun` needs an engine and a sandbox, and keeps the first finish time (M0.2c, builder-shell)
Options: stamp the time whenever asked; refuse until the engine and sandbox paths are set. Chosen: refuse with `settings-invalid` ("Setup is not finished yet.") so a skipped step cannot mark setup done, and keep the first timestamp on later calls. Reverse cost: none.

### A423 2026-10-21 `mcpPort` setting added, default 8000 (M0.2c, builder-shell)
The Lead's brief and A44 make the editor MCP port a setting. Added `mcpPort: number` to `Settings` and `SettingsPatch`, validated as 1024 to 65535 at the IPC boundary and on read. Adding a required field broke the typecheck of ui-core's `FakeDojoApi` settings fixture, so it got one line (`mcpPort: 8000`); nothing else in the renderer changed. The Settings screen has no port field yet; ui-core can add one through `updateSettings({ mcpPort })`. Reverse cost: low.

### A422 2026-10-21 The API key is stored as safeStorage bytes in base64 inside the settings file (M0.2c, builder-shell)
Options: a separate key file; electron-store under its own key. Chosen: `apiKeyEncrypted` in the same `config.json`, base64 of `safeStorage.encryptString` output, because the bytes are useless without the OS keychain and one file is simpler to back up and reset. `hasApiKey` comes from that key's presence (no decrypt). When `safeStorage.isEncryptionAvailable()` is false or encryption throws, `setApiKey` throws `secure-storage-unavailable` with what to do (unlock the keychain on macOS, sign in to your own account on Windows) and stores nothing; the error never contains the key. The key is trimmed at the IPC boundary. Reverse cost: low.

### A421 2026-10-21 Stored settings are checked value by value on read (M0.2c, builder-shell)
Options: electron-store's JSON-schema option (fails the whole file on one bad value); one zod check per value with its own default. Chosen: per value, so a hand-edited or older `loopMode` resets alone and the engine and sandbox paths survive. Reverse cost: none.

### A420 2026-10-21 Settings live in electron-store's default `config.json` and are read once into memory (M0.2c, builder-shell)
Options: a named store file; the default. Chosen: the default, because the sandbox service already wrote `sandboxPath` there (A186), so no migration is needed. The service reads the file once at startup and writes through on each change; `current()` is synchronous, as the spec asks. Reverse cost: low.

### A190 2026-10-21 The sandbox path is stored with symbolic links resolved (builder-unreal, M1.2)
Found in the integration run: a sandbox under the macOS temp folder (`/var/folders/...`, a link to `/private/var/folders/...`) failed its first build with "unable to read PCH file ... .gch" and its next build with "no such file or directory: ... .o" at link time, while clang reported the `/private/var` path. M1.2a's runs used real paths and never saw this. Options: warn about linked folders; resolve the path. Chosen: `SandboxProjectService` resolves the picked folder with `realpath` before creating `DojoSandbox`, and builds, saves, and returns only the resolved path. Reverse cost: none.

### A189 2026-10-21 IPC: `createSandbox`, `cancelRun`, and `pickSandboxFolder` answer now; `runQuest` and `rebuildSandbox` wait (builder-unreal, M1.2 and M1.3)
Options: wire every sandbox and run method now; wire only what needs no other system. Chosen: the second. `createSandbox` carries the engine and folder in its request, so it works today; `cancelRun` cancels any tracked run through `RunRegistry`. `runQuest` needs the quest by id (content service) and the engine and sandbox paths (settings service); `rebuildSandbox` needs the settings service. Fix round 1: M0.2b merged a folder picker, so `pickSandboxFolder` now answers the picked folder with `folderWarnings`, and `create` also sends each warning as a `preparing` progress line. `SandboxProjectService.rebuild`, `folderWarnings`, and `RunnerFactory` are ready for them. `HandlerDependencies` gains `getSandboxService`, `runRegistry`, `sendProgress`; `src/main/ipc/trackRun.ts` stamps progress with the run id and turns a cancel into `run-cancelled`. Reverse cost: none.

### A188 2026-10-21 Process trees: own process group plus SIGKILL on macOS, `taskkill /T /F` on Windows; only the quest test file gets a header comment (builder-unreal, M1.3)
Options for cancel: kill the root process only; kill the tree. Chosen: the tree. On macOS every build and test process starts in its own process group (`detached`) and cancel sends SIGKILL to the group, which reaches UBT, clang, and the editor's helpers; on Windows `taskkill /PID <id> /T /F`. A unit test with real processes proves the run resolves when the process exits although a grandchild still holds the output pipe, and that the group kill ends the grandchild. Fix round 1: once the root process has exited, Windows does not run `taskkill` (macOS still kills the group). `taskkill /T` finds the tree through the root's process id, which is gone after exit and may already have been reused by an unrelated program, so a late kill could end the wrong tree. Children that outlive the root on Windows are left alone (none were seen on the Mac apart from the storage daemon, which is meant to outlive the editor). Quitting the app cancels every running build and test first (`RunRegistry.cancelAll` on `before-quit`, waiting up to 5 s), so no UBT or editor is orphaned holding UBT's mutex. Header comment: the spec asks for a comment naming the quest; adding it to learner files would shift every compiler line number away from what the learner sees, so only the test file gets it. Reverse cost: low.

### A187 2026-10-21 The real-engine integration test is opt-in with `DOJO_ENGINE_ROOT` (builder-unreal, M1.3)
Options: run it whenever an engine is found; run it only when asked. Chosen: only when `DOJO_ENGINE_ROOT=<engine root>` is set (and skipped when that folder has no engine). It takes about two minutes and starts UBT and the editor; running it inside every `npm test` would slow every builder session on this Mac and make parallel worktrees queue on UBT's mutex. Command: `DOJO_ENGINE_ROOT=~/Applications/UE_5.8 npx vitest run tests/integration`. Reverse cost: none.

### A186 2026-10-21 The sandbox path is saved in electron-store's default file under `sandboxPath` until the settings service exists (builder-unreal, M1.2)
The spec says SandboxProjectService remembers the sandbox in settings, but `src/main/services/settings/` is still empty. Options: keep it in memory; write a separate file; write the `sandboxPath` key of electron-store's default store, which is where `Settings.sandboxPath` will live. Chosen: the third, behind a `SandboxPathStore` interface (`ElectronStoreSandboxPathStore`), so the settings service reads it with no migration and can replace the adapter. Reverse cost: none.

### A185 2026-10-21 Runners report progress without the run id; the IPC layer stamps it. `IRunner.run` takes the quest's id, title, and tests (builder-unreal, M1.3)
The spec's `IRunner.run` passes `onProgress(e: BuildProgressEvent)`, but `run` has no run id and no start time. Options: add a run id parameter; let services emit `ProgressUpdate` (the event without `runId` and `elapsedMs`) and stamp both in one place. Chosen: the second (`trackRun`), so no service can send a wrong id. The quest parameter is narrowed to `{ id, title, tests }`, all a runner reads, which keeps runners independent of the content types. Reverse cost: low.

### A184 2026-10-21 The sandbox is created as `<picked folder>/DojoSandbox`, and only into an empty or new folder (builder-unreal, M1.2)
Options: copy into the picked folder itself; create a subfolder. Chosen: a subfolder, so picking `Documents` does not spill a project into it; an existing non-empty `DojoSandbox` is refused (`sandbox-copy-failed`) instead of overwritten, because it may hold the learner's quests. `Binaries`, `Intermediate`, `Saved`, and `DerivedDataCache` are never copied from the template, and a rebuild deletes them first. The sandbox path is saved before the first build, so a failed build is fixed by a rebuild, not a new sandbox. EngineAssociation is written as `major.minor` (`5.8`), the launcher's form. Reverse cost: low.

### A183 2026-10-21 Command-line arguments are shaped per platform: whole on macOS, quoted and passed verbatim on Windows (builder-unreal, M1.3)
Unreal's Windows command line parser reads `-ExecCmds="Automation RunTests X; Quit"`; Node's normal Windows quoting would send `"-ExecCmds=Automation RunTests X; Quit"`, which Unreal reads as `Automation`. Options: a shell; build the Windows command line ourselves and pass it verbatim. Chosen: the second. `PlatformPaths` gains `optionArgument`, `pathArgument`, and `verbatimArguments`; on macOS each argument goes whole, as M1.2a verified. Fix round 1: with verbatim arguments Node joins the program path into the command line unquoted too, so `C:\Program Files\Epic Games\...` would split at its spaces; `NodeProcessLauncher` now passes `argv0` as the quoted program path whenever arguments are verbatim. Unverified on Windows (problems.md). Reverse cost: low.

### A182 2026-10-21 On Windows the build runs `UnrealBuildTool.exe` directly instead of `Build.bat` (builder-unreal, M1.3)
Node refuses to spawn a `.bat` or `.cmd` without a shell (since the 2024 security fix), and a shell would bring back quoting problems. Options: `cmd.exe /d /s /c` with hand-made quoting; call UBT's executable, which `Build.bat` itself ends up running for installed engines. Chosen: UBT directly, same arguments (`DojoEditor Win64 Development -Project="..." -WaitMutex -Progress`). Cost: an engine built from source whose UBT was never compiled would fail to start; the error says so. macOS keeps `Build.sh` (verified). Unverified on Windows. Reverse cost: low, one method in `WindowsPlatformPaths`.

### A181 2026-10-21 Quest folders are named by quest id, each one is an include path, and the template's sample moves to `Quests/sample-health/` (builder-unreal, M1.2)
The spec puts learner files in `Quests/<quest-id>/` and tests in `Tests/<quest-id>.Test.cpp`, but content tests include learner headers by bare name (`#include "DojoExLantern.h"`), and a later quest includes an earlier quest's header. Options: put tests inside the quest folder; add each quest folder to the include paths. Chosen: `Dojo.Build.cs` adds every `Quests/*` folder as an include path, so bare includes work from tests and from later quests. The template's sample now uses the same layout (`Quests/sample-health/`, `Tests/sample-health.Test.cpp`, `#include "SampleHealthComponent.h"`), so the app rewriting the sample lands on the same files instead of making a duplicate class. Two quests must not share a header name (A40 already asks for unique class names). Reverse cost: low.

### A180 2026-10-21 A quest runs exactly the tests it declares, anchored (builder-unreal, M1.3; amended in fix round 1)
The spec's command runs `Automation RunTests Dojo.<world>.<quest>`, but quests carry no world or test-prefix field. Every `IMPLEMENT_*_AUTOMATION_TEST(Class, "Name", ...)` name is read from the quest's test source. First version: the filter was the dotted prefix those names share. The review showed on the engine that `RunTests` matches substrings, so quest `X.Y.Z` also ran another quest's `X.Y.ZOther` and failed a correct quest. Amended: each declared name is anchored and the names are joined with `+`, and report entries whose name the quest does not declare are dropped. On Windows the filter is `^Name1$+^Name2$`. On macOS it is `^Name1+^Name2` with no end anchor: `LaunchMac.cpp` (5.8.3, line 117) replaces every `$` on the command line with `"` so `-execcmds` can be typed in Xcode, which turned the filter into broken quotes that swallowed `-NullRHI`; the editor then started Metal and hung until the 10 minute limit (seen in the first fix round 1 engine run). On macOS a longer name that starts the same way still runs, and the report filter keeps it out of the verdict. `PlatformPaths.testNameEndAnchor` holds the difference. A quest whose tests declare nothing gets a plain error instead of running every test. Reverse cost: low.
### A214 2026-10-21 Details of the three checks added in M1.8 round 2 (builder-content-engine)
The Lead asked for: cross-quest includes declared on the A268 brief line, a concept quest with a non-explain question, and a required-piece label core of at least 4 characters (from M1.7b, A302). Open points decided:
- A quest counts as declared on the "Compiles against earlier quests:" line only through its `Quests/<id>/` path, because A268 lists the path with every id; this keeps hyphenated words in the sentence from being read as ids. The line may start with list or emphasis marks. A boss uses its spec, though bosses have no code today.
- "At least one question" means the `questions` list; the predict question does not count, because it is not graded.
- The label core is the reviewer's `labelCore`. That module cannot load in the CLI (it imports prompt files through Vite), so the verifier has a copy in `tools/verifier/checks/labelCore.ts`, and a unit test asserts both give the same core for a set of labels. A pure module that both import would remove the copy; that change is in builder-review's folder.

Reverse cost: none.

### A213 2026-10-21 One alias table: content-engine uses app-shell's `buildGlossaryAliases` (M1.8 round 2, builder-content-engine)
On rebasing onto main, M0.2b had already added `GlossaryAlias` (in `src/shared/types/catalog.ts`) and `buildGlossaryAliases`, and M1.8 had its own of both; the two type names collided and the two identifier rules disagreed (mine called "Actor Component" an identifier because of its capital C). Options: keep both; replace app-shell's with mine (their files); use theirs. Chosen: use theirs. M1.8's type and builder are deleted, and the matcher, TermLinker, GlossaryService, and the glossary check read app-shell's table, so the table the renderer receives over IPC is exactly the one the verifier checks. A201's rule is now the one `isIdentifierText` implements (no spaces; underscore, `::`, digit, or a capital after the first letter). Reverse cost: none.

### A390 2026-10-21 A world's stage path wraps into balanced rows that fit the card (M2.1c, builder-ui-core)
The real Pack 1 World 1 has 12 stages; in one row they need about 1,060 px, wider than a contract card at 1100x700, so the World Map scrolled sideways. Options: scroll inside the stage strip (hides the boss and later stages off to the right); shrink the nodes and labels (titles become unreadable); wrap the path. Chosen: wrap. `StagePath` measures its own width (ResizeObserver), fits `floor(width / 88 px)` stages per row, and splits the stages into the fewest balanced rows (12 at 1100 wide become 6 and 6; at 1440 they fit in one row). Each row is an equal-column grid with its own connecting line, and the boss stays last. The fake's first world now mirrors the real World 1 (12 stages, real titles) so the fake e2e would catch a regression. Reverse cost: low, one component.

### A323 2026-10-21 One question appears at most once per drill (M1.6b fix round 1, builder-rules)
A310 lets one concept question ask about every term of its quest, so two due terms of one quest could show the same question twice. Each drill variant now carries the question or block it shows (`contentId`), and composition skips a variant whose question is already in the drill, in every step (required formats, topic mix, fill). The skipped term stays due for the next drill. Reverse cost: none.

### A322 2026-10-21 A world's test-out holds its taught terms and only unlocked patterns (M1.6b fix round 1, builder-rules; amends A313)
A312 limited unlocks to terms some concept quest teaches, but test-out still required every listed term, so a world naming one untaught term could never test out; and the test-out drill asked about patterns the learner had not built yet (against A64). Chosen: the test-out term set is the world's terms that a concept quest teaches, used the same way by `getTestOutStatus`, the test-out drill, and `finishDrill`; the drill also holds the world's patterns whose card is already unlocked, and no other patterns. A313 otherwise stands. Reverse cost: low.

### A321 2026-10-21 The daily cap of 20 counts the whole day, not each call (M1.6b fix round 1, builder-rules)
The cap applied per call, so repeated drills could clear a backlog of 200 in one day, against the spacing the cap protects. Chosen: the cap left today is 20 minus the distinct items already reviewed since local midnight (from the review log, any source, including a concept quest's terms); `dueToday`, the Studio summary, and drill composition all read it, and the rest slides to tomorrow. Reverse cost: none. Fix round 2: drill filler never holds an item due by the end of today, so the overflow beyond the cap is not reviewed as filler and really slides to tomorrow.

### A320 2026-10-21 Practice drills are allowed but pay nothing without a scheduled due item (M1.6b fix round 1, the Lead's decision)
A due drill with nothing due was filled from met items and paid 10 XP every time. Options: refuse such a drill; allow it as practice. The Lead chose: practice drills stay allowed, but drill XP, streak credit, and the daily drill offer (and its chest) count only when the drill held at least one scheduled due item within today's cap. "Drill me" and hover-injected items do not count as scheduled due. The Parsons fallback never pays (A319); a test-out drill pays only if it held a due item, and tests the world out either way. Reverse cost: none.
### A343 2026-10-21 The clay range's reference integration and the spec gaps it found (author-foundations, World 2 boss)
Building the clay range in C++ (A67) found four gaps in the text, now fixed in the spec, both plans, and the rubric: nothing started a round (round 1 now starts with play and R starts later rounds between rounds, as in World 1); the plans read a destroyed disc's fate (A342), so hits are now recorded when Space goes through the range and an invalid disc without one fell; a bonus target dropped after its first hit and could never take the second, so the range raises it again after a hit that does not break it; and the round could end while a bonus earned on the ninth disc was still lent, so it now ends once the tenth disc and any bonus are done. The range keeps its own list of penalty dummies, so the spawner's only addition is DestroyAll. The integration is `tools/solutions/f2-boss-clay-range/` (ADojoF2ClayRange, the extended spawner, two tests). Reverse cost: low.

### A342 2026-10-21 World 2 text follows UE 5.8's garbage elimination (author-foundations, World 2 fix round 1)
From the World 2 review, confirmed by the reviewer with a probe on 5.8.3: garbage elimination is on by default, so when an actor is destroyed, the next collection sets every reflected reference to it to null. A strong reference therefore does not keep a destroyed actor alive; it points at a ghost until the next collection, while a raw pointer without UPROPERTY dangles. The destroy and weak-reference cards, the spot-the-bug item, the spotter's alternative and test wording, and the thrower breakdown (no GetFate on destroyed discs; a hit is recorded when the shot goes through the thrower, and a tracked disc that turns invalid without one is a miss) now say this. A334 still holds: the clay disc test reads a destroyed disc only between ticks, when no collection runs. A335 still holds, reworded: the spotter's reflection test checks that it does not claim ownership through a strong reference. Reverse cost: none.

### A341 2026-10-21 The target rack resets recalled targets with ResetForRound (Lead, World 2 fix round 1)
From the World 2 review: Recall repaired only broken targets, so a target recalled with one hit, or still raised, carried that into its next use. Decided by the Lead: the rack calls the pop-up target's `ResetForRound`, added by the World 1 boss; bosses are never skippable, and A65 fills it from the boss reference when needed. The rack declares the World 1 boss in its prerequisites and its "Compiles against earlier quests" line, and a new test checks that a half-hit target recalled and lent again needs two fresh hits and rests lowered. Reverse cost: low.

### A340 2026-10-21 Boss reference integrations replace their pieces' files with extended copies (author-foundations, M2.3b fix round 3)
A67 asks every boss for a compiled C++ reference integration. The training yard's additions (Lower, the miss count, ResetForRound, ResetHits, PointsPerMiss) live inside World 1 classes, which a subclass cannot extend (private fields, Hit is not virtual) and a second class of the same name cannot redefine. Options: subclasses with copied rules; extended copies of the piece files that replace the originals when the boss is built. Chosen: `tools/solutions/<boss-id>/<piece-quest-id>/` holds extended copies under the piece's own file names; the boss's own code (`DojoF1YardDirector`) sits at the top of the folder and its tests in `<boss-id>.Test.cpp`. The pieces' own tests run against the extended files, which proves the additions changed no rules. Content-engine supports this layout in M1.8 round 2. The reference integration follows the C++ plan: round 1 starts in BeginPlay, R restarts between rounds, a non-looping 45 second timer ends the round, and keys route through HitTarget, StrikeDummy, and RestartRound so tests can press them without a player controller. Reverse cost: low.

### A339 2026-10-21 The training yard round resets at its start, with a per-round bell flag and per-target lower checks (author-foundations, M2.3b fix round 2)
From the M2.3b re-review and a full read-through of rounds 1 and 2: a Blueprint Delay ignores new calls while counting, so only the first raised target ever dropped; the bell's "once" was never cleared, so round 2 never rang; and keys pressed between rounds would add misses to the next round. Options for the reset: at round end; at round start. Chosen: round end stops the timers and keys and shows the score; round start resets targets (ResetForRound), dummies (ResetHits), the "bell rung this round" flag, and the stored rise times, then starts the timers. The auto lower stores each target's rise time and a 0.1 second looping timer lowers every target up for 2.5 seconds or longer, in both plans. Keys only act while a round runs, and a C++ director must call EnableInput to receive them. Reverse cost: low (spec text only).

### A338 2026-10-21 The training yard boss resets targets with a new ResetForRound (author-foundations, M2.3b fix round 1)
From the M2.3b review: the round reset used Repair, which only acts on a broken target, so a target lowered at round end kept its hits, misses, and MISS label into the next round. Options: change Repair to work from any state (it would break World 1's tests and its lesson that each function guards on state); add a fifth boss addition. Chosen: `ResetForRound` on the pop-up target, lowered from any state with hits and misses cleared and the label refreshed, used by both plans' reset step. This replaces A331's "Repair clears misses". The keeper subtracts all misses from the points earned and clamps at 0, so misses made at 0 still count later. Reverse cost: low.

### A337 2026-10-21 World 2 boss: one addition to the spawner, Duck Hunt as the reference (author-foundations, World 2)
The clay range must remove penalty dummies at round end and strike the oldest standing one. Options: give the range its own list of dummies; add `DestroyAll` to the dummy spawner and read its list. Chosen: the spawner gains `DestroyAll` (the creator of the dummies also removes them), and the plans may keep the oldest-dummy lookup on either side. The disc, spotter, and rack need no additions. Reference game: the clay shooting mode of Duck Hunt. Best round: 175 (10 discs at 10, 3 bonus targets at 25). Reverse cost: low.

### A336 2026-10-21 The clay disc test depends on a fix to FDojoTestWorld's tick (author-foundations, World 2)
`FTimerManager::Tick` skips itself when `GFrameCounter` has not changed since its last tick, and `FDojoTestWorld::Tick` never advances it, so timers and `SetLifeSpan` fire at most once per test. Options: avoid timers in World 2 tests; advance `GFrameCounter` in the test; fix the helper. Chosen: fix the helper with `++GFrameCounter` per frame. It is unreal-pipeline's file, so it was reported to the Lead; the same fix landed on main with M1.3 (8665386), and World 2's final proof ran on main's template. Reverse cost: none.

### A335 2026-10-21 The spotter's weak-pointer test checks reflection (author-foundations, World 2)
The spotter must not keep its target alive, which behavior alone cannot show in a test without forcing garbage collection. Options: force a collection inside the test (slow, and it touches the whole editor); check through reflection that the spotter has no strong object property besides its label. Chosen: the reflection check. It catches a `UPROPERTY TObjectPtr` (the usual first try); it cannot catch an unreflected raw pointer, which the rubric and the AI review cover. Reverse cost: low.

### A334 2026-10-21 The clay disc test reads a destroyed disc before collection (author-foundations, World 2)
The disc's fate is set in EndPlay, after which the actor is destroyed. Options: add a delegate (World 5 material) or a static counter; read the fate through the test's raw pointer, which is safe because world ticks never run garbage collection. Chosen: read it, with a comment in the test saying why it is safe there and not in game code. Reverse cost: low.

### A333 2026-10-21 Pack 1 World 2 shape (author-foundations, World 2)
World 2, "Memory and ownership", continues the training yard. Six concept quests: garbage collection; TSubclassOf and SpawnActor; Destroy, IsValid, and EndPlay; weak and soft references; the three ways to create objects; TSharedPtr and TUniquePtr. Four builds on the fade ladder, two reusing World 1 on purpose: worked dummy spawner (TSubclassOf of the World 1 dummy), faded clay disc (SetLifeSpan and EndPlay), stub spotter (TWeakObjectPtr; the world's deliberate failure is a strong pointer checked against nullptr), blank target rack (an object pool of World 1 pop-up targets, named only in its Alternative). Breakdown: the clay disc thrower, with a spawn-per-throw plan and a reuse plan. Soft references, smart pointers, NewObject, and ConstructorHelpers are taught in concept quests and drills only. Reverse cost: medium.

### A332 2026-10-21 A66 applied to the State pattern card (author-foundations, M2.3b)
The `state-recall-3` spot-the-bug item in `content/patterns.json` used `EDojoDoorState::Closed` and friends. Renamed to `EDS_Closed`, `EDS_Opening`, `EDS_Open`, `EDS_Closing`, `EDS_Locked` (initials of the part after the Dojo prefix, A267); the bug line is unchanged. Reverse cost: none.

### A331 2026-10-21 The boss miss rule is a boss addition, not a change to the World 1 build quests (author-foundations, M2.3b)
A330 needs a miss count somewhere and a penalty in the score. Options: change the pop-up target and score keeper build quests (their tests, solutions, and starters) so misses exist from the start; keep those quests as they are and list the miss count on the target and `PointsPerMiss` (5, floor 0) on the keeper as boss additions, next to `Lower` and `ResetHits`. Chosen: boss additions. The build quests teach one idea each, and a miss counter would add a second idea to the faded quest; the boss is where the earlier pieces are extended for a bigger system. The target's label reads MISS until its next state change. Reverse cost: low (spec text only).

### A330 2026-10-21 A hit on a lowered or broken target is a miss that costs 5 points (Lead, M2.3b)
From the M2.3 re-review: pressing keys on lowered targets cost nothing, so mashing 1 to 6 broke every target as it rose. Decided by the Lead: a hit on a lowered or broken target is a miss, costs 5 points, the score never goes below 0, and the miss shows on the target's label. Added to the boss spec, both reference plans, the integration rubric, and the feel criterion. Reverse cost: low.

### A251 2026-10-21 The fake data source is refused in packaged builds (M2.1, builder-ui-core; fix round 1, Lead request)
Options: trust that nobody sets the storage key in an installed app; strip the fake from production builds at build time (the e2e tests run the production build, so they would lose it); ask main. Chosen: ask main. `createMainWindow` adds `--dojo-packaged` to `additionalArguments` when `app.isPackaged`; the preload exposes `window.dojo.isPackaged()` (synchronous, from `process.argv`), added to `DojoApi`; `chooseDataSource` returns the real bridge whenever it is true. The fake's chunk still ships but can never load in an installer. Touches app-shell files (`createMainWindow.ts`, the preload, `ipc.ts`, the preload bridge test) at the Lead's request. Reverse cost: low.

### A250 2026-10-21 Skill mastery is drawn as a distinct shape per level (M2.1, builder-ui-core; fix round 1)
Options: four new mastery color tokens; steps of the pack accent's fill (the first version, too close to tell apart); one shape per level. Chosen: shapes in the pack accent, readable without color: an empty ring (attempted), a half-filled circle (familiar), a full circle (proficient), a full circle with a check (mastered), each with its name ("Proficient"), and branch and skill names as words ("Objects", "Soft refs") instead of ids. Outcome colors stay reserved for test results (A93). Reverse cost: one component, `MasteryGlyph.tsx`.

### A249 2026-10-21 Placeholder screens removed; the Studio's heading is the studio's name (M2.1, builder-ui-core)
Every screen is real now, so `PlaceholderScreen` (A97) is deleted. The Studio's h1 is the learner's studio name ("Night Owl Studio"), falling back to "Studio" when progress is not available; the other screens keep their screen name as the h1. Reverse cost: none.

### A248 2026-10-21 Pattern card tier frames get three tokens (M2.1, builder-ui-core)
Options: reuse foundations amber for gold and paper for silver; three tier tokens. Chosen: `--color-tier-bronze #d39468`, `--color-tier-silver #cdd2dc`, `--color-tier-gold #ffc94d` in `tokens.css` and the gallery, so a gold card never reads as "foundations pack". Reverse cost: low.

### A247 2026-10-21 Level, shipped titles, and the engine room are derived in the window from IPC data (M2.1, builder-ui-core)
`PlayerProgress` has no level and no shipped-titles list. Options: wait for M1.6 to add them; compute them in the renderer from what IPC returns. Chosen: compute. Level and XP into the level come from `getRules` (`levelCurveBase`, `levelCurveGrowth`, `roles`), which that type documents for this use, so no rule constant lives in the renderer. The shipped-titles wall is the first passing attempt of every boss; the engine room shows `studio.modules` with humanized ids. If M1.6 adds these fields, the screens switch to them. Reverse cost: low.

### A246 2026-10-21 Codex entries have no example section; "where you meet it" comes from quest terms (M2.1, builder-ui-core)
The spec lists "example" per entry, but `GlossaryEntry` has no example field. Options: add the field (content-engine and content-foundations work); omit the section until it exists. Chosen: omit, and list the quests whose `terms` include the entry (plus its `firstQuestId`) with their state as "where you meet it". The 19 categories are a select, not a row of chips, so the list keeps its height at 1100x700. Reverse cost: low.

### A245 2026-10-21 Buttons into screens that do not exist yet are disabled with a visible reason (M2.1, builder-ui-core; fix round 1)
"Start drill" (Studio) and "Test out" (World Map) need the drill screen and `startDrill` (M2.2); "Continue" needs the quest screens. Options: hide them; make them do nothing; show them disabled with the reason. Chosen: disabled, inside `DisabledWithReason`, a focusable span that is the tooltip trigger, so the reason shows on hover and on keyboard focus even though a disabled button gets neither. Continue opens the World Map. Reverse cost: none; M2.2 wires them.

### A244 2026-10-21 First-run is a route inside the frame, reached from the Title (M2.1, builder-ui-core)
Options: redirect a fresh learner to setup automatically; ask their name on the Title and send them on with one button. Chosen: the second, at `#/first-run` (not in the rail), so the learner sees where they are before setup starts. Steps: engine (detected list, manual pick, toolchain check), sandbox folder (warnings shown), first full build (phase list, elapsed time, bar, last log line, cancel, plain-English failure with the compiler output behind a toggle, retry), open the editor once (optional), done (`completeFirstRun`). Reverse cost: low.

### A243 2026-10-21 The Mac never shows the fast loop (M2.1, builder-ui-core)
`supportsFastLoop` is true for 5.8 on the Mac, but Live Coding does not exist there (A45). Options: trust the flag; decide in the window by OS. Chosen: `lib/loopDescription.ts` says "Headless loop" on macOS for every engine and loop mode, with the reason; Settings shows "Fast (Windows only)" disabled on macOS. Unit tested. Reverse cost: none.

### A242 2026-10-21 A small query cache written in the app, no data-fetching library (M2.1, builder-ui-core)
Options: TanStack Query (a new dependency, needs the Lead's approval); a cache in the app. Chosen: `data/QueryCache.ts` (about 100 lines: cache by key, keep the last answer while refreshing, turn rejections into `AppError`, invalidate by prefix) with `useDojoQuery`, `useDojoAction`, `useBuildProgress`, and `useSandboxBuild` on top. Unit tested. Reverse cost: low; the hooks are the only callers.

### A241 2026-10-21 Content methods were declared in the renderer until M0.2b added them (M2.1, builder-ui-core; superseded in fix round 1)
The first version declared `listPacks`, `getGlossary`, and `getPatternCards` renderer-side. M0.2b merged them into `src/shared/ipc.ts` (`listPacks` answers `PackOutline[]`), so fix round 1 deleted the renderer declaration and the bridge wrapper; `DojoClient` is now `DojoApi`, and the World Map and Studio read quest outlines. Reverse cost: none.

### A240 2026-10-21 Every screen reads through one data layer, with a typed fake DojoApi for e2e and dev (M2.1, builder-ui-core)
Most services behind the IPC methods are stubs (M1.6, M1.7, M0.2b). Options: build screens against the stubs (every screen would show an error); a fake inside each screen; one renderer data layer over a `DojoClient` that is either the real bridge or a typed fake. Chosen: the third. Screens never touch `window.dojo`; they call `useDojoQuery` and `useDojoAction`. `data/fake/FakeDojoApi.ts` implements every contract method plus the content methods (TypeScript fails if the contract changes), answers copies after 60 ms, simulates a sandbox build with progress events, and uses the real `content/glossary.json` and `content/patterns.json`; quest-flow methods (run, submit, drills, hints) answer `not-implemented` until M2.2. Three scenarios: `returning`, `fresh`, `first-build-fails`. Switch: `localStorage["dojo.dataSource"] = "fake:<scenario>"` (the e2e tests) or `VITE_DOJO_FAKE=1 npm run dev`; anything else is the real bridge, and a packaged build ignores both (A251). The fake is a separate lazy chunk, so the real app does not load it, and the rail shows a "Fake data" badge while it is active. Reverse cost: low.

### A319 2026-10-21 Drill sessions, what finishing pays, and the Parsons fallback (M1.6b, builder-rules)
Sessions live in memory between `startDrill` and `finishDrill`; every answer is saved as it is given, so a restart only loses the unanswered items. Options for finishing early: pay anyway; pay nothing. Chosen: a due or test-out drill pays the flat drill XP and keeps the streak only when every item was answered; an unfinished drill pays nothing and ends the session. The Parsons fallback opens after two failed submits of a build or refactor quest that is not passed, holds one Parsons block, is graded and scheduled like any item, and pays no drill XP or streak (it is help, not a drill). A due drill with nothing met yet answers `quest-locked` ("Nothing to drill yet") instead of an empty session. Reverse cost: low.

### A318 2026-10-21 Tests-only graders until the review service is wired; a self-checked explain item is not scheduled (M1.6b, builder-rules)
`ReviewService` needs the settings service, which is not built, so `index.ts` passes `createTestsOnlyGraders()` (the review folder's `TestsOnlyProvider`, unchanged) behind a `ProgressGraders` interface that `ReviewService` already meets. A tests-only explain grade is a self-check with a fixed score, not evidence of memory. Options: schedule it like any answer; do not schedule it. Chosen: do not. The item keeps its due time, nothing is logged, and the result shows quality 0 with the model answer. Swapping in `ReviewService` is one line in `index.ts`. Reverse cost: none.

### A317 2026-10-21 A boss breakdown is graded and kept but records no pass (M1.6b, builder-rules)
A boss passes on its integration rubric (D14), which arrives with the submit flow. Options: record the boss breakdown as a boss attempt; grade and keep the plan without an attempt. Chosen: the second. `reviseBreakdown` on a boss returns the grade and an unchanged loot view (no XP, no attempt record); the plan and note are saved. Reverse cost: low.

### A316 2026-10-21 Breakdown flow details (M1.6b, builder-rules)
The first submitted plan is kept as `QuestProgress.submittedPlan` (new field, a type addition) so the grader sees it beside the revision even after a restart, and a revision without a first submit is refused. The reference plan shown first is the one sharing the most piece labels with the learner's plan (closest first, so the learner compares like with like); ties go to the first plan. Reference plans are sent to the grader in the board's shape: each piece's purpose is its label, and edge labels map to kinds by word ("own" owns, "listen" listens-to, "spawn" spawns, anything else then). Reverse cost: low.

### A315 2026-10-21 How answers are matched (M1.6b, builder-rules)
Multiple choice accepts the authored index as a string or the choice text (choices are shuffled on screen, A61). Fill-blank matches any accepted answer ignoring case and extra spaces. Which-pattern matches the pattern id; spot-the-bug matches the line number as a string. In a concept quest, an explain answer that only a self-check graded is left out of the pass count, so a missing reviewer never fails the quest. Reverse cost: none.

### A314 2026-10-21 Parsons line ids (M1.6b, builder-rules)
The renderer sends `lineIds` for a Parsons answer. Chosen ids: `line-<index>` for the block's lines in authored order and `distractor-<index>` for distractors; ui-quests shuffles them for display. An answer is right when the lines it names read exactly as the block in order with no distractor, so two identical lines (for example two closing braces) may swap. Reverse cost: low while no screen sends them.

### A313 2026-10-21 Only a world's test-out drill tests it out (M1.6b, builder-rules; replaces part of A130)
A130 tested a world out after any drill. With `startDrill({ kind: "test-out", worldId })` in the contract, the test-out is explicit: it opens when every term of the world is proficient (`getTestOutStatus`), holds that world's terms and patterns, and when finished with every item answered the world is tested out if every term is still proficient (a miss drops one level and fails it). Due drills never test a world out. Reverse cost: low. Fix round 1 (A322): the term set is the world's taught terms, and only patterns with an unlocked card are asked.

### A312 2026-10-21 Only taught terms gate a quest, and a concept quest is never locked by its own terms (M1.6b, builder-rules)
The spec says a quest opens when its terms are at least familiar. Taken literally, a concept quest that introduces a term stays locked forever (nothing makes the term familiar before it), and so does any quest naming a term no concept quest teaches. Options: drop the term rule; exempt concept quests and untaught terms. Chosen: a concept quest is never gated by its terms, and a term gates other quests only when some concept quest teaches it. Reverse cost: low.

### A311 2026-10-21 The content mapper reads pack outlines and marks every quest as adding a class (M1.6b, builder-rules)
`toRuleQuests` builds `RuleQuest[]` from `listPacks()` outlines (pack, world, and quest order; kind, difficulty, xp, prerequisites, terms, patterns, skills, scaffold). Content has no field saying a build adds no new class, so `addsNewClass` is true for every quest as A132 asked, and the daily quest offers no short build yet. Follow-up for content-engine: a front matter field (for example `fastLoop: true` on worked builds that add no reflected declarations) that the mapper reads. Reverse cost: none.

### A310 2026-10-21 Drill items are scheduled by term, pattern, and Parsons block; questions are ways to ask (M1.6b, builder-rules)
The spec's items are glossary terms, pattern cards, Parsons blocks, and scenario questions, while content holds questions inside concept quests and cards. Options: schedule each question; schedule the term or pattern a question asks about. Chosen: the second, so mastery and unlocks follow terms. A concept question asks about every term of its quest; a which-pattern question (in a concept quest or a card's scenarios) asks about its answer pattern; a card's recall questions ask about the card; each Parsons block is its own item, keyed `parsons.<questId>.<blockId>`. Each due item is asked through a randomly chosen question, so drills vary. Keys are the raw glossary and pattern ids; `object-pool` is both, and shares one schedule because it names one idea. Finishing a concept quest reviews each of its terms once, in the same step as the attempt, with quality from the score, so terms unlock the next quests and the loot screen lists those unlocks. Reverse cost: medium once learners have reviews.

### A302 2026-10-21 Tests-only label matching ignores kind words; a partial match needs half the required core (M1.7b, Lead decision)
A158 still let "Component" cover "HealthComponent" and "Wave" cover "WaveSpawnerSubsystem". Decided (Lead): a learner label still matches when it contains the whole required label. Otherwise both labels are split into words (camel case and spaces), kind words (component, actor, class, object, event, delegate, data, asset, struct, step, system, subsystem, manager, controller, handler, service, interface, widget, base, on) and one-letter Unreal prefixes are removed, along with any word whose singular (a trailing "s" or "es" removed) is a kind word, so "Steps", "Systems", and "Classes" count like "Step", "System", and "Class" (fix round 1), and the learner's core must be part of the required core, at least 4 characters, and at least half the required core's length. "Half the required label's length" is measured on the core, not the whole label, because measuring the whole label would reject the natural "Health" for "HealthComponent" (6 of 15); proposed by builder-review and confirmed by the Lead. Reverse cost: none.

### A301 2026-10-21 Unreadable secure storage falls back like an unavailable provider (M1.7b, Lead decision)
When safeStorage cannot decrypt the key, `readApiKey` throws `secure-storage-unavailable`, which used to reach the caller and fail the submit. Decided (Lead): the service treats `secure-storage-unavailable` like `review-unavailable`: the API provider is marked unavailable with that reason (shown in Settings) and the review goes to the next pick, which after the API is tests-only with `fallbackReason`. It never blocks a submit. Reverse cost: none.

### A300 2026-10-21 A timeout falls back to tests-only with a "Retry review" hint, never to the API (M1.7b, Lead decision)
A154 sent a Claude Code timeout to the next provider like any other `review-unavailable`, so in `auto` a slow subscription review could silently become a paid API review. Decided (Lead): timeouts get their own AppError code, `review-timeout` (Claude Code past 3 minutes, and the API's connection timeout). On `review-timeout` the service answers with tests-only at once, setting `fallbackReason` (the timeout message plus "Press Retry review to ask again") and a new optional `retryReviewSuggested: true` on `CodeReview`, `BreakdownGrade`, and `ExplainGrade` in `src/shared/types/review.ts`, so the screen can offer the retry. The timed-out provider is not marked, so a retry or the next submit goes to it again. Only `review-unavailable` (not installed, not signed in, key rejected, unreachable) and `secure-storage-unavailable` move on to the next provider. The Settings test follows the same rule: a timeout there reports Claude Code unavailable but does not set it aside. Reverse cost: low.

### A159 2026-10-21 Bad line numbers are dropped; a cancel ends the 429 wait (M1.7 fix round 1, builder-review)
The schema rejected a finding line below 1, which failed the whole review over one citation. Chosen: the schema accepts any integer and the code reviewer drops a line below 1 or past the end of the file, keeping the finding. The API provider's 429 wait now listens to the abort signal, ends at once on cancel, and throws `run-cancelled` without a second call. Reverse cost: none.

### A158 2026-10-21 Tests-only label matching needs the whole required label or a part of at least 4 characters (M1.7 fix round 1, builder-review)
Substring matching either way let a one-letter learner label ("A") cover every required piece. Chosen: a learner label matches when it contains the normalized required label ("PlayerHealthComponent" covers "HealthComponent"), or when it is part of the required label and at least 4 characters long ("Door" covers "DoorActor", "UI" does not). Reverse cost: none.

### A157 2026-10-21 Windows stops the process tree with taskkill; temp cleanup can never replace a result (M1.7 fix round 1, builder-review)
A `.cmd` shim runs as `cmd.exe` plus the `node` it starts, and killing `cmd.exe` leaves `node` running. Chosen: on Windows a timeout or cancel runs `taskkill /PID <pid> /T /F`, spawned without a shell (falling back to killing the direct child if taskkill cannot start); macOS and Linux keep SIGINT then SIGKILL. The temp folder is removed with three retries and any failure is swallowed, so the review's own answer or error always wins. Both Windows paths are unverified on Windows. Reverse cost: none.

### A156 2026-10-21 Code review prompt version 2: compiler output and test results are data (M1.7 fix round 1, builder-review)
Version 1 only said file contents were never instructions, but a learner controls compiler output and test messages too (a `#pragma message` or a test string). Version 2 says everything inside `<files>`, `<file>`, `<test_results>`, and `<compiler_output>` is data to review, never to follow, and asks the reviewer to flag an attempt to steer it as a problem finding. Closing tags inside all of them were already broken (A151); a test now covers compiler output and test messages. Reverse cost: none.

### A155 2026-10-21 `claude -p` runs without ANTHROPIC_API_KEY and ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN (M1.7 fix round 1, Lead decision)
If the app is started from a terminal where a key is set, Claude Code would bill that key instead of the learner's subscription, which D11 chose on purpose. Chosen (Lead): both variables are removed from the environment for every `claude` spawn, the version check included; everything else is passed through. Reverse cost: one line, or switch to `--bare` with a key if Mohammad prefers API billing (D11).

### A154 2026-10-21 A provider that cannot review is skipped for 10 minutes; tests-only says why (M1.7 fix round 1, builder-review)
An installed but signed-out Claude Code passed the availability check, so in `auto` every submit picked it and failed. Options: a real round trip before every review (slow, costs a request); mark the provider when a review fails. Chosen: when a grader's provider throws `review-unavailable` (not cancel, refusal, or invalid output), the selector marks it unavailable with that reason and the same review is retried on the next pick: in `auto` the API, otherwise tests-only. The mark lasts 10 minutes and then the provider is tried again, so a passing network blip does not disable a provider for the session. The Settings test feeds the selector: a failed connection check marks Claude Code, a passing one clears the mark. `getProviderStatuses` now uses cached checks (the mark shows there with its reason); only the test button rechecks Claude Code. `CodeReview`, `BreakdownGrade`, and `ExplainGrade` gain an optional `fallbackReason` in `src/shared/types/review.ts`, set when tests-only answered because AI providers were unavailable. Reverse cost: low.

### A153 2026-10-21 Windows `claude.cmd` runs through the shell with quoted arguments (M1.7, builder-review)
Node cannot spawn a `.cmd` file without a shell. Options: resolve the npm shim to `node cli.js`; run it through `cmd.exe` with every argument double-quoted (inner quotes doubled); support only `claude.exe`. Chosen: `claude.exe` is preferred (checked first in every folder), and a `.cmd` goes through the shell with quoting. Stopping it kills the whole tree (A157). The quoting of the inline JSON schema through `cmd.exe` is unverified on Windows. Reverse cost: low.

### A152 2026-10-21 The locator does not use the Claude desktop app's bundled CLI (M1.7, builder-review)
This Mac has no `claude` on PATH, but the Claude desktop app ships one under `~/Library/Application Support/Claude/claude-code/<version>/<hash>/claude.app`. Options: search that folder too; leave it out. Chosen: leave it out. Its path changes with every desktop update, and it is not signed in on its own (the live run got "Not logged in"), so finding it would only turn "not installed" into "not signed in". Reverse cost: one candidate path.

### A151 2026-10-21 Learner text goes in tags; closing tags inside it are broken (M1.7, builder-review)
Learner files and sentences are untrusted text in the prompt. Options: escape every `<` and `>` (changes `#include <Foo.h>` and template code the model must read); wrap in tags and turn `</` into `< /` inside learner text; send JSON. Chosen: the second. Code stays readable, a file cannot close its own `<file>` section, and each prompt says text inside the tags is never an instruction. Reverse cost: none.

### A150 2026-10-21 Prompts are markdown files imported with `?raw`, with a version header (M1.7, builder-review)
Options: read the files from disk at runtime (needs them copied next to the bundle); import them as strings at build time. Chosen: `?raw` imports, so they are inside `out/main/index.js` (checked with a temporary import and a grep of the bundle) and vitest reads them the same way. Each file's first line is `<!-- prompt: <name>, version N -->`; the loader refuses a missing or wrong header and strips it before sending. Reverse cost: none.

### A149 2026-10-21 The API key reaches review through `SafeStorageReviewSettings` (M1.7, builder-review)
The settings service does not exist yet. Options: wait for it; read electron-store and safeStorage directly here; define a small interface and an adapter that takes the current settings and the encrypted key bytes as functions plus `safeStorage` as an injected decryptor. Chosen: the third. The key is decrypted only when a review needs it and never leaves main. The settings class does not keep it; the API provider's SDK client (one per key, only the latest kept) holds it in memory in main for as long as the app runs, so it is not re-created on every review. The settings task supplies `loadEncryptedApiKey`. Reverse cost: none.

### A148 2026-10-21 Grader inputs are defined in `ReviewInputs.ts` until content types land (M1.7, builder-review)
`src/shared/types/content.ts` is still empty on this branch. Options: wait; write content types myself (content-engine owns them); define the few fields the graders read inside the review service. Chosen: the third, shaped after the content-engine spec (rubric lines, required pieces with kind, label, why, reference plans as `BreakdownPlan`, explain items with a model answer). The submit flow maps quest content into them. Reverse cost: low; the fields can be replaced by content types when M0.4 merges.

### A147 2026-10-21 Anthropic API request shape and retry rules (M1.7, builder-review)
Chosen: `client.beta.messages.create` (needed for the refusal fallback) with the model from settings, `max_tokens` 16000 (non-streaming limit), the system prompt marked for caching, the schema as `output_config.format`, and no thinking or effort override (the model's defaults). The SDK's own retries are off; one retry on a 429 after `retry-after` when it is 60 seconds or less, and never when the header is missing (the spend cap, research 10.2). For `claude-fable-5-1`, `claude-opus-5-5`, `claude-opus-5`, and `claude-sonnet-5-5` the request adds `fallbacks: "default"` with beta `server-side-fallback-2026-07-01`, so a policy decline is retried server side on another model; other models (Haiku) go without. Options rejected: streaming (a review is under 1k output tokens); the SDK's `.parse()` helper (the providers are transport only and the graders parse with their own schemas). Reverse cost: low.

### A146 2026-10-21 Range keywords are removed from the JSON schema the providers get (M1.7, builder-review)
Structured output rejects `minimum`, `maximum`, and `minLength`, and the SDK's own schema transform also turns string enums into descriptions. Options: use the SDK transform; write the schemas by hand; generate them from the zod schemas and drop only the range keywords. Chosen: the third. Enums and closed objects stay, the prompts state the ranges in words, and zod still enforces every range on the reply. Both providers get the same schema. Reverse cost: none.

### A145 2026-10-21 What tests-only answers look like (M1.7, builder-review)
The spec says tests-only turns test results and the rubric into findings. Chosen: code review scores the share of tests passed (progress-and-rules says review = tests when tests-only), failed tests become problem findings with the test's message, each rubric line becomes a "Self-check" suggestion, and `rubric` is left empty rather than claiming lines were met or not. Breakdown matches required pieces by normalized label (letters and digits, substring either way) and shows the reference plan that shares the fewest labels. Explain items answer score 70, verdict `partly`, and "Self-check against the model answer: ..." so the drill still moves on. Options rejected: marking every rubric line met (false) or unmet (punishing). Reverse cost: low.

### A144 2026-10-21 Strict checks after the schema parse (M1.7, builder-review)
Chosen: the code reviewer must answer exactly one result per rubric line (the quest's wording replaces the model's); the breakdown grader must answer every required piece (matched by normalized label, put back in quest order) and pick an existing reference plan; any of these failing is `review-invalid-output`, never a silent "not covered". Lenient on cosmetics: a finding citing a file that was not submitted loses its file and line, a line past the end of the file is dropped, fractional scores are rounded, and fenced code longer than one line in a finding, hint, or explanation is replaced with "(code removed: the mentor shows at most one corrected line)". Reverse cost: none.

### A143 2026-10-21 Two new AppError codes: `review-invalid-output` and `review-refused` (M1.7, builder-review)
Options: reuse `review-unavailable` for everything; add codes. Chosen: add two to `src/shared/types/errors.ts` (a one-line change in builder-shell's file), so the renderer can tell "the reviewer answered badly, submit again" and "the model declined" from "no reviewer is set up". Timeouts and sign-in problems stay `review-unavailable`; cancel is `run-cancelled`. Reverse cost: low.

### A142 2026-10-21 A chosen provider that is unavailable falls back to tests-only (M1.7, builder-review)
Options: fail the submit with `review-unavailable`; fall back to tests-only. Chosen: fall back, with the reason kept on the selection, so a missing CLI never blocks a submit. The review's `provider` field says `tests-only`, so the loot screen can show why. An explicit choice is never swapped for the other AI provider. Reverse cost: low.

### A141 2026-10-21 `status()` instead of `isAvailable()`; tests-only is not a transport (M1.7, builder-review)
The spec's interface had `isAvailable()`. Chosen: `status(refresh?)` returning `ReviewProviderStatus` (with reason and CLI version or model id), cached until a refresh, because Settings and the Title chip need the why. Tests-only has nothing to transport, so it is its own class answering each grader directly instead of implementing `complete()` badly. Reverse cost: none.

### A140 2026-10-21 `--json-schema` takes inline JSON; the prompt file sits beside the empty cwd (M1.7, builder-review)
The spec said `--json-schema <schema file>`. Claude Code 2.1.304's help shows `--json-schema <schema>` with an inline example, and the live run accepted inline JSON. Chosen: inline JSON. Each review makes a temp folder holding `system-prompt.md` and an empty `work/` folder used as `cwd`, removed afterwards even on failure. Reverse cost: none.

### A225 2026-10-21 `startDrill` request and session types live in `src/shared/types/drill.ts`; the handler stays a stub (M0.2b, builder-shell)
`startDrill(request)` takes `{ kind: "due" } | { kind: "test-out", worldId } | { kind: "parsons-fallback", questId }` (A89) and answers a `DrillSession { sessionId, request, items: { itemId, item: DrillItem }[] }`, where `DrillItem` is content-engine's type. Options: add these to `progress.ts`; a new `drill.ts`. Chosen: `drill.ts`, because builder-rules is extending `progress.ts` on its own branch and two edits there would conflict. The handler validates the request and answers `not-implemented` until progress-and-rules builds drill scheduling (M1.6); it is theirs to wire. Reverse cost: none.

### A224 2026-10-21 `pickEngineFolder` wired now: native folder dialog, then `EngineLocator.inspectFolder` (M0.2b, builder-shell)
M1.1 wired `detectEngines` and `checkToolchain` and left the picker for app-shell. The dialog lives in the composition root and reaches the handler as a `pickFolder(title)` dependency, so the handler stays testable without Electron. Cancel answers null; a folder that is not an engine, or one older than 5.4, rejects with the locator's `engine-not-found` or `engine-version-unsupported`. The dialog opens over the focused window. `getEnvironmentStatus` stays a stub: it needs the settings service. Reverse cost: none.

### A223 2026-10-21 Glossary alias table: one row per term or alias, longest first, identifiers case sensitive (M0.2b, builder-shell)
`getGlossaryAliases()` answers `{ text, entryId, caseSensitive, codeOnly, short }[]`, sorted longest text first so the longest match wins. The content-engine spec says identifiers such as `AActor` match exactly and plain words such as "actor" match in any case, without defining "identifier". Chosen rule (`isIdentifierText` in `src/main/content/buildGlossaryAliases.ts`): no spaces, and an underscore, `::`, a digit, or a capital letter after the first character. So `AActor`, `UPROPERTY`, `BeginPlay`, `GC`, `UE_LOG` are identifiers; `actor`, `Actor`, `Tick`, `garbage collection` are plain words. `codeOnly` mirrors `linkMode: "code-only"`. The TermLinker (M1.8) should use the same table so hover cards and the editor agree. Reverse cost: low.

### A222 2026-10-21 Quests reach the renderer without their hint texts (M0.2b, builder-shell)
Hints cost XP (D12) and `revealHint` is the priced door. Options: send whole quests and trust the renderer to hide hints; replace build and refactor quests' `hints` with `hintCount`. Chosen: the second (`LearnerQuest` in `src/shared/types/catalog.ts`); main services read the full quest through `ContentCatalog.getAuthoredQuest`. Answer keys (right choices, Parsons order) still reach the renderer: grading runs in main, nothing is priced on them, and the files are on disk anyway. Reverse cost: low.

### A221 2026-10-21 World Map outlines from `listPacks`, full content from `getWorld` and `getQuest` (M0.2b, builder-shell)
Options: one call returning every pack with every full quest (starters, tests, cards: several megabytes for 300 quests); outlines for the map plus per-world and per-quest calls. Chosen: the second. `listPacks()` answers packs, worlds, and quest outlines (front matter plus scaffold level) in order, enough for the whole World Map; `getWorld(worldId)` and `getQuest(questId)` answer full content. `getContentStatus()` answers the root, counts, and every load problem for the Title screen or a dev overlay. Reverse cost: low.

### A220 2026-10-21 Content loads once at startup into an app-shell `ContentCatalog` (M0.2b, builder-shell)
Options: load on every request; load once and serve from memory. Chosen: once, starting in the background right after `app.whenReady()`, so the window opens without waiting; requests made before it finishes wait for it. The catalog lives in `src/main/content/` (app-shell's composition code) and uses content-engine's `ContentLoader` unchanged. A file with problems is left out by the loader and listed in `getContentStatus`; the rest of the content still serves. Only a missing folder or a load that throws rejects the content methods, with `content-invalid`, and a failed load is forgotten so the next request tries again (no restart needed after fixing the folder). Content root: `<appPath>/content` in dev, `<process.resourcesPath>/content` when packaged (A13). No reload method yet; authors use the verifier. Reverse cost: low.
### A269 2026-10-21 Boss rhythm, input, and the two additions to earlier classes (author-foundations, M2.3 fix round 1)
From the review: with a rise every 3 seconds and 2 seconds up, the "at most 2 up" rule could never apply; the spec never said how the player hits anything; the target could not drop an unhit target; and a golden target was a new state value. Options for the rhythm: rise every 1.5 s with 2.5 s up (the Lead's example); rise every second with 2.5 s up. Chosen: every second with 2.5 s up, because with 1.5 s and 2.5 s an older target is always gone before the third rise, so the limit would still never apply. Input: keys 1 to 6 call Hit on each target and Q and E call Strike on each dummy, mapped in the level Blueprint or bound on a C++ director. The earlier classes get exactly two additions, `Lower` on the pop-up target and `ResetHits` on the dummy (needed so a second round starts like the first). Golden is a separate kind enum (`EDojoF1TargetKind`, ETK_Normal and ETK_Golden). The 150 bell threshold is reachable: the most a round scores is 180. Reverse cost: low.

### A268 2026-10-21 Cross-quest file dependencies are one line in the brief (author-foundations, M2.3 fix round 1)
For A65 the pipeline must know which earlier quests a quest compiles against. Options: a front matter field (front matter is strict, so the schema and content-engine would change first); one fixed sentence in the brief. Chosen for now: the brief carries a line starting "Compiles against earlier quests:" with each quest id and the file path. In World 1 only `f1-score-keeper` has one (f1-training-dummy and f1-popup-target). If content-engine adds a `dependsOn` front matter field, these lines move into it. Reverse cost: low.

### A267 2026-10-21 Enum value initials skip the Dojo quest prefix (author-foundations, M2.3 fix round 1)
A66 asks enum values to start with the enum's initials. Quest enums carry a quest prefix (`EDojoF1TargetState`), so the full initials would be `EDF1TS_`. Options: initials of the whole name; initials of the descriptive part only. Chosen: the descriptive part, as in the skill's own example: `EDojoF1TargetState::ETS_Lowered`, `EDojoF1GateState::EGS_Shut`, `EDojoF1TargetKind::ETK_Golden`. Reverse cost: a rename across quests, tests, and solutions.

### A266 2026-10-21 World 1 tests assert the reflection macros themselves (author-foundations, M2.3)
World 1 teaches the macros, so a test that only calls C++ functions would pass with every macro missing. Options: behavior-only tests; behavior plus reflection queries. Chosen: both. The tests ask reflection directly: `FindFunctionByName` plus `FUNC_BlueprintCallable`, `FUNC_BlueprintEvent`, and `FUNC_Native` flags; `FindFProperty` plus `CPF_Edit`; `StaticEnum` plus `GetDisplayNameTextByValue` for UMETA names. This is what makes the worked starter (Strike without UFUNCTION) compile and still fail. Reverse cost: low.

### A265 2026-10-21 The World 1 boss may drive its round from the level Blueprint (author-foundations, M2.3)
The training yard boss needs a round clock and a rise rhythm, but timers get their own world later (Pack 1 World 5). Options: hold timing out of the boss; require a C++ director with the timer manager; allow either. Chosen: allow either, with two reference plans (a C++ director, and the level Blueprint calling the BlueprintCallable API). The Blueprint route is the point of a reflection world: C++ owns the rules, the editor drives them. Reference game: the Ocarina of Time archery and shooting galleries. Reverse cost: low (one plan and one paragraph).

### A264 2026-10-21 Dash breakdown lives in World 1; LaunchCharacter added to the glossary; first quests linked (author-foundations, M2.3)
The brief puts the dash with cooldown breakdown in World 1, though its pieces use input, timers, and delegates that later worlds teach. Options: move it to World 4 or 5; keep it and say so. Chosen: keep it, and the spec says the learner only plans here. It is order 11, after the four build quests, and before the boss. Added glossary entry `launch-character` (framework, API link pinned to 5.8). Set `firstQuestId` on the 31 entries whose first quest is now in World 1. Reverse cost: low.

### A263 2026-10-21 World 1 observables are floating text labels and the `ke` console command (author-foundations, M2.3)
Every build quest needs something the learner sees in the editor. Options: static meshes (need an asset pick by hand), print strings, `UTextRenderComponent` labels. Chosen: a text render label on each actor (no asset needed, readable in the test through its `Text` field), driven from the console with `ke * <Function>`, which only works on UFUNCTIONs and so demonstrates reflection itself. Reverse cost: low.

### A262 2026-10-21 World 1 split: six concept quests, four builds, one new idea each (author-foundations, M2.3)
The research lists 29 terms for World 1. Options: one concept quest per term group of 3 to 4 (eight or more quests); six quests as the brief asks. Chosen: six concept quests (UObject and actor with the CDO; reflection, UCLASS, and the generated header; UPROPERTY specifiers; UFUNCTION specifiers; UENUM and USTRUCT; Cast and IsA), interleaved with the builds: worked training dummy (UFUNCTION and UPROPERTY), faded pop-up target (UENUM), stub score keeper (Cast and IsA, the world's deliberate failure: an empty slot and a plain actor in the list), blank practice bell (BlueprintNativeEvent). USTRUCT, the CDO, and BlueprintImplementableEvent are taught in concept quests and drills but have no build quest of their own in this world; the verifier's term coverage rule is a warning until release, and later worlds use them in builds. Reverse cost: medium (quest order and prerequisites).

### A261 2026-10-21 Pack 1 ids and names (author-foundations, M2.3)
Options for ids: long descriptive ids; short world-prefixed ids. Chosen: pack `foundations`, world folder `01-objects-and-reflection`, quest ids `f1-<slug>` (short, because the quest id becomes a folder in the learner's sandbox and Windows paths are tight), quest classes `ADojoF1<Name>` and `EDojoF1<Name>`, tests `Dojo.F1.<Quest>.<Case>` with structs `FDojo_F1_<Quest>_<Case>`. Reverse cost: medium once learners have progress saved against the ids.

### A260 2026-10-21 Quest tests include quest headers by module-root path (author-foundations, M2.3)
QuestFileWriter puts learner files in `Source/Dojo/Quests/<quest-id>/` and tests in `Source/Dojo/Tests/<quest-id>.Test.cpp`. A bare `#include "DojoF1Probe.h"` from `Tests/` failed to build on UE 5.8.3 (file not found): UBT adds the module root, not its subfolders. Options: bare includes plus a Build.cs that adds every quest folder (the sandbox template is unreal-pipeline's); include by path from the module root. Chosen: `#include "Quests/<quest-id>/<File>.h"` in tests and in quest code that reuses an earlier quest's class (the score keeper includes the dummy and the pop-up target this way). Note for content-engine and the Lead: the format examples in `content/examples/` use bare includes, which would not build in the sandbox; they are not compiled (A103), but authors copy them. Reverse cost: low (a search and replace if the pipeline later adds quest folders to the include path).
### A212 2026-10-21 When a starter check counts, and when it is inconclusive (M1.8 fix round 1, builder-content-engine)
From review. A starter that does not compile counts as "the starter does not pass" only when a build error points into its own `Quests/<id>/` or `Tests/<id>.Test.cpp`; a failure elsewhere means the starter broke other quests' code, which proves nothing about this quest, so it is a warning ("inconclusive"). The starter check is skipped, also as an inconclusive warning, when the quest's reference solution already failed its tests. A starter that builds but whose tests do not run stays a warning. Reverse cost: none.

### A211 2026-10-21 The verifier will drive Unreal through M1.3's runners, not its own spawning code (M1.8 fix round 1; decided by the Lead)
The reviewer found that the verifier's own `runProcess` cannot work on Windows (spawning `Build.bat` without a shell throws EINVAL on Node 24) and that its timeout kills only the top process, not the UBT and editor children. Options: fix the verifier's spawner; reuse the app's runners. The Lead chose reuse: once M1.3 merges, `EngineSandbox` builds through `UnrealBuilder` and runs tests through `AutomationTestRunner` (PlatformPaths, process tree kill, anchored test names), so the app and the verifier share one tested way of running Unreal. Until then the verifier's spawner is Mac only; this is fix round 2. Reverse cost: low.

### A210 2026-10-21 The verifier requires reference solutions without `--engine` too (M1.8, builder-content-engine)
CLAUDE.md says every build quest ships a reference solution. Options: check it only under `--engine`; check it on every run. Chosen: every run, as an error, and the solution folder must hold exactly the quest's file names. A missing solution is cheap to find and expensive to discover later on the Windows desktop. `--solutions <folder>` points elsewhere for fixtures. Reverse cost: none.

### A209 2026-10-21 A spelling shared by two glossary entries is an error (M1.8, builder-content-engine)
Not in the spec. The TermLinker cannot choose between two entries for one spelling, so it would silently link the first. Options: warn; error; let the first entry win. Chosen: error, with case folding for plain words. The current glossary (191 entries) has none. Reverse cost: none.

### A208 2026-10-21 How `--engine` builds and judges quests (M1.8, builder-content-engine)
Options for the solution pass: one sandbox per quest (slow: a cold build each); one sandbox with every solution, one build, one test run. Chosen: the second (one cold build of about 15 s for the examples). Options for the starter check: all starters at once (one build, but one broken starter breaks every quest's check, and a quest could fail because of another quest's starter); one quest at a time with every other quest on its solution.
